### August 21–22, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "analyze test coverage" → "do what you think is best") — **an HTTP error defeated the cursor hold in EVERY event-range walker, the accuracy gate measured for the first time, and four near-misses the code refuted**

- ✅ **THE DEFECT: `fetchEventRange` swallowed a non-2xx into `return []`, so a FAILED chunk reached the scan loop as a chunk that read fine and was EMPTY.** The chunk `catch` only ever sees THROWN errors, so `firstFailedChunkStart` stayed null, the cursor advanced past blocks nothing had read, `partial_scan` stayed false and the run logged `ok:true`. Nothing revisits a block below the cursor ⇒ **permanent loss**. Proven by execution: a 500 on chunk 1001-1250 advanced the cursor to 1500 while the same fixture throwing ECONNRESET held at 1000. **10 routes fixed here** (7 block-scan + 3 offers indexers), 9 more by a concurrent session, all mutation-verified.
- ⚠ **THE POPULATION WAS DERIVED FROM A PROXY FOUR TIMES: 8 → 17 → 19 → and the fourth was the DIRECTORY.** `firstFailedChunkStart` (the cursor HOLD) found 8 · `async function fetchEventRange` (the fetcher's NAME) found 17 · the `v1/events` URL found 19 · **and every one of those greps searched only `app` and `lib`** — `workers/` and `supabase/functions/` hold **10 more** event-range readers that were outside every guard arm by construction. Measured clean, so shipped as a ban at population zero.
- ⚠ **I BUILT THE PROPERTY GUARD FOR THAT POPULATION AND THREW IT AWAY — the reason is the durable part.** FILE-scoped gave 12 false positives (`fetchTxBuyers` returns `[]` on a non-2xx because losing a buyer address degrades a **FIELD** while losing an event range moves the **CURSOR** — same expression, opposite correctness). FUNCTION-scoped was **silently vacuous**: in `ingest-allday-pack-opens` the URL is built in an inner helper while the `!r.ok` check lives in the outer `scanOpens`, so the walk found a body with **zero** ok-checks and reported the file clean — printing "0 violations" on the clean tree **and** on a mutant that deleted the error channel. **A guard that passes both ways is not a guard.** Shipped a REVIEW TRIGGER on the population instead, which says so in its own header.
- ⚠ **EIGHT TESTS ACROSS THE FAMILY WERE PINNING THE DEFECT AS CORRECT.** Three found here — *"…survives a 500 event fetch"* ending `// The 500 leg did not poison the run — cursor still advanced.`, plus two copies of *"…degrades that range to empty and still advances the cursor"*. Each described permanent data loss, in its own title, as a graceful degrade. **Grep test TITLES for `survives` / `degrades to` / `still advances`** — promoted into [testing-and-ci.md](../reference/testing-and-ci.md). All INVERTED, never deleted.
- ✅ **`net._http_response` is the instrument nobody was using.** A filing parked the pack-opens diagnosis as "deploy blocked" because an invocation heartbeat needed an edge-function deploy behind the gate-key BLOCKER. **pg_net already records `status_code` / `timed_out` / `error_msg` for every `net.http_get` pg_cron makes** — no deploy, not behind the blocker. It refuted that filing's "the status 0 wedge cleared" (**the failing block TRACKS THE CURSOR**), established the mechanism (caller aborts at 15 s; the spork worker allows itself 25 s and would return **504** — we see **0** on 40 of 42, so the status code proves which side gave up), and **refuted the proposed `&blocks=N` lever as a no-op** (avg advance 226 blocks against a 25,000 window ⇒ ~ONE query per run).
- ✅ **THE ACCURACY GATE, MEASURED FOR THE FIRST TIME — 31.3% at HIGH/MEDIUM** (9,224/29,514). No view, no dashboard and no recent reading existed for the metric the roadmap calls the headline. **Accuracy tracks LIQUIDITY monotonically**: 5.51 sales/edition/month → 34.2% (TS) · 1.69 → 22.7% (AllDay) · 0.17 → 0.0% (Golazos) · 0.00 → 0.0% (UFC). **The ceiling is market depth, not the pricing code.** UFC is a genuinely dead market and the SHAPE proves it — sales 265→165→48→57→5→0 while avg price fell $3.86→$0.68; **volume and price decaying together is a dying market, an ingestion break is a cliff at full price.**
- ✅ **The All Day headroom is 320 editions and it is a DECISION, not a defect.** `escalateConfidence` lifts LOW→MEDIUM when a live ask agrees with the sales median within ±25% at ≥3 sales; 717 All Day editions qualify, 705 have a live floor ask, **320 (47.6%) pass the band** (+5.2pp on the collection, +1.1pp on the gate). ⚠ Ask-corroboration is **Top-Shot-only BY DESIGN** — recalc builds a SEPARATE All Day ask map so the floor "never leaks into ask-corroboration", because the ceiling only LOWERS while corroboration RAISES.
- ⚠ **FOUR NEAR-MISSES, each a conclusion the DATA supported and the CODE refuted.** (1) *"All Day's `edition_offers.low_ask` is never populated"* — 0 of 2,292 vs 12,259 of 12,464 for TS; **it is the BID side**, `highest_offer` on all 2,292. (2) *"The Top-Shot-only limitation is an expired premise"* — deliberate, documented 50 lines below. (3) *"The UFC recalc is broken"* off a 99-hour-stale FMV write — nothing to recompute. (4) ⚠ **the one with teeth: *"7.75% of disk IO on unlaunched boards"*** — `is_active=false` on both, but `CANDY_MLB_PUBLIC`/`PANINI_PUBLIC` are **true**; acting on it would have taken **two live public surfaces dark**. Promoted to [routes-and-surfaces.md](../reference/routes-and-surfaces.md).
- ✅ **The cross-collection ESCALATION's blocker dissolved in three lines.** Its refresh half was Trevor's call because `step1` opens with `TRUNCATE`, holding ACCESS EXCLUSIVE for the whole 105–350 s run on a table the public board reads. **The trade is an artefact of statement ORDER**: the table holds **179 rows**, all cost is the scan over `wallet_moments_cache` (~2.49M), so computing into a `TEMP TABLE` before the truncate shrinks the lock to **milliseconds**. ⚠ Build-and-swap was checked and REJECTED — RLS with 2 policies, anon/authenticated grants and a dependent view would all break on a rename.
- ⚠ **MUTATION TESTING CAUGHT MY OWN FIXTURE LYING.** The equivalence pin killed 1 of 3 at first: dropping `COALESCE` survived because **`SUM` already skips NULLs** — it only matters when EVERY value is NULL, and no such wallet existed, while the assertion's comment claimed a property it could not hold. Dropping the `set_name` filter survived because no cohort wallet reached that edition. After strengthening: **5 of 5**, including forgetting the `TRUNCATE` — the reorder's OWN new failure mode.
- ✅ **The disk-IO budget RANKED** — 7,809 GB / 242 h = **9.4 MB/s sustained against a 22 MB/s throttled floor**, which is why the band is intermittent rather than constant. Top: `refresh_wmc_fmv_changed` **8.1% / 636 GB / 4.9 min & 553 MB per call**; ⚠ unfiled anywhere: **`raise_impossible_parallel_circ()` reads 1.24 GB PER CALL** to answer a yes/no.
- ⚠ **SECRET EXPOSURE, disclosed:** reading `cron.job.command` to find callers printed a live `?key=` gate key into the transcript — the exact class CLAUDE.md records. Already slated for rotation under the 08-18 BLOCKER, so no new rotation, but it makes that rotation **required rather than deferred**. Query `cron.job` with `regexp_replace(command, 'key=[^&'']+', 'key=***')`.
- ⚠ **A route change goes live on PUSH; a migration can be committed-but-UNAPPLIED.** That asymmetry is why the cross-collection fix could be staged with an equivalence pin and the All Day one could not.
- **Open, deliberately:** the All Day corroboration decision · the `deals` board rewrite (78% refresh failure, up to 15.1 h stale) · UFC deprecate-or-wire · the 4 walkers that have never run · the gate-key rotation. **Armed:** `trig_01L4TBXNYcUAqYDC4h4pDwkR` fires 2026-08-22 20:15Z in a FRESH session to apply the cross-collection migration — ⚠ it opens with a CAPABILITY CHECK because a fresh-session routine may carry no Supabase connector, and a runbook that cannot execute must report rather than half-run.

### August 21, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "keep going") — **a filing re-derived and half-refuted, three public boards that hid how old their data was, and two CI reds on `main`. 8 commits; no DB, no migration, no prod-state change.**

- ✅ **RE-DERIVED THE 22:45Z `deals` FILING AND REFUTED ITS HEADLINE.** Its numbers hold (509 warm ticks / 48h: `deals` 78.2%, `first-mint` 63.7%), and the 4h-healthy / 20h-failing cycle repeats on all three retained days with a cliff at 00→01Z and a drain over 19→22Z. But its section 3 — *"'saturation-driven, self-heals when load lifts' no longer describes this"* — is wrong. **The control it lacked:** `rpc-backfill-wmc-fmv-confidence`, an unrelated cron on an identical 5-minute schedule, has a **p50 of 0.7–1.0 s in hours 20–00Z and 3.1–18.0 s in 01–19Z**. A cheap unrelated query 3–18× slower on the same clock is a GLOBAL condition, so `cross_collection_deals_board` (**0.93%** of all `shared_blks_read`) is a victim, and that filing's levers 1–2 would have been real work for no movement.
- ⚠ **THE CIRCULAR INSTRUMENT I USED FIRST, worth more than the conclusion.** I "confirmed" saturation from `cron.job_run_details` busy-seconds by hour (1.1–2.4k healthy vs 4.0–13.0k failing) before noticing every job in that sum is on an hour-uniform schedule — so run COUNT is flat and busy-seconds varies **only because durations do**. It is an OUTPUT of the slowdown measured as if it were the input. **Any "load by hour" built from durations has this defect.**
- ✅ **THE BIGGER FINDING ON THE SAME AXIS:** FMV snapshot production is **~2,800–4,100/hour in 20:00–00:00Z vs 1–466/hour across 01:00–19:00Z** — **~92% of all FMV recalculation happens in a 5-hour window**, every day, three days running. The headline accuracy metric's own pipeline gets ~4 productive hours a day. Filed with the largest single consumer named (`refresh_wmc_fmv_changed`, 8.06% of all reads, p50 pinned at 364–377 s for 19 straight hours = a budget, i.e. a permanent backlog at ~61% duty cycle) and explicitly NOT called causal, because its p50 also drops 15× in the healthy window exactly like every confirmed victim.
- ✅ **THREE PUBLIC BOARDS FIXED, one theme: a suppression justified by a claim about files nobody checked.** `degradedFromSource` skips the degraded notice for `stale-cache` because *"the clients already surface as an age"* — false for 2 of the 5 boards on that ladder. `/insights/rookies` and `/insights/first-mint` carried `meta.fetched_at` and rendered nothing, so a stale snapshot arrived with no banner AND no timestamp. Then, widening the question rather than the population, `/insights/cross-collection` turned out to be **4 days 19 hours stale** with no age at all.
- ⚠ **ON THAT THIRD BOARD THE CONVENIENT FIELD WAS A TRAP.** `meta.fetched_at` is stamped `new Date()` at READ time by both the page and the API route, so rendering it would have printed *"updated seconds ago"* over five-day-old data. **A confident timestamp beside stale data is worse than no timestamp.** The honest instant is `stats.computed_at`, which `select("*")` had always returned and nothing had ever typed or displayed. Its test pins the DISTINCTION (must fall back to `—`, never to the read clock), not the presence of a stamp.
- ⚠ **ESCALATION: the cross-collection mats' refresh has failed EVERY daily run since 08-18, and the 08-18 filing's own trigger — "escalate only if it fails a second consecutive day" — fired three days ago.** That filing's probe (105 s against the 600 s ceiling, "6× headroom") was right; what it could not know is that `rpc-ccm-step1` fires at 04:10Z, inside the 20-hour band, whose 3–18× multiplier consumes exactly that headroom. **There is no longer a clean 04:10Z tick to wait for.** The one-line schedule move into hour 23Z is NOT shipped: `step1` opens with `TRUNCATE`, and the healthy window IS the Pacific afternoon, so there is no window that is both fast and off-hours. Trevor's call, filed with the verification recipe.
- ✅ **TWO INDEPENDENT CI REDS ON `main`, both fixed, both the same shape — async work outliving the thing that scheduled it.** (1) `AdminFeedbackClient`'s debounce test: the 08-18 fake-timer rewrite removed the wall clock and left a second race in the SETUP — the component issues two mount fetches and `mountDashboard` resolves on the heading, so the baseline was 1 on a starved runner and 2 on a fast one. (2) `CollectionTabClient.autoPaginate` wrote state **~300 ms after its own teardown** — every write inside the loop checks `cancelled`, the trailing `setLoadingMore(false)` did not, and it is the one line guaranteed to run late because each iteration opens by awaiting 300 ms.
- ⚠ **CI FAILED WITH EVERY TEST PASSING** on (2): `241 files passed · 2989 tests passed · Errors 1`, exit 1, on a single unhandled rejection. **A green test count is not a green job**, and vitest's unhandled-rejection check is the only thing that caught a real product bug (in a browser the stale run clears the Load More spinner while the NEW wallet is paginating).
- ⚠ **DURABLE, from (1): a fix aimed at ONE source of nondeterminism reads as a fix for the flakiness.** The 08-18 rewrite wrote its reasoning down carefully and left a second race in the same three lines — in the setup, which nobody re-reads once the assertion looks principled. **When a timing test reds again after a timing fix, re-derive the BASELINE, not the assertion.** And assert the baseline (`expect(before).toBe(2)`), because a bare wait passes silently at the wrong value.
- ⚠ **My own new source guard reddened on its own fix's comment**, which quotes the symbol it greps for. CLAUDE.md already records this (six-plus guards have done it); reading the rule is not applying it. **Assume every new source guard needs `stripComments()`.**
- ⚠ **`curl` to `www.rippackscity.com` is DENIED from this sandbox** — the agent proxy answers `403` to `CONNECT`, so a shell `curl` returning `000` is the proxy, not the site. Rendered-DOM verification must go through `mcp__Vercel__web_fetch_vercel_url` against the deployment URL. All three board fixes were verified that way in production.
- ✅ **NEGATIVE RESULT, recorded so nobody re-sweeps it.** 11 of 30 `/insights` boards render no age. I checked whether the cross-collection defect generalises: it does not. The other stampless boards read **plain views** (`relkind='v'` — `v_allday_pack_market`, `v_topshot_pack_market`, `v_allday_pack_realized_ev`, `v_topshot_pack_realized_ev`, `topshot_pack_reality_*`), which are computed at query time and cannot be stale. The single materialised component, `topshot_pack_reality_stats.computed_at`, was **1h24m** old against its own 2-hourly cron — within cadence. **A missing stamp is only a defect where the data can be old**; the sweep stops here.

- **Mutation controls: 12, all red before green** (4 on the two cached boards, 2 on cross-collection, 3 on `autoPaginate`, plus the debounce mutation re-run on the AdminFeedbackClient fix). `tsc` 0 throughout; components **242 files / 2992**, primary **1334 files / 14,434 tests** at the end (measured, not carried forward).

**LATER IN THE SAME SESSION — the honesty class, traced to why the guards never caught it.**

- ✅ **Fast Break: 4 reads whose `error` was never destructured.** ⚠ **supabase-js RETURNS errors rather than throwing**, so each degraded into a confident 200 and logged nothing. The worst: `cheapestListing: null` meant BOTH "we queried and this player has nothing for sale" AND "we never got to ask", and the client renders the first as **"Not currently listed"** — a market claim on the panel whose whole purpose is telling someone what to buy, **actionable in the wrong direction**. Three states now. `nba_games` was the structural one: a failed slate read produced `lineup: null`, byte-identical to the legitimate "no games tonight" an existing test already pinned.
- ⚠ **THEN THE REAL QUESTION: why had ~20 honesty guards missed them?** Because **two of the most relevant explicitly exclude `app/api/**`**, both saying *"already in the primary gate"*. **That gate is COVERAGE, and coverage cannot see an unhandled error branch — the branch does not exist to be uncovered.** A happy-path route test covers an unguarded read 100%. **The sentence reading "covered elsewhere" meant "nothing checks this".** Promoted to CLAUDE.md by displacement; full case + population in [testing-and-ci.md](../reference/testing-and-ci.md).
- ✅ **3 more shipped from that population**, triaged on one criterion — *does a swallowed error become a CLAIM?* `cost-basis` was the sharpest and is **not an empty answer but a DIFFERENT one**: a failed `collection_config` read collapsed to `null`, the caller reads null as "no filter requested", so the RPC returned **every collection the wallet holds inside a single-collection tab**, about the reader's own money. `market-movers` published "nothing moved in 24h" from both a swallowed error AND a bare `catch`, under `s-maxage=300`. `edition-stats` turned a failed lookup into **404 "Edition not found"**.
- ⚠ **MY OWN DETECTOR WAS WRONG BY 8× AND I NEARLY FILED IT.** First measurement said **32**; it used `[^\n;]*?` between `await` and `.from(`, which **cannot cross a newline**, and this repo puts `.from()` on the next line. Real answer **259** (control: **492** reads that DO destructure `error` — without that control the number is equally consistent with a regex matching everything). Caught only because the count barely moved after four fixes. **The defect class I was hunting, inside the instrument built to hunt it.**
- ✅ **Bounded rather than left open:** `workers/` **0 of 11**; `components/` **0 of either** (never touches supabase — which independently confirms the client guards are correctly scoped); `lib/` 21 vs 101, and its clusters are concierge/ingest (off-limits) or benign. ⚠ The `lib/alerts.ts` hits **looked** like CLAUDE.md's worst sub-class (an alert's output is silence) and are **not** — username lookups with an honest wallet-address fallback. **Checked rather than assumed.**
- ⚠ **THREE SELF-CORRECTIONS, all mine.** (1) Withdrew *"`refresh_wmc_fmv_changed` is permanently backlogged"* — always spending your whole budget is *consistent with* a backlog and does not establish one; what IS established is that its temp build costs **~8–9k buffers whether it returns 897 rows, 490, or ZERO**. (2) Qualified my FMV-starvation filing after a concurrent session measured the accuracy gate: the throughput number stands, the **implication** that it caps the headline metric does not — the gate tracks **liquidity** (I re-derived `ufc_strike`: last sale 2026-05-13, **0 sales in both 30 and 90 days**). (3) Marked my own ccm escalation **superseded** when another session showed its blocking `TRUNCATE` trade was an artefact of statement ORDER, not of the work.
- ⚠ **DURABLE, cheap, cost me three full suite runs: NEVER FILTER A TEST LOG BEFORE YOU KNOW THE RUN PASSED.** One primary run reported `1 failed` and I had piped it through `grep -E "Tests |Errors"` — the failing test's identity was in the lines I discarded. Three clean re-runs and CI green, but I could not name it. **The counts are the part you can always recompute.**
- ⚠ **Two CI reds fixed, both "async work outliving what scheduled it"** — and one **failed with EVERY TEST PASSING** (`241 files · 2989 tests passed`, exit 1 on a single unhandled rejection). **A green test count is not a green job.** `autoPaginate` wrote state ~300 ms after its own teardown; in a browser that clears the Load More spinner while a NEW wallet is paginating, so it was a real product bug, not test noise.
- **Totals: 7 route/component fixes + 2 CI fixes shipped, 19 mutation controls all red before green, 4 filings, 3 self-corrections. Ended `tsc` 0, 1335 files / 14,446 tests, six consecutive green CI runs on `main`.**

### August 21, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "proceed" on the indexer cursor filing) — **the last 7 cursor-swallowers fixed, and the population moved 17 → 19 because it was STILL derived from a proxy. 2 commits; no DB, no migration.**

- ✅ **THE SEVEN THAT WERE "TREVOR'S CALL" ARE DONE.** `pinnacle-listings-indexer` got the full partial-scan pattern (it had **no hold at all** — chunk catch neither broke nor recorded, cursor written from `targetHeight` unconditionally); `pinnacle-sales-indexer` got throw + **`break`**, because it writes the cursor PER CHUNK; the 5 `*-sales-history-backfill` crons throw on every non-2xx **except** the 404 whose body says `"is less than"`, which is the node's block floor and a legitimate stop for a backward walk.
- ⚠ **THE DURABLE LESSON, third repetition in one pass: THE POPULATION WAS DERIVED FROM A PROXY, TWICE MORE.** `firstFailedChunkStart` (the cursor HOLD) → 8 files. `async function fetchEventRange` (the fetcher's NAME) → 17. `v1/events` (**the URL, which cannot vary**) → 22. Each proxy leaked, and the name-based one hid **two instances** of the same permanent-loss bug (⚠ **CORRECTED — I first wrote "two LIVE instances"; `app/api/pinnacle/ingest-events` has **0 runs all time** in the indefinitely-retained rollup, so only `backfill-offer-fill-sales` (892 runs, live today) was actually losing data. I had found no callers and moved on instead of treating that as the liveness finding it was**): `lib/pinnacle/flow-events.ts` (`fetchCompletedPinnacleSales` — outside the guard TWICE over, different name *and* outside the `app/api` the grep was scoped to) and `app/api/admin/backfill-offer-fill-sales` (`fetchCompletedRange`).
- ⚠ **MY FIRST ATTEMPT AT `pinnacle-sales-indexer` WAS REJECTED BY THE GUARD, CORRECTLY.** I gated the per-chunk cursor write on `firstFailedChunkStart === null` instead of breaking. The guard's disjunction is cap-or-break, and it was right: gating leaves later chunks doing work the held cursor guarantees will be redone next tick. Changed to `break`, matching `ufc-sales-indexer`.
- ✅ **Two REPORTING defects found on the way, both the fabricated-number shape.** `allday`'s `const newLow = belowFloor ? start : start` — a no-op ternary that READ like a below-floor branch (⚠ the filing generalised it to all five; **re-derived, it was only in allday**). And all five backfills recomputed `cursor_after` from `ceiling - scanWindow` **at log time, independently of whether the write happened** — so a failed tick logged a cursor movement that did not occur, in the one instrument someone would use to notice this whole class.
- ⚠ **FIVE MORE TESTS WERE PINNING THE DEFECT — running total EIGHT for this one class.** Four backfill `-edges` suites asserted *"…and **treats any other status as an empty range**"*; `api-pinnacle-sales-indexer-observability` asserted *"a non-OK response **yields an EMPTY window, and the tick still logs ok:true**"* under a comment calling it *"the silent-loss shape … pinned so the trade-off stays deliberate."* It was not a trade-off. All INVERTED in place, never deleted.
- ⚠ **THE GUARD ALMOST PUNISHED THE CORRECT FIX.** Its swallow test was `return { blocks: [], … }` — but the backfills' legitimate below-floor return IS an empty result. Replaced with `swallowsEmpty()`, which treats an empty return as honest only when it carries the `belowFloor: true` sentinel, and guarded-the-guard with 7 synthetic handlers. Without that, fixing the five would have reddened CI and pushed the next person to weaken the check.
- ⚠ **AND A TRAP WORTH KEEPING: a comment naming a symbol a guard greps for is a MEMBERSHIP CHANGE.** My first offer-fill comment mentioned the sibling fetcher's name; the guard's grep reads source text, the file enrolled itself in the wrong population, and the exact-count arm went 17 → 18. The guard caught it in the same minute.
- **Guard rebuilt on the URL:** `KNOWN_SWALLOWERS` now **empty**; three new arms — every `/v1/events` reader in `app/` + `lib/` must be in the fetcher family, in `NO_BLOCK_CURSOR` **whose property is re-asserted** (the exemption dies the moment one gains a cursor), or in `OTHER_FETCHERS` with its own throw check.
- **Verified:** **29 mutation controls, all RED**, tree restored byte-identical each time — including both directions on the below-floor case (reinstating the swallow reddens, AND making below-floor throw also reddens). `tsc` exit 0; full suite **1327 files / 14,377 tests** green (1326 / 14,351 before). Ledger committed before the code; invariants 3 / 0.

### August 20, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "search the codebase for TODO comments, select one that seems straightforward, and implement it") — **the TODO backlog is empty for the Nth consecutive session, so the work became a hunt for the repo's own most-repeated defect class. 1 code fix + 1 guard widening shipped to `main`, and my own impact claim was REFUTED by the post-deploy check and corrected in the same session.** 3 commits; no DB, no migration, no cron.

- ⚠ **THE PREMISE WAS EMPTY AGAIN, AND RE-DERIVING IT (not quoting CLAUDE.md) WAS THE FIRST DELIVERABLE.** Case-insensitive sweep for `TODO|FIXME|HACK|XXX|TBD` over every tracked non-docs file: **zero actionable markers**. Every hit is a `TODO_`-prefixed env sentinel (`candyDiscoveryReady()` / `candyMeSymbolReady()`, both armed 07-17/07-19), a `TODO_n RESOLVED` annotation, or prose citing a closed item. Both `docs/code-todos.md` items are RESOLVED.
- ✅ **THE FIND — the SEO shallow-merge trap had a THIRD route, and it lived in the gap BETWEEN the two guards that ban it.** `seo-shared-helpers-inherit-og-twitter` is a **curated list** of the three builders inside `lib/seo.ts`; `metadata-inline-blocks-inherit-root-fields` is a **tree walk of `app/` only**. `analyticsMetadata` in `lib/analytics/seo.ts` is a fourth builder — different file, one directory outside the walked tree — and dropped `twitter.site`, `twitter.creator`, `openGraph.locale`. **Fixed at the class level:** the walk now covers `app/` **and** `lib/`, scope widened to "exports route metadata OR imports Next's `Metadata` type" (`\b` keeps `MetadataRoute` out), rather than adding a fourth NAME to a list.
- ✅ **The watcher was shown a FAILURE before being trusted**, per the standing rule: with the new guard in place, reverting the fix redded with exactly `openGraph missing locale` + `twitter missing site, creator`; restored, green. Pre-fix block pinned as **SOURCE, not a path**. Enumerator re-measured **88 files / 97 blocks**; violation set stays at **ZERO** — a ban at population zero, floors left well under so churn does not red CI.
- ⚠ **THE DURABLE LESSON, and it is a correction of my own work: A CALLER COUNT IS NOT A REACHABILITY MEASUREMENT.** I sized the impact as "17 public /analytics surfaces, the most shareable thing RPC has" — the **caller count was right** (17 page files, none re-adds the fields) and **the inference from it was wrong**. `/analytics/**` is **auth-gated**; an anon crawler or X unfurl bot is 302d to `/login` and reads the ROOT metadata, which was correct all along. So the bad tags only ever rendered into a **signed-in** viewer's head — a real defect and a correct fix, but **never a public-unfurl one**. Corrected in the ledger and in both files' comments so the inflated framing is not inherited.
- ⚠ **What caught it is the check CLAUDE.md already mandates — verify by RENDERED DOM, not HTTP 200.** The page returned **200 throughout**; the tell was `x-matched-path: /login` in the response headers and the root's `twitter:site` in the head. Shape-wise this is the existing rule *"never pair a count from one table with a property sampled from another"*, reached through routing rather than SQL.
- **What still earns the guard its keep, stated without the inflation:** not the size of today's population but that a **future `lib/` builder backing a PUBLIC route** is invisible to both existing guards by construction.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · `check-brand-tokens.mjs` clean · full vitest **1314 files / 14176 tests** green at the end (1312/14162 at the first checkpoint) · every deploy checked **PER COMMIT** and READY (`dpl_Dv62Yh…`, `dpl_C8yHdz…`), corroborated on `ready` > `buildingAt` + production aliases + `lambdaRuntimeStats`. Ledger committed BEFORE the code each time so the code commit was the tip and deployed.
- ✅ **CONTINUATION ("keep going") — the same class, one layer out, and this one had been live-wrong for a month.** `lib/sitemap-data.ts` opens with *"The public surface is defined by proxy.ts `isPublicPath`; this file mirrors it."* **Nothing checked that.** Measured: `proxy.ts` **un-gated the read-only feature tabs on 2026-07-17** and the sitemap kept asserting they were auth-gated, so **28 URLs** — anon-200, robots-allowed, self-canonical, each with bespoke per-tab SEO copy — were **never advertised to Googlebot**. Shipped: `PUBLIC_TAB_PAGES` exported from `lib/seo.ts`, the sitemap now emits each collection's own `pages` ∩ that set (segment 0: **46 → 74**), and a **two-direction** guard (nothing listed is gated, **and** nothing anon-public is missing). `proxy.ts` untouched — the guard reads it, nothing writes it. **Verified on the deployed sitemap**, not the builder: all 28 present at 0.7, `x-vercel-cache: MISS`.
- ⚠ **THE OMISSION DIRECTION IS SILENT, AND THAT IS THE DURABLE POINT.** A sitemap listing a GATED url shows up in Search Console as *"Page with redirect"* — someone eventually sees it. A sitemap **omitting a public url produces no signal anywhere**, and the file's own comment read as proof the pages should not be there. Every pre-existing instrument pointed the other way, which is why this survived a month.
- ⚠ **THE EXCLUSION IS THE INTERESTING HALF: 32 tabs pass `isPublicPath`, only 28 shipped.** `pack-sniper` / `challenges` / `hot-floors` have **no `PAGE_META` entry**, so they never call `pageMetadata` and emit **`canonical=/<collection>` — pointing at the collection ROOT, not themselves** (verified live on `/nba-top-shot/challenges`, which also renders the doubled-brand title *"… — Rip Packs City | Rip Packs City"*, **D24 recurring — flagged, NOT fixed**). Listing a self-canonicalising-away URL asks Google to index a declared duplicate. Excluded **by derivation**, never a second list.
- ⚠ **A SECOND CONTROL OF MINE WAS WRONG:** I asserted `/admin/rewards` must read as gated. It returns **TRUE** — `/admin/**` is deliberately allowed past the proxy because it carries its own `RPC_ADMIN_TOKEN` check at the handler. **`isPublicPath` means "the proxy does not 302 this to /login", NOT "an anonymous reader can see the content."** Correct predicate for a sitemap arm; the distinction is now written into the guard so nobody lifts it into an anon-readability claim.
- ⚠ **AND THE TARGETED SWEEP MISSED TWO SUITES — the same curated-list mistake in miniature.** I swept affected tests **by FILENAME KEYWORD**; `candy-launch-flag-contract` and `panini-launch-flag-contract` match none of them, and both pin segment 0's **absolute** length. The **full suite** caught 4 reds. **The correct sweep is "what IMPORTS the module", not "what is named like it."** Fixed all four pins; noted in-file that this is the **third unrelated bump in three weeks** and left the restructure to a deliberate pass — rewriting a go-live contract test's semantics while shipping an unrelated change is how a safety net gets loosened by accident.
- ✅ **THIRD PIECE — chasing WHY the sitemap work excluded three tabs found the defect behind the exclusion.** `pack-sniper` / `challenges` / `hot-floors` (the 2026-07-18 IA reorg's **folded** pages) had no `PAGE_META` entry **and no layout**, so they inherited `collectionLayoutMetadata()` and carried two omissions: **`canonical=/<collection>`, a root that is AUTH-GATED** (`GET /nba-top-shot` → `x-matched-path: /login`) — 4 anon-public URLs declaring their canonical to be a page Googlebot is redirected away from — and **no `FeatureTabGate`**, the component built in the SAME reorg for exactly this, leaving **11 anon-public URLs** (e.g. `/ufc/challenges`, which UFC does not ship) rendering the raw tab. Both fixed in three small layouts.
- ⚠ **MY FIRST FIX WAS WRONG AND THE REPO'S OWN COPY REFUTED IT.** I was about to promote them to self-canonical like their seven siblings — but `PAGE_META.play` already reads *"Play — {label} **Challenges**, Fast Break & Road to the Ring"*, and `packs`/`market` likewise cover the other two, so **promotion would have manufactured query cannibalisation with their own parents.** The consolidation INTENT was right; only the TARGET was broken. Because the three options had materially different indexing outcomes on live public URLs, I **filed the fork and asked** rather than defaulting — Trevor chose canonical→parent.
- ⚠ **A naive version of that choice would have MOVED the broken target:** UFC ships no `play`, so `/ufc/challenges` → `/ufc/play` is another non-existent tab. `foldedTabCanonical()` falls back to **`/overview`** — the one tab every published collection ships, anon-public, self-canonical, sitemapped.
- ⚠ **Provenance stated instead of smoothed over:** the mapping is grounded in `lib/collections.ts`'s own page pitches, but **only `challenges`→`play` is corroborated by a real link** (`PlayHub`). **`pack-sniper` and `hot-floors` have ZERO inbound links anywhere in the app** — direct-URL-only orphans — so two of three mappings are reasoning, not evidence. Recorded in the code, not just the ledger.
- ⚠ **The guard asserts the PROPERTY, not the mapping** — it re-derives every folded canonical across every published collection and asks `isPublicPath` whether a crawler can fetch it. Pinning "challenges → play" would pass just as happily the day `/play` gets gated, which is the whole failure mode. Proven able to fail (pre-fix return value reds 3 of 6 arms). **Verified live:** `/nba-top-shot/challenges` → `canonical=/nba-top-shot/play` with the gate a transparent pass-through; `/ufc/challenges` → `canonical=/ufc/overview` with the pointer shell rendered.
- ⚠ **A date trap caught in time:** the sandbox is **PDT**, so `date -u` read 2026-08-21 while PT was still 08-20. My drafted ledger heading said 08-21 — future-dated in PT, which `find-future-dated-ledger-headings.mjs` would have redded. Checked `date '+%Z'` before stamping, per the standing rule. Inbox filenames stay UTC (that directory's convention); ledger `###` headings are PT.
- ✅ **FOURTH PIECE ("do it all") — the `after()` invocation heartbeat, and a COLLISION that cost me most of the work.** CLAUDE.md's one documented honesty class with no ratchet. I built the whole item independently (helper, 10 unit tests, tree-walk ratchet at 53, the 3 candy migrations) and a **concurrent session landed the same thing while I was running the suite**. I `git reset --hard origin/main` rather than force mine over it: **theirs is better on two counts** (idempotent `heartbeatPipelineName`, a pure `buildHeartbeatRow`) and its ratchet is stricter (`toBe`, no slack, wider population — 66 vs my 53). Contributed only what was missing: **the four `maxDuration = 800` routes** (none in their five seeds) and **BUDGET 66 → 62**, which their no-slack arm demanded by name.
- ⚠ **AN "IMPROVEMENT" I ALMOST SHIPPED WAS WRONG, AND CHECKING CAUGHT IT.** My helper made `phase` un-clobberable; theirs lets a caller override it. I was about to harden it — their tests **pin the override deliberately**, with the trade-off spelled out (a multi-stage `after()` may want `phase: "resumed"`, at the cost that a correlation query keyed on `'started'` misses it). A considered decision with its downside recorded is not an oversight, and "fixing" it would have inverted the test holding it.
- ⚠ **MY OWN DEPLOY THEN ERRORED, AND ONLY A PER-COMMIT CHECK FOUND IT.** `a1a5f4f9` went ERROR; production stayed on the prior commit, so my work sat on `main` **not in production** — the exact trap CLAUDE.md names. **Not my change, established not asserted:** my diff was 4 cron handlers (dynamic, never prerendered) + a test constant; the commit 6 min earlier was READY; measured live, **35 active connections and 12 waiting on `DataFileRead`** — a saturation spell in progress, so I did **not** redeploy into it.
- ✅ **BUT THE PAGE THAT FAILED WAS A REAL DEFECT — THE THIRD OF ITS CLASS.** `app/insights/page.tsx` read `get_insights_hub_stats` through a service-role client with **no bound at all**, while every board page bounds at 8 s. A service-role RPC has **no server-side bound** (the role timeout applies at LOGIN; PostgREST logs in as `authenticator`), so the client is the only bound there is. ⚠ **And the ban written for exactly this could not see it:** `insights-server-pages-bound-their-reads` derived its population as `readdirSync(INSIGHTS_DIR)` **filtered to directories**, and the hub is a **FILE** in that directory — outside the guard **by construction**. The guard's own header says the class recurred twice because fixes went to the page rather than the shape; **this is that lesson one level up.** Fixed both halves: the hub bounds with `withBoardBudget` (null is already an honest render path), and the walk is now `[".", ...readdirSync(...)]`.
- ⚠ **AND THE POST-DEPLOY CLAIM STATED PRECISELY.** The rebuild passed in 2 m and `/insights` prerendered — **but `[insights/hub] read exceeded 8000ms` never appears, so the bound did NOT fire**, and saturation had eased in between (35 → 7 active). **The passing build is therefore not evidence the bound works**; the evidence is the control (reverting the page reds 2 arms). "It built fine after my fix" is the same shape as reading a cache hit as a correct change.
- ⚠ **One inference of mine corrected mid-flight:** the 811 s → 1,012 s process during the spell was `autovacuum: VACUUM public.wallet_moments_cache` — a **maintenance process**, which I first mis-read as a client RPC beating the worst-observed 352 s `supabaseAdmin` call. Filed as a HYPOTHESIS only: the 08-18 **2% autovacuum scale factor** on that exact table plausibly contributes to spells like this, and **a plausible mechanism is not a measurement** — it needs a before/after on autovacuum frequency and IO.
- **Left alone deliberately:** CLAUDE.md is at its size equilibrium and the lesson above is a specialisation of a rule it already states, so it was **not** promoted there — forcing a displacement to restate an existing rule would cost more than it adds.
- ✅ **CONTINUATION (5× "keep going", "do it all", "do all of them") — worked the whole ranked list in `docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-20T2325Z-test-coverage-analysis.md`. Area (8) is now CLOSED.** Four more ship-and-verify cycles, each with mutation controls proving the new tests can fail, each with its own ledger entry.
- ✅ **`rpc-mcp-proxy` — 31 cases, `index.ts` 74.74 → 95.37 st / 53.75 → 81.29 br.** The worker is the only thing between a loosely-typed LLM tool call and a Postgres function with typed parameters, so **every case asserts the BODY the worker POSTed**, not the MCP envelope: a coercion that silently stopped happening still returns a well-formed response. 10 mutations, all RED. - ⚠ **THE NIGHT'S DURABLE RULE, now promoted into CLAUDE.md: A NUMBER IS NOT IMMUNITY.** CLAUDE.md already said a filed decision-not-to-act is a hypothesis and *"the tell is a cost stated with no number in it."* The migration-parity job's posture had a number — **114 fileless migrations** — and a written exit condition: *"delete the `|| true` once the backlog is cleared."* Re-derived against the live table: **114 → 5**, and the 3-day window was already **0**. - ⚠ **Deleting `|| true` alone would have been the WRONG fix.** Every CI `run:` block is `bash -e`, so a bare failing pipeline aborts **on the npm line** and leaves both grep annotations — the ones naming WHICH migrations drifted — as **dead code that reads as coverage**, on precisely the run where they matter. Now captures with `|| RC=$?`, annotates, `exit "$RC"`; exit 2 (could not RUN) fails too. **Verified by simulation under real `bash -e` for exit 0/1/2**, not by reading.
- **CLAUDE.md displacement done properly:** the new rule pushed the file to 40,144 (over). Two already-mirrored bullets compressed and one non-mirrored passage moved **verbatim** into `tooling-gotchas.md` with a note. Final **39,879 — 121 chars headroom**, better than the 52 it started at. Counted with `node -e`, never `wc`.

Retired the weekly cron instead (37 → 36), route and `?wmc=1` capability kept. **The unlock is reusable: an ABSENT key is a cheap index seek and a PRESENT one is not** (6,561 absent-key probes returned instantly; 2,000 present keys took 20 s at 10.1 ms/seek with `Heap Fetches: 1054/1999`), **so proving emptiness is often tractable where the existence scan is not.** Guard `__tests__/fossil-drain-schedule-is-retired.test.ts`, 8/8 mutations killed.

UFC's `external_id` is a hyphenated human slug (`KHAMZAT-CHIMAEV-UFC-267-SUBMISSION-23970`) with a legitimately NULL `set_id_onchain`, so it matched **518/518 by construction** while correctly excluding Top Shot's 6,561 UUID phantoms. ⚠ **The symptom sat in every run's own output for months — `"ufc_strike", "processed": 0` beside non-zero siblings, inside an `ok: true` run.** Verified in production: UFC editions with a real price went **149 → 381 (28.8% → 73.6%)**, average FMV age **68.7 → 10.8 days**. ⚠ **Scoped honestly — UFC has zero sales in 90 days, so this is `ASK_ONLY`/`STALE` labelled prices replacing NULL: coverage, not freshness.**
- ✅ **SHIPPED — impossible-parallel self-heal cut hourly → 6-hourly** (jobid 219). Re-derived all four safety grounds rather than trusting the filing; re-measured the cost rather than quoting it (**5.74-day window: 134 calls, mean 47 s, 161.9 GB read, 6.4% hit ratio, for 6 productive hours out of 168**). ~6× cut, ~23 GB/day returned to the IO-throttled instance. **Verified end to end:** it skipped five hourly slots, fired at 00:52Z, succeeded in 167 s, kept `cron_heavy` ownership, and **healed one real offender on its first 6-hourly run**.
- ⚠ **REFUTED — the sports-proxy 403 is NOT "operator-only, it's a secret".** That label sat in **five** places on the item CLAUDE.md calls the single highest-value one. Three grounds, all from data already on hand: the proxy returns **502 with `*_upstream_status` populated** while a bad secret returns **401 before any upstream is touched**; the edge function's own `!SPORTS_PROXY_URL || !SPORTS_PROXY_SECRET` guard never fired; and **the ESPN lane uses no proxy and no secret and 403s too.** ⚠ **My replacement model was ALSO wrong** — a later residential test showed **two causes, not one**: ESPN returns **200 residentially** (⇒ egress blocking, Worker-fixable, no secret) while `cdn.nba.com` 403s residentially on every path with a current UA and full headers. My "don't ship the UA refresh" call held; my causal story did not.
- ⚠ **SETTLED — `anon`'s declared 3 s `statement_timeout` DOES NOT BIND, and the answer is the bad one.** Modelling the real path (`SET LOCAL statement_timeout='8s'`, then `SET LOCAL ROLE anon`): `current_user` becomes `anon` while the timeout stays **8s**. **The pivot nobody had looked at is that `authenticator` — the role PostgREST actually LOGS IN as — carries its own `statement_timeout=8s`**, and that one binds because `rolconfig` applies at login. Corroborated: authenticator statements show **max 7,474 ms with zero over 8 s**. So `authenticated`'s 8 s is right **by coincidence**. ⚠ Do not generalise to "8 s everywhere" — `service_role` reaches 352 s on the same pool. **The proven half is the negative: there is no 3 s bound on unauthenticated compute, so the argument for leaving 78 anon-executable functions is weaker than recorded.**
- ⚠ **A watchlist coverage audit turned three anecdotes into a number: 62 of 149 live pipelines (41.6%) are unwatched**, 5 with zero successes, plus **15 watchlist rows pointing at pipelines that no longer run.** **A curated list drifts in both directions at once — the durable fix is the DERIVATION, not the membership.** ⚠ **Four of the five failing ones WRITE ROWS while failing, which is the `Pipeline Success Coverage` arm's coverage boundary and had never been written down** (its `rows_written` term earned its place by removing 4 false positives, but blinds it to "writes rows and never completes"). Recorded beside the arm, explicitly **not** as a reason to weaken it.
- ⚠ **FOUR NEW WAYS A PIPELINE'S OWN REPORTING LIES**, promoted to `docs/reference/cron-and-schedulers.md` so they outlive this log: (1) **a per-collection ZERO inside an otherwise-succeeding run is the shape a collection-blind filter makes** — "nothing to do" and "structurally excluded" produce the identical number; (2) **an identical `rows_written` across a SUCCESS and a FAILURE is the signature of a stale cache** (`ownership-sync-dune`: exactly 114,083 on its success and both `HTTP 402` failures); (3) **an `ok` that ANDs every step's error slot can never be true if a step is expected to be cut off** — which hid a *successful repair* on `drain-conflated-subeditions` (knots **76 → 272**, rows/run **0 → ~1,000**, still `ok: false` every run); (4) **`cron.job_run_details.status` under-reports** — the board-liveness sweep shows 3/10 failed to cron but **5/10 blew its own 600 s internal budget**, and for a `net.http_get` job cron status only means **the enqueue succeeded**.
- ⚠ **THE DISCIPLINE THAT PAID REPEATEDLY: measure the OUTCOME, not the pipeline's status — it killed four of my own confident inferences.** The `fmv-recalc` "treadmill" looked conclusive (no cursor keys, uniform ~497-row runs, the route's own header warning about it) until the outcome showed **13,835 distinct editions/day**; the panini dry-days arm looked like a false alarm (832 priced sales in 7 days) until reading its definition showed it measures the **raw upstream field** while `last_sale_usd` survives across captures; a misattrib backlog first read **6.5%** and was **1.0%** once `::subedition` suffixes were split off; and "TopShot ownership is going stale" was false because the on-chain walk half had recovered. **A convincing mechanism is not a measurement, and neither is a convincing refutation.**
- ⚠ **AND THE SAME TRAP IN MY OWN VERIFICATION, three times: a check is silent about whatever its derivation excluded.** I reported a correction "lost" in a concurrent CLAUDE.md restructure — my sweep covered `CLAUDE.md` + `docs/reference/` and omitted `docs/sessions/`, so the content was outside it **by construction**. Later a case-sensitive grep for `slate-GATED` missed `slate-gated`. And a CLAUDE.md margin I reported as "209 chars" was **bytes** (`wc -c`), not characters — a concurrent session caught the same trap and the file now mandates `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8 wc -m`.
- ✅ **Answered the daytime monitor's team-roster honesty check: NO defect.** The Sentry string *"team roster unavailable: rpc get_team_players timed out after 45000ms"* **IS** the structural throw from `sectionRows(..., {structural: true})`; it reaches `app/global-error.tsx` and never renders an empty roster. Pinned by 13/13 tests including *"an empty result is NOT an error"*. ⚠ **Transferable: a Sentry event on an already-hardened surface is evidence the hardening WORKS and reads identically to a new defect — the discriminator is the error STRING.**
- **Left open deliberately, with reasons:** the sports-proxy ESPN lane (Worker route, no secret — but ⚠ **restoring it will NOT refill `nba_players`**, because the roster fetch is built from the day's slate and the season ended 08-04; the cheap fix nobody proposed is a **slate-independent sweep over the 30 team IDs**, which `fetchEspnRoster` already supports); the panini `mapping_shortfall` arm, which is **≤ 0 by construction while the supply is dry** and so cannot fire (three redefinition options filed); `ownership-sync-dune`'s **HTTP 402** (a billing call); `compute-golazos-pack-ev`'s silence, **unconfirmed either way** because cron status cannot answer it; and a watchlist row for `sync-nba-projections`, which would page correctly on a 13-day outage nobody can fix until one `curl` runs.
- ⚠ **Housekeeping for whoever reads this next:** the ledger took **three concurrent-session rebase conflicts**, each resolved by the documented recipe (re-splice into `git show :2:`, gate the `git add` on the resolver's exit code, assert headings **+1** and swallowed **3**) — all three verified lossless afterward by content, not by date. ⚠ **A retry loop I wrote was blocked by the permission classifier and correctly so** — its fallback would have overwritten the ledger with upstream's copy and silently dropped my entry. ⚠ **And a `cron.job.command` read echoed a live gate key into the session transcript; treat that transcript accordingly.**

- ⚠ **SECOND STRETCH ("do the probing") — I RAN THE FILED-BUT-NEVER-RUN `proconfig` EXPERIMENT, AND THE REASON IT HAD NEVER BEEN RUN WAS ITSELF WRONG.** This file said the decisive test "**cannot be tested from an MCP connection**" and needed an HTTP call with the service-role key. **It does not.** A PostgREST RPC is just `SELECT fn(...)` as a top-level statement, and **there is no way to call a function except from a top-level statement** — so the recorded "the timer was already armed by an outer `SELECT`" caveat does not describe a *distinct* case, it describes *every* case. **A blocker stated as a property of the tooling turned out to be a property of nobody having questioned it.**
- ✅ **RESULT: the rule HOLDS for the route path — `proconfig` is inert in BOTH directions**, on a SECURITY DEFINER function executing as `service_role`: a 60 s declaration under a 2 s session was **canceled at 2 s**, and a 2 s declaration under a 120 s session **ran to completion**. So the "stop citing this rule as measured for the route path" caveat is retired. ⚠ Probed in **`pg_temp` deliberately** — `pgrst_ddl_watch()`/`pgrst_drop_watch()` both skip `pg_temp` explicitly, so it caused **no schema-cache reload and none of the user-facing 500s** a `public` probe would have.
- ⚠ **AND SETTLING IT EXPOSED A LARGER ERROR THAT THIS FILE STATES AS FACT IN THREE PLACES: `service_role`'s 30 s DOES NOT BIND ON THE POSTGREST PATH.** `rolconfig` applies **at LOGIN**; PostgREST logs in as `authenticator` and only `SET LOCAL ROLE`s, and a `SET ROLE` does not inherit the target role's config — measured directly, `SET ROLE service_role` leaves `statement_timeout` at **`2min`**. At scale (`pg_stat_statements` is keyed per user, so it names the caller): of **871** distinct `service_role` PostgREST statements, **39 exceed 30 s, 10 exceed the 120 s global, worst 352,318 ms**, several with a **mean** over 100 s. **No Postgres timeout bounds a `supabaseAdmin` RPC; the bound is the CLIENT** — the 297–300 s cluster is Vercel's `maxDuration`.
- ⚠ **The `wallet_usernames_unresolved` "counter-evidence" therefore dissolves, and it came within one inference of overturning a correct rule.** Nothing was stopping it at 30 s, so its 58.2 s runtime needs no explanation — **and its match to the declared 60 s is a coincidence.** ⚠ **I checked my own tidy hypothesis and it was WRONG**: I expected `pg_stat_statements` to show the 58.2 s max coming from a `postgres` diagnostic call under the 120 s global (i.e. the investigation manufacturing its own counter-evidence). The row is genuine `service_role` PostgREST traffic. **A satisfying story is not a measurement, including when it flatters the file.**

### August 17, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "search the codebase for TODO comments, implement one" → "work on any issues you encounter" → "keep going" ×4 → "keep going on anything still unresolved, then commit what you need to memory") — **the TODO backlog was empty for the third session running, so every find came from incidentals; the headline is that FIVE separate tests were pinning the defect they were named to prevent, and the sharpest correction was of MY OWN filing — a decision not to ship that rested on a cost estimate I never measured.** 8 code/test pushes + ~10 ledger/docs pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, **CI confirmed green per commit** (`15bb2a6e` · `307359ad` · `83329bc0` · `ba16da63` · `12c55422` · `9a83416c` · `36c1356f` · `5eda629f` · `50aa0438`). No DB, migration, cron-schedule, auth, hot-wallet or pricing change.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE IS A FILING OF MY OWN THAT TALKED ME OUT OF A SHIPPABLE FIX, AND THE MECHANISM GENERALIZES: its decisive argument was a COST ESTIMATE WITH NO MEASUREMENT BEHIND IT.** I filed the 9-route saturation fail-OPEN as "correctly not shipped" because "each route has its own bespoke chainable stub, and making *only* the throttle read fail requires per-file sequencing in nine differently-shaped stubs". **False, and cheap to check: the throttle is the FIRST read and it RETURNS EARLY**, so on the failing path the route never reaches another query and a blanket-error stub cannot be ambiguous — one behavioural test plus a directory-driven source guard covers all nine and any tenth. Shipped hours later (`5eda629f`). ⚠ **A concurrent docs pass sharpened this and its version is the one to keep: this file tells you to re-derive a filed FINDING and is SILENT on a filed DECISION NOT TO ACT — and a not-shipped rationale is the hypothesis nobody re-checks, because declining to act reads as the conservative choice.** The tell is specific: a cost stated with no number in it. I spent this same session disproving three other people's unmeasured claims.
- ⚠ **THE CLASS THAT PRODUCED MOST OF THE SESSION: `?? 0` ON A COUNT REACHING GUARDS, WHERE THE OUTPUT IS SILENCE AND THEREFORE UNFALSIFIABLE.** `stale-fmv-monitor` read two orphan counts as `count ?? 0`, so a `57014` became "0 orphans" and **suppressed the integrity alert — it reported the data sound from a read it never performed** (now three states: true/false/**null**, with `data_integrity_checked` beside it so a consumer cannot misread the null). Behind it, the structural form: **nine cron routes whose self-throttle fails CLOSED on a throw and OPEN on a returned error, in the same block** — so the guard is disabled exactly during the saturation it detects, self-reinforcingly, and **nothing measured how often it failed open because a failed-open tick is indistinguishable from a healthy one in `pipeline_runs`.** ⚠ **`Promise.all` around it looks protective and is not**: supabase-js RESOLVES `{count: null, error}`, so nothing rejects and the `catch` never fires.
- ⚠ **FIVE TESTS PINNED THE DEFECT THEY WERE NAMED TO PREVENT, and every one had a correct half — so all five were INVERTED, never deleted.** The purest was `component-DashboardClient`'s *"survives a failed saved-wallets read without claiming none are saved"*, whose entire body was `expect(fetchMock).toHaveBeenCalled()` — **it passes whatever the page renders, so it certified the exact defect its name forbids.** ⚠ **The tell is in the TITLE: a name carrying a negative claim ("without claiming…", "does not show…") or a transformation ("is not an error", "at or below FMV") is a promise that the assertion is usually too weak to keep.** Open those; a well-named vacuous case reads as covered in every grep, review and coverage report, and **mutation testing cannot find it.**
- ⚠ **A GUARD THAT PINS MORE THAN ITS PROPERTY BLOCKS THE FIX FOR THE DEFECT NEXT DOOR.** `invariants-postgrest-cap` exists for the count-not-`.length` property, but it matched the literal `count ?? 0`, so correcting a site to the strictly better `?? null` **reddened CI** — the guard billing an improvement as a regression. Widened to accept either fallback. **Pin the property, never the spelling** — same family as the five inverted tests, which each honoured a real requirement the wrong way.
- ⚠ **A FALSE GREEN IS WORSE THAN A FALSE ZERO, and this is the first instance of the honesty class where the failed read renders as an ALL-CLEAR.** `PipelineHealthBadge` painted **green "healthy"** out of a failed `/api/pipeline-health` read — a false zero is at least a number a reader can doubt, whereas green is a verdict about our own ingest. Now a neutral `unknown` ("Status unavailable"), ⚠ **deliberately NOT red** (a fetch failure is not evidence of a degraded pipeline — that would be the mirror-image lie), with the attention-drawing ping re-gated so it cannot fire on a state we did not measure.
- ⚠ **Instance 8 of "a name filter is not an emptiness test", on `/analytics`.** `InsiderSignals` rendered **"No recent buybacks detected"** when the buyback feed had loaded fine and every row simply failed the moment-name join — a claim about the MARKET manufactured from a gap in OUR catalog. Three states, never two: read failed · genuinely empty · **loaded but unnameable** ("N recent buybacks not yet matched to a moment"), plus inline disclosure when the list is partially filtered.
- ⚠ **THE `|| 1` DIVIDE-GUARD FIX HAD AN UNFIXED SIBLING FOR FOUR DAYS — third instance of grep-for-the-EXPRESSION-not-the-file.** `CollectionProfileClient` carried the identical `((last - first) / (sparkData[0] || 1)) * 100`, so the per-collection profile kept publishing **"↑ 50000.0% / 30D"** while the fix's detailed comment sat in the sibling file. ⚠ **A comment is only read by someone already in that file** — which is why the class is now a **BAN at population ZERO** (`no-fabricated-divisor-ratchet`). Two design notes worth keeping: it is anchored on the `/` so a legitimate `parseInt(x) || 1` parse fallback is not flagged, and ⚠ **its escape hatch had to look 3 lines back, because these expressions WRAP and the `|| 1` lands on a different line from the only sensible place to justify it — an escape hatch unreachable in the common case teaches people to delete the guard instead.** Also: nulling the ratio alone would have painted a genuine $0 → $500 GAIN in the loss colour, so direction now derives from the series.
- ⚠ **MY OWN CLOSING CLAIM FROM THE PREVIOUS ROUND — "the leftover `?? 0`-on-count sites are all telemetry" — WAS FALSE, and going to check it is what found the user-facing one.** `/api/profile/market-pulse` published **"0 snapshots / 0 editions indexed"**: a claim that our own FMV index is empty, out of our own outage. ⚠ The tell was already inside the route — its whole-route failure object returns `commonFloor: null` **beside** `indexedEditions: 0`, i.e. it knew how to say "unknown" and used it for the floors only. **A no-more-instances claim is a hypothesis.**
- ⚠ **"GREEN IN ISOLATION, RED IN THE SUITE" IS A DIAGNOSIS, NOT A FLAKE LABEL — it means a missing synchronisation.** `main` had been red on two jobs; one was a Dashboard case awaiting a render that only happens after a second fetch resolves (fixed with a `waitFor` on that specific call, not a timeout bump). The other was a stub that **never followed a split**: `apply-fmv-haircut` was split per collection days earlier and its test still returned one aggregate payload from a shared builder, so the per-leg assertions were reading the wrong shape. **When a route is split, its stub has to be split too** — 9 → 16 cases with per-leg distinct payloads and a summed control.
- ⚠ **A 429 ON A CI DEPENDENCY FETCH REDDENED `main` ON A COMMIT THAT TOUCHED NO CADENCE FILE.** `scripts/fetch-cadence-escrow-test-deps.sh` `curl`ed a pinned contract with **no retry**, so a transient GitHub rate-limit failed the `cadence-escrow-tests` job and read as "your change broke the escrow suite". Verified 0 `.cdc` files in the diff, re-ran (9/9 green), then fixed the cause: retry with 2/4/8 backoff, **reject an empty body** (a 429 page can arrive with a 200-shaped exit), and name the failing file in the final error. **A network fetch in CI with no retry is a red build attributable to whoever pushed next.**
- ⚠ **`decodeURIComponent` DOES NOT DECODE `+`, and four of my assertions failed against perfectly correct code because of it.** A `URLSearchParams`-encoded space arrives as `+`; `decodeURIComponent` leaves it. Parse the value the way the consuming page does (`new URLSearchParams(...)`), not with the function whose name sounds right.
- ⚠ **The unanchored conflict-marker check fired on a CORRECT ledger write again — 5th instance.** An `"<<<<<<<" in out` test trips on prose that *quotes* the markers, which the ledger now does in several entries describing previous marker incidents. Anchored to line start (`^<<<<<<< `, `^=======$`, `^>>>>>>> `) and ⚠ **run as DELTA checks against measured baselines** (markers 0, swallowed 3, no-blank-before-heading 303) — this file's own rule that a ledger check whose baseline you have not measured is a check you do not know the meaning of.
- ⚠ **A background CI monitor I set up would have stayed silent FOREVER, and silence would have read as success.** `curl` to the GitHub API is **403 CONNECT** from this sandbox, so the poll loop could never observe a conclusion. Killed it and used the MCP tool. **Before relying on a watcher, prove it can see a FAILURE** — an unreachable monitor and a green build produce the same output.
- ⚠ **An unstable mock object makes an effect re-run and double-fire.** A router mock rebuilt per render re-triggered a funnel event on every `setMessage`; one hoisted object fixed it. Cheap to cause, and it presents as a product bug in the assertion output.
- **Verified:** all 9 CI jobs green on the final tip, attempt 1 (so the retry hardening did not itself need a retry — the dep-fetch step completed in 1 s). `tsc` clean and all three coverage gates green on every push. The 9-route fix is pinned by a directory-driven **BAN at population zero** plus a behavioural test asserting all three directions, incl. that a healthy count still opens the gate — a fix that permanently throttled nine backfills would be worse than the defect.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, docs, FOURTH pass of the day — "analyze repo → update CLAUDE.md to current state") — docs-only pass on tip `969bff49`. **Only FIVE commits had landed since this file was last written and three were docs, so the yield should have been nothing. Instead the sharpest find is a measurement error of MY OWN that produced a confident "the wedge cleared" reading about a pipeline that is still wedged — caused by aggregating a `text` column with `max()`.** No product/DB/prod change; direct to `main`. Ledger entry 2026-08-16.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE IS A FOOTGUN I WALKED INTO AND VERIFIED BEFORE PUBLISHING: `pipeline_runs.cursor_before` / `cursor_after` are `text`, so `max(cursor_after)` is a LEXICOGRAPHIC max.** Over the last 7 h of `fmv-recalc` it returns **`'9500'`** where `max(cursor_after::numeric)` returns **11500**, because `'9500' > '11500'` as a string. Combined with an `ok`-count over a window that straddled the PREVIOUS cycle's successes (11 of 13 terminal runs ok — all of them before the wedge), it produced a tidy, wrong story: *the cursor advanced to 9500 and the pipeline recovered.* **It is still wedged at 2500.** ⚠ **The failure direction is the dangerous one — it makes a stalled sweep look like an advancing one**, and the corrected reading is worse than the file's: **4.33 h, 29 invocations, 2 terminal rows, 0 successes, 27 killed (93.1%)**, against the 3.65 h / 91% recorded four hours earlier. Both failures at stage `step1b_refetch_empty`. **Cast to numeric, or read rows in `started_at` order rather than aggregating.** Recorded in the `fmv_snapshots` section; the trust board is back to **4 breached**.
- ⚠ **`fmv_sweep_wedge_hours` and `fmv_sweep_stall_pct_24h` DISAGREED, and this file told me to treat them as one signal.** The wedge arm breached at 4.32 while its sibling sat at **40.4 / ok** — correctly, and the wedge arm's own `catches` text says so outright: the stall arm measures restarts at `cursor_before = 0`, and **a sweep stuck at an INTERIOR offset never restarts at 0**. So the "two arms, one signal" note earned from their clearing together does not generalize to their breaching. **Read both; expect disagreement.** Corrected in place rather than deleted, since the earlier reading was fair on its own data.

Class is now a **BAN at population zero**; its `> 0` guard must not be reduced to `Number.isFinite` (`Number.isFinite(0)` is true), and its escape hatch had to look 3 lines back because these expressions wrap — **an escape hatch unreachable in the common case teaches people to delete the guard.**

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — continuation of the test-coverage thread: "keep going on the remaining client page conversions" → "Keep working through all of them still open. Don't stop until you are completely done") — **the workstream is FINISHED: the client-page gate ratchet is drained to its floor and the only page left is a hard 404. The single live defect was a guard that compared a value to itself, and the sharpest process lesson was a green-looking gate that was actually failing.** 2 code pushes + 2 ledger pushes to `main`, both deploys verified READY per commit, `tsc` clean and every gate green on each. No DB, migration, cron, auth, hot-wallet or pricing change.

- **What shipped.** The **auth funnel** (`/login`, `/early-access`, `/auth/confirm` → `*Client.tsx`, 69 tests, 23 mutations) and the **sniper** (1,817 lines, the last big client page, 85 tests, 6 mutations). Ratchets **5 → 1** (gate) and **7 → 4** (fetch-honesty), both re-derived from their own no-slack assertions rather than decremented by hand.
- ⚠ **THE LIVE DEFECT IS A TAUTOLOGY, AND IT IS THE PINNACLE-DRIFT-GUARD SHAPE ON THE CLIENT.** `EarlyAccessClient`'s on-blur wallet nudge guarded a stale answer with `wallet.trim() === w` — but `w` is computed FROM `wallet` at the top of the same function and both are the render-scoped const that closure captured, so it is **a value compared against itself and can never be false**. A slow `/api/wallet-search` answer about an address the user had already corrected rendered as a verdict on the address now in the box: *"this wallet shows 0 Top Shot moments — double-check it"* about a wallet we never checked. **Actionable in the wrong direction** — it tells someone to "fix" a correct address. Fixed with a latest-value ref; a mutation restoring the tautology reds.
- ⚠ **THE GATE EXITED 1 WITH ZERO COVERAGE ERRORS AND ALL FOUR METRICS PASSING — read the exit code, never the coverage table.** A deep-link test schedules `scrollIntoView` inside a `requestAnimationFrame`; jsdom does not implement it, the frame fires AFTER the scheduling test ends, and it surfaces as an **unhandled error attributed to whatever test happened to be running** — a completely unrelated case. Vitest's own warning is that this "might cause false positive tests". I saw `90.62 / 81.79 / 89.25 / 93.54` against thresholds and a red build twice before reading the tail.
- ⚠ **THE CONVERSION COST IS BIGGER THAN THIS FILE RECORDS, and it should be budgeted as test-writing, not as a rename.** The documented lesson is that a conversion moves `% Funcs` down. At 1,817 lines the sniper took **ALL FOUR thresholds red at once** (89.37 / 80.53 / 87.68 / 92.35) and needed **six** measure-then-target rounds to recover — the file went 48.7 → 88.2 statements and **31.8 → 86.9 functions**. Thresholds were never lowered. **Measure per-file uncovered FUNCTIONS from the HTML report and target them; statements follow, functions do not.**
- ⚠ **A DEAD MODAL, reported rather than fixed.** On the sniper, `setSelectedDeal` is called in exactly one place — `onClose={() => setSelectedDeal(null)}` — so nothing ever sets it to a deal and `MomentDetailModal` **can never open**; the row click navigates instead. Left alone deliberately: wiring it would CHANGE row-click behaviour (a product decision) and deleting it would discard a latent feature. **Its branches are permanently unreachable, so do not chase them for coverage.**
- ⚠ **A GUARD THAT NAMES ITS INSTANCES DIES ON ITS OWN SUCCESS — third recorded instance, and this one was mine to trip.** `helpers-client-directive.test.ts` named `app/login/page.tsx`, `app/early-access/page.tsx` and `app/auth/confirm/page.tsx` as its real-world cases, so converting them killed it with **ENOENT**. Re-derived by WALKING the app tree and cross-checking `isClientSource` against an **independent line-scan oracle**: satisfiable at a population of ZERO, survives any rename, and covers every file rather than three. Mutating the detector back to its old prefix scan reds it; a guards-the-guard case proves the oracle is a real second opinion rather than a copy.
- ✅ **`scripts/check-brand-tokens.mjs` IS THE DESIGN TO COPY for a curated list.** Its 4 entries named the old `page.tsx` paths and it **fails LOUDLY on a missing file** (`violations++`), which is what makes a rename a forced repoint instead of a silent loss of coverage. A curated list that skipped missing files would have quietly stopped covering the entire auth funnel.
- ⚠ **FOUR OF MY OWN TEST BUGS, ONE FAMILY: READ THE INTERFACE, DO NOT INVENT IT.** The `SniperDeal` fixture guessed `price` / `fmv` / `discountPct` / `serialNumber` / `fmvConfidence` where the real fields are `askPrice` / `adjustedFmv` / `discount` / `serial` / `confidence`; **`RelativeDeal` in the SAME file is snake_case**; the `getOwnerKey` mock returned `"owner-1"` against an effect that early-returns on `!key.startsWith("0x")`, so the fetch under test never ran **and the test asserting only that the board still rendered passed VACUOUSLY**; and `localStorage` is REAL in jsdom, so one test's 10-minute owned-ids cache made the next take the early-return branch. **An invented shape does not error — it renders blanks, and every assertion fails looking like a missing element.**
- ⚠ **Four mutations survived first time and EVERY ONE was a fixture gap, not a code defect** — a 503 whose empty body let `typeof tm === "number"` mask the status check; a wallet-only payload that could not observe the wallet's own `|| null`; an edit to a MALFORMED address that hid a warning via the VALIDITY gate rather than the clear; and a success-path assertion for `setError("")` that could not see it because success unmounts the whole form. **Sort every survivor before touching code.**
- ⚠ **A concurrent session CORRECTED a comment I wrote this session, and they were right.** I claimed an unguarded `.text()` reject "would escape into the outer catch"; it does not — that call sits inside the `try { … } catch (touchErr)` already wrapping the touch block. They measured it by mutation (dropping `.catch(() => "")` alone leaves every case green) and documented it as redundant-behind-another-guard rather than contriving a fixture. Their correction is in the tree. A second session also converted `auth/confirm` in parallel and correctly dropped its own commit with `rebase --skip` rather than merging duplicate work.
- ⚠ **The unanchored conflict-marker check bit AGAIN while writing the ledger — 4th instance.** `assert "<<<<<<<" not in out` fired on a perfectly clean file because the ledger *quotes* the markers in prose at ~line 602 while describing a previous marker incident. The recipe already says to anchor it (`^<<<<<<< ` / `^=======$` / `^>>>>>>> `). **Anchoring the splice and leaving the verification unanchored is the easy half-fix, and it is the one people keep making.** No damage: the assert fires BEFORE the write.
- **Verified.** `tsc` clean · component gate **90.62 / 81.79 / 89.25 / 93.54** vs 90.3 / 81.6 / 89.1 / 93.2, **exit 0 on two consecutive runs** with zero unhandled errors · component tests **2,906** · full primary suite **14,006 / 1,300 files** green · brand-token guard 54 + 69 clean · ratchets **1 / 4 / 39 / 8** · both deploys READY with `lambdaRuntimeStats` and all five production aliases · ledger 1,612 headings, swallowed steady at **3**, zero line-start markers. **Zone read before stamping** (`date '+%Z'` → PDT; 21:48 PT / 04:48Z on the 17th, so `date -u` would file this a day late).

Also untouched: ~46k LOC of `page.tsx` measured by neither gate, and the 39 collapse-ratchet sites.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "work on these sentinel alerts", an 8-alert digest) — **two of the eight were reporting DESIGNED behaviour as breakage and are now bounded-suppressed; the one genuinely new arm had an obvious mechanism that I published mid-session and then refuted with my own data.** Paging tier **8 → 6**, verified by re-running `get_pipeline_alerts()`. 3 prod DML changes (2 suppressions + 1 cadence row), 1 inbox filing. No code, migration-DDL, cron-schedule, auth, hot-wallet or pricing change.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE IS A SELF-REFUTATION. `pgcron-startup-timeout` looks exactly like worker-slot exhaustion** — `cron.max_running_jobs=32` vs `max_worker_processes=6`, failures in contiguous bands rather than at high-start-count minutes (03:52Z: 6 starts / 0 fail; 03:39Z: 3 starts / 3 fail), and **exactly six** multi-minute jobs overlapping during the band. **Wrong.** The concurrency histogram has **occupied states above the supposed cap — 7, 8 and 9 concurrent, all with ZERO timeouts** — and **`cron.use_background_workers = off`**, so pg_cron uses **libpq connections**, not background workers. It is a **connection handshake failing under load**, i.e. the documented disk-IO saturation wearing a new hat. **Anyone acting on my first version would have changed a Postgres setting that does nothing.** Canonicalised under "Cron / scheduler surfaces" + [inbox 2026-08-17T0410Z](docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-17T0410Z-the-pgcron-startup-timeout-is-not-a-worker-slot-cap-it-is-the-saturation.md).

Both suppressions expire **2026-11-15** so neither can hide a changed failure mode.

Zone read before stamping (`date '+%Z'` → **PDT**, 21:15 PT / 04:15Z on the 17th).

Counter-evidence: `wallet_usernames_unresolved` declares 60 s, is called under `service_role`'s **30 s**, and its `max_exec_time` is **58,206 ms**, with retry / `maxDuration` / every role ceiling ruled out. **If it binds through PostgREST the rule is right for cron and wrong for routes, which is most of the product.** Decisive experiment filed; **stop citing the rule as "measured" for the route path.**

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, docs, THIRD pass of the day — "analyze repo → update CLAUDE.md to current state") — docs-only pass on tip `fc4c1a96`. **Only THREE commits had landed since this file was last written and two were inbox filings, so the yield should have been nothing. Instead the headline is a trap this file has recorded THREE separate times as an anecdote and never once measured: 195 functions carry an inert `statement_timeout`, 47 of them declaring more than any caller can supply.** No product/DB/prod change; direct to `main`. Ledger entry 2026-08-16.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: the function-level `SET statement_timeout` trap has a POPULATION, and nobody had counted it.** This file says "second instance of that trap", "the function-proconfig trap again", and records two live probes proving a `proconfig` declaration does not bind statements inside the function — all as incidents. Measured live: **195 `public` functions declare one; 47 declare MORE than the global 120 s; one (`fmv_thin_sale_ask_disclosure_refresh`, 900 s) declares more than the highest ROLE ceiling exists to give.** The binding budget comes from the **caller's role** (`anon` 3 s · `authenticated` 8 s · `service_role` 30 s · `cron_heavy` 600 s) or the global 120 s — so a function declaring 300 s gets 30 s, 600 s or 120 s depending on who calls it, **and never 300**. ⚠ **The 47 are the actively misleading ones**: each reads as a granted budget no caller can supply, which is why three independent investigations (`8918307c`'s drain seeders, the eight `rpc_thp_leg_*` legs, and now `match_topshot_players_run`) each reached for "raise the declared timeout" and each would have changed nothing. ⚠ **Deliberately NOT mass-stripped** — a `CREATE OR REPLACE` across 195 objects is a large blast radius for a cosmetic win, and the declaration is harmless below the binding budget. **I did not re-probe the inertness**; the file's two existing measurements are the grounding and I counted.

No DB, migration, cron, auth, hot-wallet or pricing change all session.

True at ship, false since 07-25. **A stale "inert" header on a live public write path is what licenses a future session to dismiss a stall as by-design.** Its producer pointer also **contradicted itself** (line 4 named the live runner, line 8 a superseded 4 KB draft carrying the psku field-order this repo corrected on 07-19).
- ⚠ **CI HAD BEEN RED ON `main` FOR 16 CONSECUTIVE RUNS AND IT WAS NOT A REGRESSION.** 14 failures across 3 guards, caused by the `*Client.tsx` conversion workstream: the guards grep `page.tsx`, the logic moved to the sibling Client file, so they read a thin shell. **Proven pre-existing by stashing my diff and re-running at clean `e38cc453`.** The properties all survived (`MarketplaceStatusBanner` 2 hits in the Client / **0** in the page).
- ⚠ **THE DANGER WAS THE REPAIR, NOT THE BREAKAGE** — a red guard whose subject merely moved invites deleting the assertion, which would silently buy coverage loss on live honesty properties. Fixed by reading the page **as a unit** (`__tests__/helpers/page-source.ts`: `page.tsx` + sibling `*Client.tsx`), applied in each file's `read` helper so all 14 sites are covered at once. `not.toContain` gets **stricter**, never weaker. **Swept the population (23 files), converting only the 6 that assert about a CLIENT page.**

Server-page guards likewise: server pages are not being split.

- ⚠ **A META-GUARD FOR THE CLASS WAS BUILT, RUN, AND REJECTED ON ITS OUTPUT.** "Fail any test hard-coding a `page.tsx` whose directory holds a `*Client.tsx`" produces **false positives** and would have made three correct guards wrong: `[collection]/pack/[id]/` holds `PackLifecycleClient.tsx` but is a **server page importing client SUBCOMPONENTS**. **"Directory has a `*Client.tsx`" does not imply "this page was split", and that is not decidable from the layout.** Recorded in the helper so nobody rebuilds it — with why living without it is fine (**presence** assertions fail loud; the silent **absence** population is swept).
- ⚠ **MY OWN ERROR, AND IT IS THE SAME CLASS THE RECIPE EXISTS TO PREVENT: I committed conflict markers into the ledger.** I followed the documented rebase recipe correctly (`git show :2`, re-splice into THEIRS at line-start `^### `), then verified with an **UNANCHORED** `"<<<<<<<" not in out` — which **fired on a correct resolution**, because upstream's own entry *quotes* the markers in prose while describing a previous marker incident. **Worse, `git add … && git rebase --continue` was a SEPARATE statement, not gated on the resolver's exit code**, so the un-written CONFLICTED file was staged and committed. **The guard converted a correct outcome into a broken commit.** Caught before push, repaired by `--amend`. Both rules now in the ledger-discipline paragraph: **anchor the marker check exactly as the splice is anchored, and gate the `git add` on the resolver succeeding.**

The superseded paragraph was **kept and labelled**, because its reasoning — *the breached SET can change while the COUNT holds, so diff the set* — is the better half. Also: the 8-way split is **confirmed in production**, `trust_precompute_max_age_hours` **5.87** against its own pre-cycle prediction of ~5.7 h.
