(2) I asserted *"the runner began sending a wallet-scoped session"* — **the runner has no Panini auth code at all** (its only `Authorization` is our ingest bearer; the browser signs natively), so the change was **upstream**. (3) **The real headline: `panini_sale_price_capture_dry_days` is CRYING WOLF** — it counts dry days on a field **deliberately abandoned and REPLACED** on 08-08, while the replacement works: capture went **5.0% (08-07) → 8.9% (ship day) → 22.7%** and holds **21–39%**, incl. **22.9% today on 17,809 rows**. **Panini sale capture is not dry; only the dead field is**, and the arm reads 19 **+1/day forever** — the `ufc_fmv_stale_hours` shape a **third** time.
- **The durable rule from that: a monitor's `catches` text is a SNAPSHOT of what was known when it was written, and a later commit can settle its open question without anyone editing the arm — so the arm keeps asking and every reader inherits a solved question as an open one. Grep the code history for the subsystem before quoting a monitor's open question as open.** One `git log -- <script>` would have saved the whole detour.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: `v_rpc_trust_health_freshness` is APPLIED AND LIVE, and this file said the opposite in two places — one of which cited its absence as PROOF.** It went in through `apply_migration` as `20260816173845_audit_20260816_trust_health_freshness_companion_view`, sits in `schema_migrations`, and answers: **19 rows, per-metric age 0.20 h → 5.85 h.** The prior pass had written *"`v_rpc_trust_health_freshness` still **absent from live**, confirming the committed-UNAPPLIED migration is genuinely unapplied"* — a re-measurement that was correct at 16:41Z and became a load-bearing false statement when someone applied it hours later. ⚠ **A "committed UNAPPLIED" note has a SHELF LIFE and nothing announces its expiry** — same family as the disclosed-limitation-that-goes-stale class already recorded under the alerts work, but worse, because turning the absence into confirmation makes the stale claim read as *verified*. The cost is two-sided: a reader does not learn the per-metric-age instrument exists, and a reader who trusts the note might re-apply it.
- ⚠ **AND THE 5.85 h IS NOT JUST A HEALTH READING — it independently CONFIRMS the 8-way trust-precompute split.** It lands on the split's own predicted ~5.7 h steady state, derived from per-metric ages rather than from the max-age arm the split's whole transient story was about. **Two instruments, arrived at separately, agreeing.** Use this view rather than hand-deriving per-leg freshness from `rpc_trust_health_precompute`.

Live carries the post-rebuild column list (`collection_slug` first, plus `rows_attributable` and `status`), which is exactly the shape whose column RENAME the file records as having errored `42P16` — **so the columns themselves are the evidence, no `schema_migrations` lookup required.** ⚠ Note the direction: this file already warns at length that **absence from `schema_migrations` is not evidence a migration never ran** (the `execute_sql` + `SET LOCAL ROLE cron_heavy` case). These two are the INVERSE — *the prose claimed unapplied and the object was live* — and nothing in the file guarded against that.

That is not a redefinition — **twelve commits between 07:58 and 16:49 PT walked the gate ratchet 32 → 30 → 29 → 28 → 25 → 24 → 21 → 19 → 17 → 15 → 13 → 11 → 10 → 9**, every step a real conversion or honesty fix, with fetch-honesty tracking it 31 → 12. **A ratchet is a countdown somebody is actively working**, so a figure written at 16:15Z was wrong by 21 within five hours, and a session planning off "~30 client pages remaining" would scope against a population that no longer exists. The canonical bullets now carry the rate, not just the value, alongside the existing "read it off `const BUDGET`" instruction. **The `*Client.tsx` conversion workstream is very nearly done.** ⚠ **AND THE 9 / 12 / 38 ABOVE WERE THEMSELVES UNDERCOUNTS — the BASIS changed on 2026-08-16, so do not diff against them.** Live is **6 / 8 / 39**, but that is not "one conversion": six more conversions landed AND a detector bug was fixed that had been hiding pages from three ratchets at once. The +1 view is the freshness view itself, which is why the two findings are one event.
- **Canonical DB-pin figures were stale on both halves — 169/168 over 160 files → 180 pins / 179 fns / 172 `.sql` files.** ⚠ Recorded with the reason files ≠ pins **in both directions**: several pins share one file (the nine MV-refresh wrappers), and the file count includes `_helpers.sql`, which is not a pin — so `ls supabase/tests/*.sql | wc -l` is not a pin census however naturally it reads as one.
- **Verified CURRENT — the useful negative result, and one live number that HELD:** **the trust board is unchanged from the 23:40Z reading an hour earlier — still 3 breached, same set, same values** (`panini_sale_price_capture_dry_days` 19 · `public_board_slow_count` 9 · `unmapped_resolution_backlog_max` 291), and the view answered promptly rather than timing out. Also holding: **9 CI jobs** (the corrected count, verified by enumerating the `jobs:` keys rather than grepping) · 38 Vercel crons · 20 GHA workflows (17 scheduled) · 17 worker dirs · **33 concierge tools** · 44 OG cards · **47 edge inline-copy pins** · 367 tables · **0 RLS-off** · `check_public_security_invariants()` and `check_anon_write_surface()` both **0 rows** · primary gate 91.3/78.6/93.1/93.4 · component 90.3/81.6/89.1/93.2 · worker 85.1/72.1/83.8/88.1 · server-page ratchet **8** · ledger **1,575 headings, swallowed steady at 3, zero conflict markers**.
- ⚠ **The `published: true` grep still reads 6 against a true 5** (a doc comment quotes the literal) — re-confirmed rather than assumed, since this file's own lesson is that a recorded over-count correction is a FIXED OFFSET that silently absorbs real growth. It is still correct today; the sibling CI-jobs offset was not, which is why both get re-derived rather than trusted.
- **No tail roll due** — Recent sessions still spans exactly Aug 16 · 15 · 14. **Zone read before stamping** (`date '+%Z'` → **PDT**; 16:57 PT / 23:57Z — this sandbox is PDT, so `date -u` is the SAME calendar day and the reflexive "subtract 7 h" would land a day early).
- **NOT verified this pass, stated rather than implied:** `node_modules` is absent in a fresh cloud sandbox, so **no suite was run** — the primary/component/worker test COUNTS quoted elsewhere in this file are carried forward from the sessions that measured them, not re-confirmed here. ### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "additional analytics around the NBA Top Shot buyback wallet" → "this should be a buyback wallet too" → "dig deeper on all of this" → "keep going" → "check the drift workflow's recent runs") — **I shipped an analytics board and then spent the rest of the session proving its headline number was fabricated. The board said 161,797 moments acquired; the true figure is about 6. Root cause was one missing `ORDER BY`.** ~6 code pushes + ~6 docs/ledger pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, every deploy verified READY per commit, `tsc` clean and both gates green throughout (12,726 → **12,917**). 4 prod migrations + 1 MV + 1 pg_cron job + 1 edge-fn deploy (v27 → v28).

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: the surface I shipped at 19:34Z was publishing an artifact by 20:00Z, and I only found it because "dig deeper" made me test my own claim.** `topshot_insider_buybacks`'s `direct_transfer` arm is produced by diffing daily `wallet_holdings_snapshot` rows, and the snapshot **offset-pages `wallet_moments_cache` with `.range()` and no `.order()`**. Four independent measurements: **41,301 of 41,307** distinct moments recorded as acquired were **already held on the first snapshot** (only **6** were ever new); **62–86%** of daily arrivals were present two days earlier, across nine day-triples; **0 of 200** sampled appear in `sales` **against a positive control of 208/208** for marketplace rows on the same join key; holdings sit flat at ~52,120 while the table claimed ~6,500/day.
- ⚠ **THE PROOF CAME FROM THE SCHEMA, AND SO DID THE REASON IT HID FOR THREE MONTHS.** `wallet_moments_cache` carries `UNIQUE(wallet_address, collection_id, moment_id)`, so duplicates are impossible at source — live, **52,120 rows / 52,120 distinct**. The snapshot held **52,123 entries / 45,059 distinct**: ~7,064 read twice, ~7,061 missed. **The two roughly CANCEL, so the count was within 3 of the truth and every count-based check passed.** The walk read the right NUMBER of rows and the wrong SET; only a DISTINCT count or a set comparison can see that.
- ⚠ **THE RULE WAS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN, VERBATIM, AND UNENFORCED.** `lib/supabase-paginate.ts` states it exactly; the one place that **hand-rolled its own paging loop** instead of calling `fetchAllPaged` is where it broke. **A comment is not a check** — met on this repo's own written rule. Now a ban at **zero** (13 → 11 → 0).
- ⚠ **THREE OF MY OWN INFERENCES WERE WRONG, EACH KILLED BY A MEASUREMENT, AND THEY ARE THE MOST TRANSFERABLE PART.** (1) I called flat holdings a "smoking gun" that 6,535 daily acquisitions were impossible — **conflating NET change with GROSS flow**; the true diff was 5,821 in / 5,432 out, entirely consistent with net +4. (2) I said the 13.6% duplicates mean `moment_count` overstates the real holding — **it does not**, it is within 3, and the cancellation is the whole story. (3) I hypothesised the drift workflow had been **silently green** on a dead PAT; it had been **loudly red and correct every day**. **Each wrong inference was plausible, and each was settled by one query.**
- ⚠ **THE DRIFT DETECTOR WORKED AND NOBODY READ IT.** `edge-fn-drift` failed every scheduled run since ≥08-09 — `exit 1` means drift found — listing `snapshot-institutional-wallets` daily. **A permanently-red scheduled workflow is indistinguishable from a broken one at a glance.** Canonicalised under the GHA bullet, with the two-PAT distinction (repo secret VALID, local shell token REVOKED).
- **What shipped for the product:** `/analytics/buyback` + `/api/analytics/buyback` (MV `topshot_buyback_daily` at (wallet, day, method, edition, seller) grain, 205k → 19.6k rows, reconciled exactly; `rpc_topshot_buyback_analytics` one-pass for all periods, **2.16 s / 394 disk reads** vs 5.77 s for a single seq-scan aggregate; pg_cron jobid 333). Then **re-scoped to verified marketplace purchases only** — 431 all-time, $10,081.93 — with `coverage.excluded_snapshot_rows` disclosed so a small board reads as "we discarded 161,366 rows we could not stand behind", not "Top Shot stopped buying".
- ⚠ **A LIVE PRE-EXISTING DEFECT FELL OUT OF IT:** `/api/analytics/insider/signals` had been rendering the artifact rows on `/analytics` as **"insider buyback detected"** — they outnumber real rows ~375:1 and carry today's date, so they occupied every slot. Filtered, mutation-pinned. The dormant `topshot-insider-detect-patterns` edge fn read the same table unfiltered and is now filtered too (**0 cron callers, 0 `pipeline_runs`, 0 alerts ever** — latent, not live; that it emitted nothing was luck, artifact rows carry a NULL `serial_number`).
- ⚠ **DEPLOY: I under-reached, and the caution's other half was load-bearing.** I declined the MCP edge-fn deploy as "an unproven layout" — the nested-path layout I had written into the inbox **as a hypothesis** was exactly what worked. But the drift measurement was NOT ceremony: v27 carried `import_map: false` while the repo imports by bare specifier, so **a naive redeploy would have boot-failed a live daily pipeline**. Trevor's CLI attempt 401'd on the revoked PAT — *after* a successful upload, so **the deploy error said nothing about the deploy**; separating that took testing the credential independently.
- **Verified:** primary **12,917** / 1,275 files green (91.75/79.20/93.49/93.82); component **1,896** green (90.58/82.19/89.52/93.49); brand-token clean; `check_public_security_invariants()`, `check_anon_write_surface()` and `check_secdef_anon_exec_drift()` all clean after the migrations. Edge fn **v28 ACTIVE, `import_map: true`, `verify_jwt: false`**, verified by boot probe (a `401` with a **plain-text** body is the function's own check; a gateway rejection would be JSON).
- ⚠ **STILL UNVERIFIED, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS: the boot probe proves the module LOADS, not that it READS correctly.** That is the next 06:00 UTC run, judged on **`entries = distinct_ids` (~52,120)** — **not** on `entries`, which was always right. ⚠ **That run will also record its largest-ever arrival burst (~7.5k) as it diffs a complete set against the last corrupt baseline — the fix working, not a regression.** Single digits the day after is the real confirmation. **Do not revert on the burst.**
- **Left open:** **25 other drifted edge functions** (check each one's `import_map` before redeploying — same boot-fail trap); `compute_institutional_wallet_diff`'s **departure arm**, unexamined and consumer-less; the **161,366 artifact rows retained deliberately as evidence** with no reader; `0xb6f2481eba4df97b`'s 43,675 rows that resolve to **no edition at all**; and the revoked local PAT, operator-only to rotate.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "set my RPC's wallet avatar to the RPC logo" → "that should be the default for all users" → a 4-item profile list → "try to bring tomwagmi's image live" → "do it all" → "keep going" ×2) — **a one-line cosmetic request that turned into the avatar SYSTEM, and the two most useful moments were both me being wrong: a question I asked Trevor rested on a premise I never checked, and the one task I could not complete was one I should not have forced.** 5 code pushes + ~7 ledger pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, every deploy verified READY **per commit** (0 ERROR deploys all session), `tsc` clean and both gates EXIT 0 on each. 4 prod data mutations, all one-row, all on `profile_bio`.

- ⚠ **THE DEFAULT IS RENDER-TIME, AND THE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATION WOULD HAVE BEEN SILENTLY INERT.** `ALTER COLUMN avatar_url SET DEFAULT '<logo>'` never fires, because `app/api/profile/bio/route.ts` POSTs `avatar_url: avatarUrl ?? null` and **an explicit NULL beats a column DEFAULT** — it would have sat in the schema looking correct while covering **no new signup**, with a permanent backfill obligation papering over it. Keeping NULL as "has not chosen" also preserves the only way to answer *who has actually personalised* — which a backfill destroys irreversibly. **19 of 20 profiles are on the default; I reverted my OWN row to NULL so Trevor tracks it like everyone else.**
- ⚠ **"4 WALLETS" WAS A COUNTING BUG SITTING BETWEEN TWO CORRECT NUMBERS.** `saved_wallets` is keyed per `(wallet_addr, collection_id)`, so one Dapper address is four rows; the page rendered `wallets.length`. **The FMV and moment totals beside it were right** — they SUM across those same rows — which is exactly why it survived. The fix had to be server-side: addresses are stripped from the public payload, so **no client can dedupe**. Now a `wallet_count` scalar, and the tile **omits the line** rather than falling back. Recorded as the fourth fabricated-number shape.
- ⚠ **A PRIVACY ASSERTION GUARDING THAT STRIP WAS VACUOUS** — it asserted no `wallet_address` property; the column is **`wallet_addr`**. It would have passed on a payload leaking every address, and it mattered the moment the query started SELECTing the address to count it.
- **Rewards/achievements/P&L off the profile.** The "+50 Status" note lived in `ShareProfileButtons`, which has **four consumers**, so it was leaking onto three surfaces beyond the one reported. Cost-basis was already owner-only, but ⚠ **the gating was never the risk — the SURFACE was**: this is the page a collector POSTS, and a card that renders for the owner is one screenshot from public.
- ⚠ **MY OWN OPEN QUESTION RESTED ON A FALSE PREMISE.** I asked Trevor whether to take `/rewards` down. **It has been a hard 404 the whole time** (`notFound()`, unconditional) — one `cat` would have told me. The real exposure was three surfaces still ADVERTISING it: `/pricing` ("earn Status + Credits"), `/dashboard` (credits as the verify incentive), and `/profile/edit` **linking to the 404** to equip a cosmetic **19 of 20 collectors cannot get**.
- ⚠ **THE GUARD I WROTE FOR THAT CAUGHT A SITE I HAD MISSED, ON ITS FIRST RUN** — I fixed the challenge INSTRUCTION and left the SUCCESS receipt ("✓ Wallet verified — +500 credits earned"), which is the line more people read. **Fixing the pitch and leaving the receipt is the natural half-sweep.** And ⚠ **mutating my own guard exposed that it hard-failed on launch day** — see the self-retiring-guard bullet under Testing.
- ⚠ **I COULD NOT RESOLVE TOMWAGMI'S NFT IMAGE AND STOPPED RATHER THAN FORCE IT.** OpenSea, Etherscan, every public Ethereum RPC **and** `i2c.seadn.io` are all `403 CONNECT`/`EGRESS_BLOCKED` from the sandbox. Two workarounds were available and both were **declined as the same bypass through different infrastructure**: `net.http_*` from the production DB (the classifier blocked it, correctly) and a purpose-deployed edge function. **Using our own production egress to reach a host the sandbox proxy denies is circumventing the control, not working within it — and an operator authorizing the TASK does not convert a platform network control into something waivable through an agent session.** That route was independently dead anyway: the `rpc-edge-fn-deploy` skill is explicit that a gate-key secret is dashboard-only and "can never be completed by an agent session", so it would have deployed and failed closed — the ~40 h silent-outage shape. **Trevor pasted the URL in seconds.** ✅ Set, then trimmed `?w=2000` → `?w=500` for OG 4 MB headroom, via a **guarded `replace()`** that no-ops if the stored value is not what the entry assumes.
- ✅ **THE DURABLE ANSWER TO THE WHOLE THREAD: STOP ASKING FOR A URL.** Asking a collector for an image URL asks them to do a job browsers make hard — the obvious thing to copy is the PAGE address, which is valid, serves HTML, and fails silently. Validation only warns; **the picker is the fix**, measured first (81.9%–100% image coverage across the five largest wallets, and `/api/profile/top-moments` already returns a COALESCE'd thumbnail chain). ⚠ **It writes the SAME `avatar_url` field** — no new column, no second save path — so a picked URL inherits the default, the warning, the load check and the OG https gate unchanged.
- ⚠ **Three vacuous assertions of my own, all caught by mutation, all the same family**: an absence assertion whose fixture would never have produced the thing (achievements), and two over-broad negatives that matched unrelated copy and failed against CORRECT code (`not.toContain("TR")` → "TROPHY"; `/WALLET/` → "SAVED WALLETS"). Also a host pattern that flagged `assets.nbatopshot.com` — **the warning firing on exactly the link it had just asked for** — and then a `.jpg` fixture that MASKED the fix, needing an extensionless CDN path to become load-bearing.
- ⚠ **Two pre-existing tests asserted REMOVED behaviour and were INVERTED, not deleted** (cost-basis owner-gating; the two reward-note cases). A passing test asserting a promise is the thing holding the promise in place.
- **Verified:** 0 ERROR deploys · no new Sentry issues (`firstSeen:-8h`) · primary **12,842 → EXIT 0** (91.75/79.20/93.48/93.82) · component **EXIT 0** (90.78/82.27/89.35/93.72) · collapse ratchet **39 → 38** · ledger 1,553 headings, swallowed steady at **3**, zero markers. **No tail roll due** — Recent sessions still spans Aug 16 · 15 · 14.
- ⚠ **THE "LEFT OPEN" ITEMS BELOW WERE SUPERSEDED LATER THE SAME DAY, AND HOW IS THE USEFUL PART.** (a) asked *whether tomwagmi's avatar renders* and guessed the risk was the 4 MB OG cap. **The real answer was that it could never render: `proxy.ts`'s enumerated `img-src` CSP does not list the NFT image hosts, so the browser refused it before a byte moved** — I had attributed a CSP failure to a size cap. (b) filed a general image proxy as an un-taken SSRF-surface design; **it was then built** (`lib/media/avatar-proxy.ts` + `app/api/public/avatar-media`, host-allowlisted, SVG excluded, redirects refused), which is what makes (a) work at all. **The lesson to carry: when an image "does not render", check the CSP before the URL** — every other explanation is more interesting and less likely.
- **STILL OPEN:** (c) **File upload** — not built; the picker removes most of the demand. (d) **Cosmetics are dormant**, not broken, while `/rewards` 404s. (e) **Hosts outside both the CSP list and the proxy allowlist remain undisplayable** — the status quo, now DISCLOSED to the collector (`unsupported-host`) instead of silently accepted. (f) The **OG card deliberately still hotlinks**: it renders server-side where no CSP applies, and routing it through the proxy would nest a 6 s timeout inside its own 4.5 s budget.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "search for TODO comments, implement one" → "keep going" ×3 → "Proceed") — **the TODO backlog is genuinely EMPTY, so the ask resolved in minutes and everything after came from following one incidental finding; the session ended having caught a LIVE 12.4-hour pricing outage nobody had flagged, and having refuted THREE filed plans by measuring them.** 4 code pushes + ~6 docs/ledger pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, every deploy verified READY per commit, `tsc` clean and the full suite green throughout (12,592 → **12,830**). No DB, migration, cron, auth, hot-wallet, or FMV pricing-MATH change.

- **The literal ask took one grep: ZERO actionable TODO markers** in `app/ lib/ components/ workers/ scripts/ supabase/ __tests__/ e2e/ cadence/`. Every hit is a `TODO_n RESOLVED` annotation, a `TODO_`-prefix **sentinel guard** (`candyDiscoveryReady()`), or prose citing a closed item. ⚠ **The one tracked item, `docs/code-todos.md` #2, had been IMPLEMENTED on 2026-08-05 and never closed** — its handoff still said the retirement SQL was "NOT run — gated on a cleanup decision" while prod had already dropped both tables, three functions and four cursors. **Two documents describing objects that no longer exist**; the next reader following either would have hit `42P01`, which is what my first query did.
- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE, found incidentally while verifying that closeout: `fmv-recalc` had written ZERO rows for 12.4 h** — last success 06:48Z, 17 consecutive runs with `cursor_before='0' cursor_after='0'`, **all dying at 45–139 s against a 300 s ceiling, so none were `maxDuration` kills.** Mechanism: **`IN_CHUNK` equals `DEFAULT_LIMIT` (both 500), so a full page is EXACTLY ONE chunk** — hence `1 chunk fetch errors` means *the only* chunk. One transient pool timeout emptied `salesPage`, the cursor never advanced, nothing downstream ran.
- ⚠ **THREE FILED PLANS DIED ON MEASUREMENT THIS SESSION, and the third is the sharpest: the remedy had ALREADY BEEN TRIED.** `fmv-recalc`'s filed fix ("cut page size per invocation") is refuted by `corr(rows_written, duration_ms) = **0.103**` over 80 runs at fixed page size; by a **101**-edition page taking **227 s** where the average **500**-edition page took **194 s**; and decisively by **the route's own comment showing the page size was already cut 900 → 500 on that exact reasoning** when the kill rate was **23.6%** — it is now **53.4%**. *Read the code's own history before re-applying a remedy to it.*
- **Two retry fixes, one class:** a cheap read with no retry whose failure discards an entire batch run. `ownership-onchain-walk` (failed both daily ticks 08-15/16, `wallets_walked 0`, never touching its own 720 s budget) and `fmv-recalc`'s two step-1 reads. ⚠ **The BACKOFF is load-bearing, not the retry** — `rpc-with-retry`'s default is ~250 ms, sized for a page render a human waits on, and lands entirely inside a saturation spell. ⚠ **`withQueryDeadline` deliberately does NOT retry and that is also right**: its reasoning ("a retry doubles the hold on the pool that is saturating") holds for a render and **inverts for a background sweep**, where not retrying spared the pool nothing because the route re-ran the whole page next tick.
- ⚠ **New primitive `queryWithRetry` takes a FACTORY, not a builder — the subtle part.** A PostgREST builder is a **single-use thenable**: awaiting the same object twice yields the first attempt's settled result, so a retry closing over one would retry *nothing* **while passing any test that counts awaits**. Extracted a shared `retryLoop` rather than copying (proven shared: mutating it reds BOTH suites).
- ⚠ **A ratchet-style rule worth keeping: the value of a retry scales INVERSELY with cadence.** ~50 pipelines logged pool-acquire failures in 72 h (~1,050 total), overwhelmingly `wallet-backfill-allday` (534 of 2,717 runs). **By COUNT `ownership-onchain-walk` is a rounding error at 2; by RATE it is the worst on the board at 2 of 3** — because it runs once daily and has no natural retry, where a `*/20` pipeline self-heals. That is why the other ~49 were deliberately NOT swept (in most, the RPC *is* the work).
- **Shipped the monitoring arm `Ownership Index Freshness`** (sentinel, warn 30 h / crit 72 h). ⚠ **Deliberately NOT a `pipeline_cadence_watchlist` row** — the cron fires perfectly and a failing run still writes a row, so every cadence instrument read healthy through the whole outage; `rows_written > 0` is the entire difference. ⚠ **An empty `pipeline_runs` window is CRITICAL, never ok** (retention ~73 h, so the query goes empty exactly when the outage outlives it); it dates the outage from the indefinite rollup and states the retention bound rather than inventing an age. ⚠ Reads `pipeline_runs`, not `max(observed_at)` — no index on that column, and adding one is wrong since the walk upserts it on every row; **proxy verified exact to the millisecond**.
- ✅ **VERIFIED END-TO-END IN PRODUCTION, via a path worth remembering: the hourly `pipeline-sentinel.yml` job echoes the response body, so its GHA logs verify any sentinel change for free** — no token, no proxy, and no `workflow_dispatch` (which would page a human). It returned `Ownership Index Freshness · warn · "Last ownership write: 53.4h ago (ownership-onchain-walk)"`.
- ✅ **Ran the trust-precompute split's own falsifier at 18:48–18:55Z — it PASSED** (leg 324 fired on its cron minute, arm **17.87 → 11.84 ok**, predicted peak 17.9 measured 17.87). Three corrections came with it: **leg 324 takes 367 s, not the recorded "~78 s"** (4.7×, changing the monolith's story from *nearly fit* to *never had a chance*); **six legs' real runtimes sum to 748 s against the 600 s the monolith had for eight** (a measured proof replacing an arithmetic one built on `statement_timeout` declarations this file proves are inert); and ⚠ **the falsifier nearly cried wolf — it conflates "did not fire" with "has not FINISHED", and leg 324 finished with 53 SECONDS to spare.**
- ⚠ **The trust board's breached SET changed twice while the COUNT held at 5** — `trust_precompute_max_age_hours` cleared, `fmv_sweep_stall_pct_24h` newly breached at exactly 50/50. **Diffing the count alone shows "no change" across a fix landing AND a new arm firing.** Also: `v_rpc_trust_health` would not answer at all under saturation, even filtered to breached rows — **the sentinel's `Trust Health` check is the dependable fallback**.
- ⚠ **My own errors, all caught by measurement rather than review.** I offered two hypotheses for a throughput collapse and **both were wrong** (`wallets_walked` is 799 on both days — a fixed batch, so `rows_found` is wallet-size variance, not a signal); I passed `list_deployments` a `since` **20 minutes in the future** and got the documented empty list that reads as "no deploy"; a case-wrong `grep` told me a CLAUDE.md edit had vanished; and ⚠ **I dropped a `git stash` before confirming my extraction from it had worked** (`git stash show -p <stash> -- <path>` is invalid syntax and returned an empty patch). Recovery was cheap only because the splice asserts before writing and the text was still in the transcript. **Verify, then drop.**
- **Ledger discipline held across 6 rebases** on an unusually busy day (13 commits landed mid-session): every conflict resolved mechanically from `git show :2` + re-splice into the FRESHLY-READ file, never by hand-editing markers and never by writing back a stashed whole-file copy. Heading count +1 exactly each time; **swallowed steady at 3** throughout.
- **Left open, each with its check:** ⚠ **RE-BREACHED 2026-08-17 04:50Z — the "CLOSED" below was true when written and did not hold for four hours; do NOT read it as the current state.** The 23:40Z verification was real (the cursor marched 6000 → 10500 and the cycle **wrapped cleanly at 00:08Z**), and the new cycle then advanced 0 → 2500 before **wedging at cursor 2500 from 01:08Z**, putting `fmv_sweep_wedge_hours` back to **3.65** vs breach 3. Measured off the **`fmv-recalc-heartbeat`** denominator (the correction that already retracted one wrong diagnosis here): since 01:28Z, **23 invocations, 2 terminal rows, 0 successes, 21 killed (91%)** — materially worse than the ~32% that retraction established and the ~53–65% recorded above. ⚠ **A 05:24Z reading recorded "STILL WEDGED AND STILL WORSENING — 4.33 h, 29 invocations, 0 successes, 27 killed (93.1%), it has not self-cleared this time". THE SWEEP RESUMED FOUR MINUTES LATER, at 05:28Z, and that snapshot is SUPERSEDED — but the way it was wrong is the durable part, because it is this pipeline's THIRD failed characterization in two days.**
⚠ **MEASURED OVER 24 h INSTEAD OF OVER ONE STALL, THE REGIME IS THE OPPOSITE OF "WEDGED" (2026-08-17 11:37Z).** Gaps between successive cursor ADVANCES: **median 11.6 min, p90 25.3 min, max 260 min — and exactly ONE gap over 3 h in the whole day.** In the same window: **176 invocations, 59 terminal rows, 35 ok, 35 advances, 19,176 rows written**. So the normal operating mode is a healthy advance every ~12 minutes; a "wedge" is a **rare, isolated multi-hour stall**, not a state the pipeline sits in. ⚠ **Throughput is FLAT, not degrading** — 19,176 rows/24 h against 18,720 on 08-15 and ~19,216 on 08-16 — so the chronic ~66% kill rate is background cost, not the stall mechanism, and the two must not be conflated.
⚠ **THE ARM IS THEREFORE CORRECT AND WILL FLICKER, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME AS CRYING WOLF.** `breach_at = 3 h` sits ~7× above the p90 gap, so it fires only on genuine stalls — but the stalls last 4 h and **self-clear unaided** (three recorded now: 12.17 → 0.09, the 4.3 h stall on 08-17, and one at 3.87 h and counting when this was written). **Any single reading of this arm is a coin-flip on whether you caught a stall or the recovery**; the standing instruction to re-read it before opening an investigation is right, and *"it has not self-cleared this time"* is precisely the sentence a snapshot cannot support. **Judge it by the gap DISTRIBUTION and by `rows_written` over 24 h, never by hours-since-advance at one instant.** ⚠ **Platform saturation does not account for it**: in the same minute, 36 connections / 2 IO waits and **10 failures / 196 runs (5.1%)** platform-wide over 30 minutes. ⚠ **State only what is measured — this pipeline has now had TWO failed diagnoses in one day** (the route's own `(saturation-class)` self-label read as a measurement, then a "deterministic page-0 poison" claim retracted as a selection artifact), so do not assert a mechanism from the cursor position alone. ⚠ **It is a COVERAGE outage, not an FMV outage** — the previous cycle completed, so nothing is unpriced; prices are ageing. It also self-cleared once before (12.17 → 0.09 unaided), so **re-read the arm before opening an investigation**. The original entry follows. ✅ **the `fmv-recalc` fix is now VERIFIED in production (2026-08-16 ~23:40Z) — this item is CLOSED.** It was judged by its own stated pass condition rather than by the commit: `cursor_after` is advancing off `'0'` (ten consecutive runs marching 6000 → 6500 → … → 10500) with `rows_written` 489–500 each and `ok: true` throughout. Corroborated by the trust board, where `fmv_sweep_wedge_hours` went **12.17 → 0.09** and `fmv_sweep_stall_pct_24h` **50 → 32.9**. ⚠ One cosmetic artifact worth not misreading: two invocations can process the SAME cursor page (9500→10000 ran twice at 23:28 and 23:29, because the cursor advances at the END and runs take 150–343 s against a shorter cron interval). That is idempotent — FMV writes are delete-then-insert — so it is mild duplicated work, **not** a cursor defect. ⚠ **The `ownership-onchain-walk` retry is validated by the 2026-08-17 13:30Z tick, NOT the 08-16 one — and the 08-16 row is a FAILURE sitting in `pipeline_runs` that will read as "the fix did not work" (verified 2026-08-16 23:xxZ).** That tick ran at **13:30Z** and the fix (`d140cf9d`) deployed at **17:55Z**, so it predates the fix by **4.4 h** and structurally cannot contain it. **Do not revert on it.** The fix was re-read and is sound for this exact failure: the route throws its OWN labelled error from a **returned** `wErr` (`route.ts:157`), so `rpcWithRetry` sees a returned error rather than a rejection it would re-throw — the `threw:` prefix in `pipeline_runs` is the route's outer catch, not a raw throw escaping the helper — and the transient matcher explicitly covers `"connection pool"` (`rpc-with-retry.ts:281`, with its own note that `"timed out"` as two words is NOT caught by `"timeout"`). **Judge it by `wallets_walked > 0`, never by `ok` alone.** ⚠ Meanwhile `topshot_ownership` is genuinely stale — **58.1 h at 23:00Z 08-16, freshest `observed_at` 2026-08-14 13:30Z over 267,742 rows** — so the new `Ownership Index Freshness` arm (warn 30 h / crit 72 h) is correctly in **warn** and **crosses into CRITICAL at ~72 h, which lands almost exactly on the 08-17 tick**: it clears if the tick succeeds and pages if it does not, which is the arm working, not a new incident. The **20:07Z trust re-breach is still expected and is NOT a regression** (leg 326's pre-split 07:07:21Z write ageing past 13 h; green for good at 20:48Z). And on 08-14 the walk logged `vanished` (1,960) **exceeding** `confirmed` (1,748) — the one number I could not explain from batch composition.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "how is progress for pack ownership / opens / purchases / sales on Top Shot + All Day?" → "keep going" ×6 → "decide on what's best for RPC and our future users") — **the ask was a status question. Answering it honestly meant correcting my own reporting twice, and the two biggest finds were not about packs at all: an unauthenticated 46-second query nobody calls, and a monitor counting its own test suite's output.** 3 prod migrations applied · 2 deploys READY · 3 new guards · 2 inbox filings · every security invariant still 0 rows.

- ⚠ **THE PACK ANSWER, AND THE PART MOST LIKELY TO BE RE-OPENED AS AN INCIDENT.** Sales is the best-covered stream (585k TS back to 2023-09, 552k AllDay back to 2022-12, 100% dist_id + price); opens are deep but thinly valued (TS 92.3% dist / **30.9% pull-value**, AllDay 100% / **2.2%**); ownership is weakest and structurally so. ⚠ **The TS primary `dist_id` curve — 98% (Apr) → 59% (Jun 8) → 10.3% (Aug 3) — is a MATURATION CURVE, not a collapsing pipeline**: `dist_id` resolves on pack OPEN, and of 4,193 unresolved packs in the Jun-8 cohort only **82 (2.0%)** have ever been opened. **The durable consequence: a pack never opened never gets a `dist_id`, so for a sealed TS pack we know WHO holds it but not WHAT it is** — and sampled two ways (n=2,146), only ~**8.5%** of TS packs we have ever seen are still sealed, i.e. we know a holder for roughly **3–4% of the 651,183 sealed** ones.
- ⚠ **AllDay realized pull value is capped at ~14% by pull-level INGEST, not by the rollup** — `allday_pack_pull` covers ~393k of 2.82M rips, and a proper 5% hash sample shows **78% of packs blocked on unresolved `edition_id`**. Filling the FMV gap is a **0.4 pp** move; the real fix is the resolver `get_allday_unresolved_pulls`, which exists with **zero callers**, behind the WAF-403 AllDay consumer GQL.
- ⚠ **TWO OF MY OWN MEASUREMENTS WERE WRONG AND BOTH CORRECTIONS ARE THE TRANSFERABLE PART.** (a) AllDay `total_opened`/`total_sealed` being 100% NULL is **deliberate** ("degenerate for AllDay" — upstream `availableSupply == totalSupply`), not a gap. (b) **An unordered `LIMIT` is not a sample, it is physical order**: it reported `edition_id` on 0.1% of pull rows against a true **22%**. Every population figure after that used `abs(hashtext(k)) % N`.
- **Shipped for packs: `v_pack_pipeline_health` rebuilt + applied.** It was Top-Shot-only (four live AllDay cursors invisible), **cried wolf** on two by-design-complete cursors as an ever-growing `seconds_since_update` (2,098 h), and **blew the 60 s budget** on two bare `count(*)`. ⚠ **Its load-bearing property is a REFUSAL**: `pack-events-ingest-backfill` drives FOUR cursors and writes ONE `pipeline_runs` row, so its `rows_found` is unattributable — dividing it would have marked both *finished* TS cursors `wedged`, an incident manufactured from the monitor's own blind spot. Hence `rows_attributable=false` → neutral `static`. A mutation forced `map_broken` into existence (a fresh cursor with zero runs = this view's own cursor→pipeline map has drifted).
- ⚠ **THE SECURITY FIND: `compute_pack_ev_from_pool_tier_weighted` costs 45.8 s / ~17.4 GB per call, is anon-executable, and nothing calls it.** Chasing the class found **8 orphaned anon-executable functions**, incl. `get_wallet_cache_count` at **39.4 s**. All revoked; canonical detail now lives in Security posture, including why a **DB-side caller sweep alone would have been catastrophic** (32 of 37 report zero DB callers and most are live product RPCs) and why `bool_and` over an overloaded function's privileges lies.
- ⚠ **THE MONITOR FIND: the day-old concierge outcome check was counting ITS OWN SIBLING'S FIXTURE.** The `support-chat graceful-degradation` check deliberately writes a `concierge_unavailable` row every smoke tick; the outcome check read those rows back with no `is_smoke_test` filter and reported a total outage. Measured: of **905** conversations since 08-02, **902 were smoke tests, 3 real, and ALL 863 degraded rows were smoke rows — real degraded ZERO**, with the only 3 real conversations SUCCEEDING. ⚠ **So CLAUDE.md's "~780 degraded conversations" was never user impact** (corrected in place). **This is the guard-scope class in its sharpest form: a monitor whose input set includes another monitor's output — look for it wherever a check reads a table the test suite also writes.**
- ✅ **Then closed the gap that left**, on the "what's best for future users" steer: with real traffic ~0–3/day the honest check returns *no verdict*, so a dead concierge still paged nobody. Added the **only HARD live check** — does it ANSWER, or hand back a fallback — decisive and not model-dependent, so it can page where the three CONTENT probes are correctly soft. ⚠ **The hard/soft split is the design**: degraded category → hard; transport failure → `soft: true` (⚠ `couldNotRun` alone is NOT enough — it only changes the Sentry title while `smoke-gate.py` keys on `soft`). ⚠ The daily probe was also **incidental** — the 09:00–09:24 UTC window fires only if some caller ticks inside it, and the one guaranteed caller runs at 12:11 — so `?concierge=1` is now pinned to the scheduled run and **gated OFF for push** (~720 calls/day otherwise). Verified **behaviourally**: a test reproduces the outage and proves it hard-fails.
- **Guards added:** the smoke-row exclusion, the live-answer probe, and `migration-new-function-states-its-anon-exec-decision` (freezes the anon-exec population). All three **strip comments first** — each one's own prose names the identifiers it greps for.
- **Decisions made and NOT taken, on product merit:** the AllDay backfill latch (worker; pure waste, zero user impact, and a guessed seed constant would create a real data gap), `opened_count`'s missing writer (1,218 opens ≈ 0.04% drift), and the remaining **78** anon-exec invoker functions (most legitimately reachable; blind revoke would break live surfaces).
- ⚠ **`rpc-topshot-pack-opens-history` (jobid 56) returning `done: true` ~96×/day is a DELIBERATE STANDBY — do not unschedule it.** It looks like a dead cron on every instrument; the edge fn source says it is inert-safe and resumes if the decommissioned sporks return. I nearly retired it before reading the source.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "concierge should be able to pull the cheapest listing" → "keep going" ×8 → "do the scanner change so price-only alerts actually ignore FMV") — **the ask was a missing capability; the thread turned into four consecutive instances of ONE class — a surface asserting something it could not observe — and the last three were created or exposed by the fixes for the earlier ones.** ~10 code pushes + ~6 ledger/docs pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, every deploy verified READY per commit, `tsc` clean and the full suite green throughout (12,556 → **12,593**). 1 prod DB change (`audit_20260816_price_only_alerts`), verified live and byte-identical to the committed file.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: a price-only alert was structurally incapable of firing at ANY price, and a SECOND independent defect would have made the fix change nothing.** Full measurements are in the canonical bullet under "A failed read must not render as an answer". The durable process lesson is that **the zero result after the fix was an HONEST zero** ($4.00 floor vs a $0.60 cap) and therefore indistinguishable from "still broken" — proved end-to-end instead with a throwaway `channels = '{}'` subscription, which the dispatcher's `FOREACH` cannot enqueue on, so the query runs and nothing is delivered.
- ⚠ **A MEASUREMENT KILLED THE OBVIOUS DESIGN.** The price view's first draft carried FMV context via a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` on `fmv_snapshots`: **28,117 ms vs 320 ms without**, on the cheapest possible slice, with `computed_at <= now()` pruning already applied. 99% of the cost for a column that is by definition not a condition. **The view carries no FMV, and the message omits the clause rather than inventing one.**
- ⚠ **TWO TESTS PINNED CLAIMS THE PRODUCT COULD NOT SUPPORT, hours apart, and both were GOOD tests** — well-named, well-commented, correct when written. The first held a *limitation* in place after it was removed (`/at or below FMV/i`); the second held a *reassurance* (`/not an error/i`) about a feed measured writing zero rows for a day. **When you remove a limitation — or discover you never had grounds for a reassurance — grep for every place you documented it: code copy, tests, and prose, in the same pass.**
- ⚠ **FINISHING THE THREAD IS WHAT FOUND THE LAST DEFECT.** Having made an alert able to fire, I checked the last leg: `alert_deliveries` **834 all-time, newest 52 days ago** while the dispatch crons ran. That silence was honest — but tracing *why* the serial pool held 3 rows exposed `search_serial_deals` asserting its own feed's health, and behind that an ingest ~60% `egress_blocked` whose fix (`workers/atlas-proxy`) is still inert.
- **Also shipped:** `/api/edition-floor` (TS GQL through the proxy; the unattributable Flowty leg removed; 503 instead of a fabricated floor), the new **`get_edition_listings`** tool — 32 → **33 concierge tools** — for "is this one listed right now", `setName` on `search_live_deals`, the canonical-edition dedupe on three surfaces, bot `maxDuration` 60 → **90** (it must outlive the concierge's own 60 s cap, or the reply is composed and never sent), and a smoke check that measures the **outcome** (share of real conversations that got a fallback) rather than probing a dependency that costs credits per run.
- ⚠ **DIAGNOSES OF MINE THAT WERE WRONG AND WERE CORRECTED BY MEASUREMENT, not review:** a "Pinnacle edition" answer I blamed on the catalog was my own tool missing the canonical predicate (Trevor's pushback); "your credit balance is exhausted" narrowed to "Anthropic returned that error to this key" after Trevor said he had $18.71; and a Discord "probably unregistered" hypothesis disproved by the GET diagnostic he ran (all four commands registered and DM-capable — plain DMs are structurally unreachable, `/ask` only).
- ⚠ **Process: the commit ORDER decides whether code ships.** One push put the CLAUDE.md commit at the tip; `vercel.json`'s `ignoreCommand` treats a `*.md` tip as docs-only and **skips the build**, so the concierge fix would have sat unshipped while looking pushed. Reordered so the code commit is last. Five ledger rebases, every conflict resolved mechanically (`mine + theirs`, never hand-editing markers); heading count **1512 → 1514**, swallowed steady at **3**.
- **Left for an operator, both filed:** the Anthropic `403 credit_balance` on the Vercel key (likely a workspace spend limit, not the balance), and the `atlas-proxy` `wrangler deploy` + Cloudflare-egress probe.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "analyze test coverage → propose" → "All of these. Don't stop until you've exhausted everything you can possibly do") — **the three coverage gates were all green with the intended margins, so every find came from code NO gate measures; the headline is that a closed-set claim this file made THE SAME DAY was already false, reopened by a scheduling change with no code in it.** 8 code pushes + 8 docs pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, `tsc` clean and every gate green on each. **DB pins 169 → 180 over 179 fns; `.sql` test files 160 → 170; edge inline-copy pins 18 → 47.**

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: "the scheduled SECDEF write surface is CLOSED: 52 of 52" was recorded here on 2026-08-16 and re-derived the same day as 52 of 63.** Eight of the eleven newcomers are the `rpc_thp_leg_*` legs, and **nothing about them changed** — the 8-way split retired jobid 287 and created per-leg jobs 324–331, so each leg's NAME now appears directly in `cron.job.command` where only the orchestrator's did before. **A closed-set claim over "scheduled X" can be reopened by a pure SCHEDULING change: the pin count never fell, the population grew.** The other three are **non-SECDEF**, invisible to a SECDEF-scoped sweep — including `pinnacle_fmv_recalc_render_all`, a PRICING writer. All 11 now pinned (63/63) with ~45 mutations, all killed.
- ⚠ **One of them had DRIFTED, and the drift checker is structurally blind to that case.** `reconcile_all_saved_wallet_stats`'s only committed migration declares a zero-arg FUNCTION; live is a three-arg PROCEDURE with a soft deadline and per-wallet COMMITs. `npm run db:pins:check` reads only functions already in the PINS array, **so an UNPINNED function can drift indefinitely with every check green.** Snapshot migration `20260816181600` closes it.
- ⚠ **And the drift guard could not parse `CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE` at all**, making every procedure in this database unpinnable. It failed SAFE (a null extraction reds the pin) — but "safe" meant the pin could never be WRITTEN, which is indistinguishable from nobody having got round to it. Widened, with guards-the-guard cases incl. a not-vacuous check that at least one pin really is a procedure.
- ✅ **CORRECTS THIS FILE: the `pinnacle_fmv_history` ASK_ONLY drop is FIXED.** Two places described it as open; measured live the count is **ZERO**, not 776 (`20260815172945` is applied). ⚠ **A residual 20 renders differ for a DIFFERENT reason and are now pinned as current behaviour**: the trigger's `WHEN new.fmv_usd IS NOT NULL` means a **de-pricing writes no row**, so a render that loses its floor keeps its last price as the newest chart point forever while the page shows it unpriced (oldest 54 days).
- ⚠ **THE EDGE-FN CENSUS HAD FALLEN BEHIND BY 29 WHILE ITS OWN NOTE SAID IT WAS COMPLETE.** `edge-inline-copy-drift-guard`'s 2026-08-11 comment records walking "every `_shared` export against every edge `index.ts`" and finding 13. The same walk today found **29 more**. **A hand-run census records a MOMENT and nothing announces its expiry** — so the walk now runs as a test that fails on anything unpinned (mutation-proven both ways). ⚠ **Second-order lesson: my ad-hoc script found 20 of the 29; the guard's OWN `extractFn`/`norm` found all 29.** When a guard and a script disagree about a population, **the guard's own primitives are the instrument.**
- **The largest miss was `compute-topshot-pack-ev`** — 1,583 LOC, the biggest file in `supabase/functions/**`, writer of the public **+EV badge** — absent from the pin list entirely. **10,598 LOC across 31 edge functions have ZERO `_shared` extraction and are measured by no gate**, while `_shared` itself is 2,486 LOC and 100% tested: the convention works, it is applied to 7 of 38 functions. Added a tested mirror for the +EV arithmetic (no +EV without a buyable price; break-even is not +EV; `value_ratio` withheld not fabricated; `depletion_pct` NULL when the print run is unknown; and the clamp whose real job is that **`pack_ev_latest` filters `BETWEEN -10000 AND 1000000`, so an unclamped outlier makes the pack DISAPPEAR rather than look wrong**). ⚠ **Deliberately NOT wired into the edge function** — that needs a redeploy of the pack-EV writer, a production change the ask did not call for; a source-drift guard holds the two in sync and states plainly that it pins the ARITHMETIC, not the control flow.
- **Covered the two CI-invoked scripts that had none.** `find-swallowed-ledger-headings.awk` is the sole containment for a class that has destroyed ledger data five times, and ⚠ **the obvious spelling of its rule is wrong** — "not preceded by a backtick" misses the very incident that motivated it, because that splice landed immediately AFTER a quoting backtick; the discriminator is a **closed** code span. The naive rule is now a mutation, and it is killed. The documented `| wc -l` misread is pinned as an executable case. `ingest-topshot-active-listings.mjs` needed an entrypoint guard to become importable — ⚠ **a wrong guard fails SILENTLY** (the workflow would exit 0 having done nothing), so it is verified by **SPAWNING** the script and the direct-run behaviour was diffed against the pre-change baseline: identical stderr, identical exit code.
- ⚠ **THE ONE LIVE USER-FACING DEFECT came from a CONVERSION, which is the argument for continuing that workstream on merit rather than for the ratchet number.** Splitting `disney-pinnacle/sniper` into `PinnacleSniperClient.tsx` exposed a stats bar publishing **"0 pins"** and **"FMV coverage: 0 editions"** on a failed first load — a claim about the Pinnacle market and a claim about our own pricing coverage — rendered **directly ABOVE** the FEED ERROR banner. ⚠ Its empty state was **already** correctly gated on `data`, the exact `/insights/pack-reality` trap: *a page is not made honest by fixing the component that failed*. ⚠ **The test written for that site immediately found a SECOND**, worse one: a legend publishing the same fabricated 0 **paired with an explanation for it**. No honesty guard covered the page — the existing one keeps a hand-picked list of sniper pages. Ratchets **30 → 29** and **33 → 32**.
- ⚠ **Writing those tests corrected two of my own assumptions, both fixture-shaped.** Only SEARCH is a client-side filter — variant, franchise, max price, chasers-only and sort are **server-side**, so the property is that each control *reaches the request* (a filter that silently fails to be sent does not error; the board keeps showing unfiltered rows while the control reads as applied). And the buy link is built from `disneypinnacle.com/pin/<flowId>`, **not** the deal's `buyUrl`, so asserting on `buyUrl` would have pinned a field the row never reads.
- **The conversion's gate cost, measured, because it decides whether the next one is affordable:** it added 33 functions and the fetch tests alone covered 19 (**57.6%**), taking the component gate to **88.96 — BELOW its 89.1 threshold**. Handler coverage took the file to 26/33 and the gate to **89.37**. **"Cover the handlers, not just the fetches" is the price, not advice.**
- **Verified CURRENT:** primary gate **91.74/79.16/93.46/93.81** vs 91.3/78.6/93.1/93.4 · component **90.77/82.34/89.37/93.72** vs 90.3/81.6/89.1/93.2 · worker **85.59/72.61/84.25/88.53** vs 85.1/72.1/83.8/88.1 · primary suite **12,745 passed / 1,259 files / 0 failures** · DB suite **170 files, 0 failures** on a local `postgres:16`. ⚠ Worst branch coverage on any real logic file in the measured set is 25–50% (six OG cards, one cron route) — **the measured layers are genuinely near-exhausted; every remaining gap is structural.**
- **Still open, stated rather than quietly dropped:** 29 client pages and 8 server pages on the ratchets; 10,598 LOC of inline edge-fn logic (the mirror covers the +EV arithmetic only); ~45k LOC of `page.tsx` still measured by neither gate; and the pack-EV mirror is not the deployed code path until someone redeploys that edge function.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, docs — "analyze repo → update CLAUDE.md to current state") — docs-only pass on tip `08b16613`. **This file had been refreshed 15 minutes earlier by a concurrent session and only ONE code commit had landed since — so the yield should have been nothing. Instead the headline is a canonical count that a PREVIOUS docs pass verified, recorded a gotcha for, and got wrong, because the gotcha it recorded was a fixed offset that had silently absorbed real growth.** No product/DB/prod change; direct to `main`. Ledger entry 2026-08-16.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: the canonical "8 CI jobs" was wrong, and the 08-15 pass that verified it was defeated by its own correction.** `worker-tests` — the third coverage gate, over `workers/**` — had landed in `513c514e`, announced in that very same day's session entry as "a third coverage gate for `workers/**` wired into CI", and was **absent from both the count and the enumerated list**. The mechanism is the transferable part: the file records that `grep -cE '^  [a-z-]+:$' ci.yml` "reads 9 against a true 8" because it catches `push:` under `on:`. That gotcha is a **FIXED OFFSET of one** — so when the true population moved to 9 and the grep read 10, the recorded correction turned a *correct* reading into a confidently "corrected" wrong one, and it did so **most reliably for the reader who followed the instruction**. **A documented over-count correction has a shelf life, and nothing about it announces its expiry.** Verify against the enumerated LIST, and verify the list against the file. (The sibling `published: true` grep reading 6 against 5 is the same shape and is still correct today — which is exactly why neither is self-evidently stale.)
- **Two more counts drifted the ordinary way — a number that increments when someone ships something else.** **Concierge 32 → 33 tools** (`get_edition_listings`, `0d10cbcb`, refined by `b0f6db43`: it lets a follow-up chain onto a deal already on screen and **stops dropping the serial** — on a serial-keyed market, answering about the edition when the user asked about *their* serial is a wrong answer wearing a right one's clothes). ⚠ **That count has now drifted three times in three days** (31-vs-32, then 32-vs-33), so it is a dated sample by construction. And `.sql` test files 159 → **160**, where the count also includes `_helpers.sql`, which is not a pin at all.
- ⚠ **THE LIVE-SIDE HEADLINE: the 8-way trust-precompute split has a ~6 h transient in which its own arm reads WORSE, and nothing about that looks like recovery.** Measured ~1 h post-apply: `trust_precompute_max_age_hours` **15.14 → 15.25**, i.e. *up*, on a fix shipped to bring it down. It is correct and expected — the eight jobs are spread one-per-hour on a 6 h cycle, so only legs whose cron minute has come around carry post-split data. **Exactly 2 of 8 had fired** (jobid 331 at 15:09Z / 132.1 s, 327 at 15:48Z / 164.9 s, both `succeeded`), and the precompute table showed the split cleanly: two fired legs at **0.45 h / 1.10 h**, panini 3.29 h, a **9.13–9.28 h** block awaiting its turn, and `topshot_impossible_parallel_serials` alone at **15.27 h**. From the schedules the whole curve is predictable — **the arm PEAKS at ~17.9 h just before 18:48Z**, and the first full cycle completes at **20:48Z**. Recorded in the canonical bullet with the eight schedules, because without it the next session to look sees a red arm climbing after a "verified" fix and re-opens a closed investigation. **Judge the split by `cron.job_run_details` for jobids 324–331 and per-leg age, never by the max-age arm inside its first cycle.**
- ⚠ **A characterization I was about to repeat was wrong in the OTHER direction, and the honest answer is that the window is too short for either.** `public_board_slow_count` went **14 → 12**, after the previous pass corrected an earlier "oscillating down" to "CLIMBING, not oscillating" off 3 → 9 → 14. **Both readings were fair on their own ~1-day data**, which is the finding — the table now says not to characterize its direction from fewer than several days at all. Of the five breached arms, only `unmapped_resolution_backlog_max` (258) was genuinely flat; `panini_sale_price_capture_dry_days` 18 → **19** is a dry-day counter doing exactly what a dry-day counter does (the outage continuing, not new information); and `fmv_sweep_wedge_hours` 7.40 → **9.44** is **the one arm that has risen every single time it has been read** (4.30 → 4.68 → 7.40 → 9.44 across ~26 h).
- **Folded in the one code commit (`08b16613`), whose two lessons were documented nowhere canonical and are both about failures with NO SCREEN to appear on.** (a) **An alert is the furthest-reaching member of the failed-read/false-claim family, because its output is SILENCE and silence is unfalsifiable**: `manage_alerts` defaulted `min_discount` to 25 unconditionally, so a "$0.60 or less" request was saved **strictly narrower than the sentence that created it** while the confirmation described the sentence. ⚠ The honest-looking fix (write `null`) was **worse** and was caught by running the UPDATE against the live row first — the column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 25`, so it throws **23502** and creates no alert at all. ⚠ And `0` was not "no FMV condition": the scanner read a deals board, so an above-FMV listing under the cap still could not fire — **disclosed in `applied_filters` rather than quietly shrunk**. ⚠ **That disclosure was SUPERSEDED hours later by a concurrent session doing the scanner change on Trevor's direction, and its measurements are sharper than my framing** — a price-only alert could not fire at ANY price (111 board rows, cheapest $1.00, against a raw ask universe of 4,563 with a $0.33 floor: *the listings were there, the instrument could not see them*), **a SECOND independent defect would have made that fix change nothing** (`set_names` matched EXACTLY while the filter said `Archive` and the set is `Archive Set`), and adding FMV context back would have cost **28,117 ms vs 320 ms**. Folded into the canonical bullet; **my "disclose what you cannot remove" line stands as the right call at the time, not as the end state.** (b) **The concierge was down ~2 weeks with the smoke test reporting ALL PASSED** — 150 `concierge_unavailable` vs 3 `general` in 24 h, ~780 degraded conversations since 08-02 — because the only probes were soft AND opt-in, and they were both **because each spends real tokens**. **A check that costs money per run will be made optional, and an optional check is not a monitor.** The fix measures the OUTCOME (share of real conversations that got a fallback) for one indexed read and zero credits, keyed on **category not copy**, with a 5-conversation floor so a quiet night does not page.
- **Verified CURRENT, which is the useful negative result:** 38 Vercel crons · 20 GHA workflows (17 scheduled) · 17 worker dirs · **180 DB pins over 179 fns** (169/168 before the 2026-08-16 scheduled-writer re-close) / **170 `.sql` test files** · **47 edge inline-copy pins** (18 before the same pass, now with a COMPLETENESS check that re-runs the census every CI run) · 44 OG cards · 8 collections / 5 published · ratchets **8** / **29** / **32** / **39** · primary gate 91.3/78.6/93.1/93.4 · component 90.3/81.6/89.1/93.2 · worker 85.1/72.1/83.8/88.1 · **38 trust arms, 5 breached** · **pg_cron 92/92 active** (85 → 92 is the ONE split, not seven new pipelines: −1 monolith +8 legs) · `check_public_security_invariants()` and `check_anon_write_surface()` both **0 rows** · `v_rpc_trust_health_freshness` still **absent from live**, confirming the committed-UNAPPLIED migration is genuinely unapplied · ledger 1,504 headings, swallowed steady at **3**.

So reading `last_run_at` from the rollup **fabricates silences up to 6 h long**: `offers-sweep` showed 253.7 min of apparent silence against a live last run **7 minutes earlier**, with the rollup's own `refreshed_at` 244.8 min stale, and nearly became a filed 4-hour stall on a healthy pipeline. **"Check the rollup first" is right for VOLUME and TREND and actively wrong for RECENCY — the two questions must not share an instrument.** Corrected in place with the instruction never to read `last_run_at` without `refreshed_at` beside it.

Worse, a `:13` stagger block sits in an overnight handoff marked **"ready-to-run, reversible"**: its leg one moves a job that runs *alone* 22 h/day onto minute 40, which already holds a job maxing at 618 s, and its leg two stacks onto `rpc-ccm-step2`, freshly recovered from a 44 h stale spell caused by this exact class. ⚠ **"Reversible" describes the revert path and says nothing about whether the change is CORRECT** — the two conflate the moment a block is pasted rather than re-derived. **The deeper result is now canonical: overlap tracks a job's own DURATION, not its start minute** (73 s → 6 concurrent, 611 s → **29**), so **the schedule is oversubscribed in duration and minute-level staggering cannot fix this class at all** — converging with the split's own "rescheduling only chooses which legs starve". Also folded in the `cron_heavy` reschedule mechanism now proven in production (`SET LOCAL ROLE cron_heavy; SELECT cron.schedule('<existing name>', …)` keeps the jobid AND the 600 s budget; `cron.alter_job` is denied to that role).

One shared module; `fetchPinnedWallet` deliberately NOT merged with it.

The account-claim sweep across client pages is now **exhausted**.

Both **LATENT, not live** (no in-repo consumer renders them) and documented as such rather than dressed up as an incident.
- ⚠ **A MUTATION CORRECTED MY OWN COMMENT TWICE THIS SESSION, and both corrections are the durable part.** (a) The `Array.isArray` shape guard is redundant behind the `catch` for every shape our routes produce — measured, they all throw in the `for-of`; its unique territory is a **string** body, which iterates silently to empty. (b) Branching on `error` before `?? null` is redundant across all three shapes supabase-js emits — **the load-bearing change is `?? 0` → `?? null`.** In both cases I had already written a comment claiming the guard was the mechanism.
- **CONVERSION WORKSTREAM OPENED, and its cost is now MEASURED rather than guessed.** ⚠ The cheapest shape — a client page whose only client API is a routing hook — is **EXHAUSTED** after `[collection]/packs`. ⚠ Hoisting the Suspense boundary to the server page is what makes a split *testable*, not a detail. ⚠ **A conversion moves `% Funcs` DOWN**: 100/85.9/100/100 still took the gate to 89.42 against a 89.1 threshold, because a page is many small handlers — my first pass sat at 77.8 funcs. **Cover the handlers, not just the fetches.**
- ⚠ **A RATCHET IS THE ONE CONSTANT WHERE "take mine" AND "take theirs" ARE BOTH SILENTLY WRONG.** A concurrent session ran the same workstream; each of us lowered the same budget by our own conversions only (32 and 31). **Neither was right — 33 minus three conversions is 30.** It is a COUNT of a shared population, not an opinion; re-derive it from the failing no-slack assertion. Four rebases total, every ledger conflict resolved by keeping BOTH entries and verifying heading count against `origin` each time (swallowed steady at 3).
- ⚠ **MY DEPLOY ERRORED ON `/insights/deals`, A PAGE NONE OF MY FIVE FILES TOUCH — and chasing it found the ban's blind spot.** `insights-server-pages-bound-their-reads` passed the whole time, correctly: it checks the PAGE for an approved primitive, and `readBoardOrLive` is one. **The unbounded leg was INSIDE the primitive** — `readBoardSnapshot`, called TWICE around the 8s live bound. ⚠ **It try/catches to `null` so it cannot THROW, which is exactly what made it look safe**; my first hypothesis (it throws and propagates) was wrong, and the finding only appeared once I asked what could **HANG**. Bounded at 3s → worst case 14s. ⚠ **I did NOT prove this caused the failure** and said so in code, tests and ledger — the logs never identify which leg consumed the budget.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive, cont. — "keep going" ×4) — **continuation of the coverage thread. The DB layer was already closed at 52/52, so everything came from the OTHER structural blind spot — `app/**/page.tsx`, measured by neither gate — and the yield was SEVEN live user-facing defects in five pages, four of them false claims about the reader's own account. - ⚠ **A SUB-CLASS WORTH NAMING: FOUR OF THE SEVEN ARE FALSE CLAIMS ABOUT THE READER'S OWN ACCOUNT.** That is the worst version, because the reader is the one person who knows it is wrong, has no way to tell that we know it too, and — unlike a market claim — it is *actionable*, so it makes them do something. `/my-teams` told a collector who follows six teams **"Follow a team to build your hub"** and told them to add the wallet they already added; `/fast-break` and `/road-to-the-ring` both rendered **"connect a Top Shot wallet"** to someone who has pinned one. ⚠ **That last was COPY-PASTED VERBATIM** — the identical eight-line `saved_wallets` query in both pages, carrying the identical missing `error` check — so the fix is ONE module, not two edits. **Grep for the query before fixing the file.**
- ⚠ **ALL FOUR SAT BEHIND SIGN-IN, which is exactly why no sweep had reached them.** The anon driver-message guard derives its file set from `isPublicPath`, so everything past the auth wall is outside it **by construction** — the guard-scope class, met for the *n*th time and still the most reliable place to find new work.
- ⚠ **The strongest form of the class yet, recognisable by shape: the false claim came with a GUARANTEE attached.** `/fast-break` rendered *"No active Fast Break run — we'll surface the next run here as soon as Top Shot opens it"* during a live run. Its header **subtitle** said "No active run" too and renders ABOVE the gate ladder, so fixing the card alone would have left the claim on screen.
- ⚠ **`?? 0` on a supabase COUNT, third recorded instance, on a PUBLIC shareable URL.** `/pinnacle/moment/[id]` published "Tracked holders **0**" out of a statement timeout. ⚠ Its mutation survivor is instructive and was **documented rather than contrived away**: branching on `count == null` instead of on `error` is indistinguishable in every state the client produces (supabase reads `count` off `Content-Range`; an error response carries none), so it earned a SOURCE assertion plus the reason — *check the error, never just the value* is precisely what made `?? 0` look safe.
- ⚠ **A FALLBACK is a third state that can swallow a failure, and it hides better than a plain empty state.** `/[collection]/set/[slug]`'s tier bar sampled the first 100 editions whenever the full-set count came back empty — a legitimate fallback — and the fetcher returned a bare `[]` on error. A ~3,600-edition set rendered **"COMMON · 62 · 62.0%"** against a true ~2,200, in the same type and colour as the accurate bar. Keep the fallback, withhold the section on failure, and **discard partial counts** (a truncated mix still sums to 100% and reads as complete).
- ⚠ **A legacy REDIRECT route is the highest-stakes place to conflate them.** `/edition/[id]` did `if (error || !data) notFound()` — and that route exists only to catch links already in the wild, so a timeout handed a hard 404 for a real edition to the audience least likely to retry and most likely to be a crawler. On a redirect route the right failure is a THROW; there is nothing to render.
- ⚠ **THE RATCHET'S OWN NOT-VACUOUS CHECK WENT RED ON ITS SUCCESS.** It asserted `pages.length > 10` and the population reached **8**. That is exactly the failure its own next comment warns about for naming a specific page — *"the guard would punish its own success"* — expressed as a threshold rather than a name, which is why nobody caught it while writing the warning two lines below. **A not-vacuous check must be satisfiable at a population of ZERO**; it now asserts the WALK finds pages.
- ⚠ **A HARNESS BUG OF MINE DESTROYED TWO FILES OF UNCOMMITTED WORK.** My mutation harness keyed backups on `basename`; three of six targets were `page.tsx`, so all three wrote ONE backup and the restore overwrote two pages with a third's content. **Nothing noticed** — it surfaced two mutations later as `occurrence count 0`, which reads like a stale pattern, and `tsc` stayed clean because the overwriting file is itself valid. **Key on the full path.** Recovery was cheap only because the transforms were scripted and still in the transcript.
- ⚠ **My own guard tripped on my own comment, in the same commit** — the ordering assertion failed against correct code because the comment above the branch QUOTES the copy it guards. At least the fifth instance here; the guard now strips comments **offset-preservingly** so `indexOf` ordering still holds.
- ✅ **CLOSURE CLAIM, and it is a real one: the server-page honesty sweep is DONE.** All 8 pages left on the ratchet were verified page by page to already distinguish a failed read from an absence (two carry `ok`, two a `loadError`/`errored` flag consulted before the empty state, two degrade by OMISSION, two are pinned by the source guard). **What remains is structural debt with no known defect** — the next conversion buys coverage, not a fix. ⚠ Every find above was invisible until the code moved somewhere a test could drive it, and **each was already accompanied by a comment asserting the right behaviour. The comment is not the check.**
- **THE SECOND HALF ("keep going" ×3 more) moved to the CLIENT surface and found the worst instance of the whole session — one that is not a false claim at all.**
- ⚠ **`/profile/edit` handed you a BLANK EDITABLE FORM on a failed read, and its own Save button then wrote that blank over your profile.** `loading ? "Loading…" : <form>` with no failure branch, and `save()` POSTs every field unconditionally. **The user's own trust in what we showed them is the mechanism.** A second, independent loss vector on the same page: the teams POST REPLACES the full list, so a failed picks read plus any save **deleted every favourite team — exactly what `/my-teams` reads back**. ### August 15, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "analyze test coverage → propose" → "do all of them" ×6 → "proceed with all") — **the analysis said breadth was already closed, so everything came from structural blind spots; the largest turned out to be a MEASUREMENT of my own, and the session ended by closing the platform's least-covered high-stakes layer at 52/52.** ~14 code pushes + ~14 docs pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, `tsc` clean and the full suite green throughout. **DB pins 139 → 169 over 168 fns; `.sql` test files 137 → 159.** No prod DB change all session — every migration is a snapshot committed **UNAPPLIED** and byte-identical to live.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE IS A BLIND SPOT IN MY OWN SWEEP, not in the code.** The measurement that defined this whole workstream enumerated scheduled SECDEF writers by matching `insert into` / `update ` / `delete from` / `truncate ` — and reported "33 writers, 14 unpinned". Adding **`refresh materialized view`** found **52**. A function whose entire body is `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW` contains none of those verbs, so that whole CATEGORY was invisible **by construction** — including `refresh_sets_summary`, the one this file singles out for running as `rolbypassrls` under pg_cron. **The guard-scope class, met on a measurement rather than a test.** The surface is now **52/52**, and the canonical note says "closed AGAINST THIS PREDICATE" and tells the next reader to re-derive rather than quote it.
- ⚠ **The highest-stakes pins were the ones that decide what a collector is TOLD.** `refresh_atlas_pack_ev` + `backfill_topshot_historical_pack_ev` both write the public **+EV** badge: no +EV without a known ask, a **NULL** `value_ratio` rather than a fabricated one, a delisted or zero ask treated as NO ask (not a $0 pack, which looks infinitely +EV), and a **`gross_ev <= 3 × cheapest_ask`** survivor-bias cap that discards a 40× reading rather than publish it. ⚠ Their failure policies are OPPOSITE — the hourly sweep writes a sentinel, the backfill writes nothing — and **both are right for their own job**; recorded in both pins so nobody harmonises them.
- ⚠ **`attribute_topshot_rips_empirical` GUESSES which pack a rip came from, and feeds pack EV.** The property worth the pin is that both reference pools read `method = 'rip_dist'` ONLY, so **no inference ever becomes evidence for the next one**. ⚠ The two filters MASK EACH OTHER — both mutations passed — until two purpose-built fixtures separated them (a distribution that is 25% guesswork, and an inferred row inside an already-eligible distribution).
- ⚠ **A MUTATION REFUTED A CLAIM I HAD JUST WRITTEN.** I documented a cohort's `SUM(COALESCE(fmv_usd, 0))` as stopping one unpriced moment from NULLing a portfolio. **SQL's `SUM` already ignores NULLs.** The one case it governs is the opposite and worse: a wallet whose moments are ALL unpriced is published as a hard **$0.00** rather than NULL — the `?? 0` shape, living in a DB function. Pinned as current behaviour and explicitly not endorsed.
- ⚠ **AN ASSERTION FAILED AND THE CODE WAS RIGHT, which taught the sharper property.** `LIMIT` in `backfill_wmc_fmv_confidence` sits BEFORE the join to a priced snapshot: **it bounds rows EXAMINED, not written.** That is the whole mechanism behind its permanent backlog floor and behind its becoming the instance's #1 disk reader once the queue drained.
- ⚠ **SEVEN OF EIGHT `CONCURRENTLY` MUTATIONS PASSED** — removing the keyword still refreshes, it just takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock, and no rolled-back test can watch a lock. **`pg_get_functiondef` is queryable from inside the test**, so the keyword (and a baked-in `enable_nestloop=off` planner hint) are asserted from the catalog. The nine one-liner wrappers share ONE test file, which also **proves** that `CONCURRENTLY` requires a unique index by dropping it and showing the `55000`.
- ⚠ **A MUTATION RESULT IS MEANINGLESS UNTIL THE BASELINE IS GREEN.** A scripted fixture `replace` silently didn't match while its assertion did land, so a mutation "reddened" off a broken baseline. And a first-occurrence replace hit the file's own HEADER COMMENT rather than the code. **Assert the occurrence count before replacing.** Both traps are now canonical.
- **Earlier in the same session, before the pin campaign:** a directory-driven **ban** on server pages collapsing "failed read" into "absent" (no allowlist), a **third coverage gate** for `workers/**` wired into CI (thresholds climbed 68.2 → 84.4 st), a guard asserting all three gates are actually wired config→script→CI, and a daily CI flake fixed (`rollup_pipeline_runs.sql` failed for ~3 h after UTC midnight; `db-tests` is blocking).
- ⚠ **THE WORKER ITEM I HAD LISTED AS OPEN IS NOW CLOSED, AND THE REASON IT WAS OPEN IS THE FINDING.** `vitest.workers.config.ts` recorded two paths as unreachable "with a fixture alone" — the soft-budget bail-outs (said to need a fake clock) and the chunked-write partial-failure paths (said to need "a stub that fails the Nth chunk"). **Neither premise held.** The clock can be driven from the FETCH STUB, so no `vi.useFakeTimers` and no fight with the `AbortSignal.timeout` shim; and the sequence-aware fixture **already** fails the Nth call, because an array payload yields one entry per `await`. The only real gap was capturing the upsert **OPTIONS** — `{ onConflict, ignoreDuplicates }` is what makes a replay safe and nothing in the suite could see it. **Both were parked behind an assumed tooling gap that a few minutes of reading disproved; "recorded as unreachable" is the note nobody re-checks.**
  What the tests pin: `pack-events-ingest` runs **TWO deliberate chunk policies**. A failed **WRITE** throws and **blocks the cursor** (401 rows chunked 400 + 1: the 400 bank, `writeCursor` is never reached, the next tick replays) — the fix for the incident where one 1796-row upsert timed out deterministically and **wedged the cursor while the cron showed green**. A failed **LOOKUP** is collected and **the cursor advances anyway**, so the 100 rips that resolved stay linked. ⚠ **The cost of the second policy is now a pinned property**: the unresolved rip's moment is skipped and, the cursor having advanced, **never retried** — and the run reports **`ok: false` while advancing**, so *reporting and recovering are separate things here*. Worker gate re-seated 84.4/71.2/83.2/87.5 → **85.1/72.1/83.8/88.1** in the same pass that measured the drift.

- ⚠ **Second, server-side and independent: `referrals.count ?? 0`.** supabase-js returns errors rather than throwing, so a failed count publishes a hard **0 at HTTP 200** and `/rewards` renders "No referrals yet — be the first to share." to someone who may have referred friends and earned the credits. Disabling `loadFailed` reds the board test and reproduces the original DOM verbatim; restoring `?? 0` reds the rewards test (`expected +0 to be null`).
