Ledger entry 2026-08-15.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE, now canonical in Security posture: `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` with no `WITH` clause RESETS reloptions and silently strips `security_invoker=on`** — fourth occurrence, verified empirically on the live instance rather than inferred. **A view's security mode is invisible in its OUTPUT**, so the migration that caused it verified output md5, row count, buffers and `Subplans Removed` — all identical either way — while two anon-readable deals views dropped to definer. **Nothing failed CI at all**; the invariant caught it ~3 h later. Four rules travel with it: `ALTER VIEW` is the repair (it cannot drift the query definition), **invoker is the fix and not an allowlist entry** (allowlisting accepts the exact regression the arm was built to detect), a "benign" verdict must not be inherited **by analogy** (the Candy views have anon revoked and are not the same case), and a clean invariant **does not prove the view still works** — a broken invoker view regresses by returning nothing. Guarded now per VIEW NAME, because a prior migration hardens one of its two views and forgets the other.
- ⚠ **The end-to-end check that confirmed that repair was itself a CACHE HIT, and the tell was in the payload.** "Byte-identical 30,856 bytes" came back with an `elapsed_ms` identical **to the millisecond** — which no independent execution produces. On `/api/public/insights/**`, where success sets a public `s-maxage` of 300–3600, **a byte-identical result is at least as much the signature of a cache hit as of a correct change.** Now a rule in DB "General rules": re-run with a cache-busting param, and treat a repeated value in a field that *cannot* repeat exactly (a duration, a timestamp) as the giveaway.
- ⚠ **A filed finding's IMPACT was wrong, and correcting it moved the work rather than closing it.** Deep-audit R7 reasoned that the conflation guard must be ~15 days stale because its refresh sits inside a dark-killed tick. It has a **second independent caller** (pg_cron jobid 62, `23 */6 * * *`) and measures **0.0 days stale over 931 rows** — so a session acting on R7 would have fixed nothing. That mattered beyond tidiness: `topshot_deals_vs_fmv` excludes conflated editions, so a genuinely stale guard would mean the **public deals board publishing conflated editions as deals**. The real impact is the step R7 never mentions — **zero knot resolutions in 14.9 days, ~124 accrued undrained at +8.3/night**. Third instance this week of the file's own "a filed finding is a hypothesis" rule.
- ⚠ **The one repo-side drift was a number that moved in a session entry but not in the canonical bullet — the failure mode this pass exists to catch.** `vitest.config.ts` was re-seated to **91.3/78.6/93.1/93.4** by the extraction pass; the canonical CI-ratchet bullet still read 91.2/78.3/93.0/93.35 a day later. ### August 15, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "analyze test coverage → propose" → "do all of these" → "keep going on the page.tsx extraction") — **the analysis said breadth was already closed (499/499 routes, 261/265 lib files referenced), so every find came from the STRUCTURAL blind spots — and the two best were live user-facing defects that no gate could ever have caught, because both live in code neither gate measures.** 9 code pushes + 10 docs pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, every deploy checked PER COMMIT and READY. Primary gate 11,824 → **11,935**; server-page data-access ratchet 35 → **23**; DB test files 123 → **130**; pins 125 → **132**.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: `/api/og/insights/top-sales` published "3 sales this week" against 30,592.** Every `/api/public/insights/**` route published `meta.total_rows: data?.length ?? 0` — the **capped page length**, under a name meaning the opposite — and six OG cards read it as a board total, three of them off the same `limit=3` request they used to render their top-3 rows (`squeeze` "200" vs 1,352; `trophies` "500" vs 842). **It was the NAME, not the arithmetic**: nobody reading `total_rows` has reason to suspect a page length. An OG card is a static edge-cached PNG seen only in *someone else's* timeline, so these were simultaneously the most wrong and the least observable numbers on the platform. Canonical detail is now a row in the honesty table.
- ⚠ **A broader guard for that class was built, MEASURED, and REJECTED — it would have made four accurate cards less accurate.** `pack-drops` / `pack-sniper` / `set-completers` / `rookies` all publish counts that are genuinely un-capped or pre-slice aggregates. **The defect is not "publishes a count", it is "publishes a count derived from a capped page" — and that is not decidable from the card's own source.** The rejected rule and the four verifications are recorded in the test rather than left to be re-derived.
- ⚠ **`/alerts` told a collector "No alerts yet" and "not linked" off a FAILED read.** Three fetches under one `Promise.all`, each `if (res.ok)`, with a bare `catch { /* ignore */ }`. Every claim on that page is about the reader's OWN account — the empty state **invited a duplicate** of an alert they already had, and the channel list told someone whose Telegram IS linked that it was not. **Found by sweeping empty-state COPY, not fetch code**, which is now the documented way to find the next one: the guards cover 3 hand-picked sites against ~175 catch sites across 39 client pages.
- **Closed the TopShot remap/conflation family, 2-of-9 pinned → 9-of-9** — an arbitrary line, since all nine mutate the same keying every edition-keyed FMV derives from. 5 snapshot migrations (committed **UNAPPLIED**; byte-identical no-ops, and each apply costs a ~10–20 s burst of user-facing `PGRST002` 500s), 2 needed only a test because their committed migrations were **verified byte-identical to LIVE**. ~50 mutations, all killed.
- ⚠ **Mutation testing earned its keep by finding FIVE MASKED assertions**, all one shape: a guard rejecting a bad input is unobservable unless that input would OTHERWISE succeed. And `remap_pack_pool_uuid_key`'s `edition_id <> v_canon` needed a half-migrated row which, without the guard, **satisfies its own EXISTS subquery and deletes itself**. Three sibling clauses proved genuinely UNREACHABLE and are deliberately NOT asserted, each documented with what would make it load-bearing again.
- ⚠ **The whole DB suite failed off-UTC and CI could never have seen it.** Several tests assert a rendered `timestamptz`, which psql prints in the session zone, so on a PT machine one fails on the OFFSET while describing the same instant — reading exactly like a logic bug. CI containers are UTC; **Trevor's box is PT**. Fixed at the runner (`PGTZ=UTC`), which also matches prod.

No DB, migration, cron, auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or FMV/pricing-MATH change all session — the DB and FMV lanes were concurrent sessions' and I stayed out of them, rebasing 4 times.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: a filed fix was measured and REFUTED, and shipping it would have been worse than the defect.** The inbox recommended re-pointing the tautological Pinnacle FMV drift guard at `pinnacle_fmv_history` as "a genuine cross-source check". It is not a source at all — it is written by an `AFTER INSERT/UPDATE` **trigger on `pinnacle_catalog`**, so the comparison is the same tautology one hop removed, *except* where the trigger drops rows, which it does for **776 renders**. The "proper fix" would have replaced a guard that never fires with one that pages immediately, on an unrelated defect. **Second time in three days a recommendation died on measurement** (the `websearch_to_tsquery`/stemming pair was the other) — a plausible mechanism reads as a conclusion, and the only defence is to measure before shipping someone else's fix.
- ⚠ **That investigation found a REAL defect by chasing an arithmetic gap.** `NOW()` is transaction-stable, the Pinnacle recalc writes each render twice per transaction with the same `fmv_computed_at`, and the trigger's `ON CONFLICT (render_id, computed_at) DO NOTHING` silently discards the ASK_ONLY revision. Split is exact — HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW/STALE **0 differ**, ASK_ONLY **776 of 1,053**, max **$2,974.47** — so a Pinnacle edition page plots a series whose latest point is not the FMV shown on the same page. Filed (migration on FMV capture, not autonomous). The guard was **RETIRED** rather than re-pointed; what prevents the leak is structural (ask and FMV come off the same row) and is now pinned by a SOURCE guard, because a runtime probe can only observe the consequence — which is exactly how it degenerated.
- ⚠ **A mechanism's own derivation decides what it can observe — THREE new instances this session.** (1) `withDeadline` bounds an attempt two ways and they produced two different errors; **in production the abort wins**, so the shape that escaped had none of the `RPC_TIMEOUT` properties the module advertises — and **no test could catch it, because every mock implements `.rpc` with no `.abortSignal`**, so all of them take the guard branch by construction and the guard branch was already correct. (2) The metadata honesty layer was the only one of five with **no guard**, and both pack pages' `generateMetadata` discarded the `ok` their own page BODIES use. (3) My own new guard was **silently broken and still passed its own coverage assertion** — a whole-file `\([\s\S]*?\)` backtracks across function boundaries, so it discovered **1** fetcher instead of 54.
- ⚠ **An instrument's REASONING can be sound while its PREMISE is false.** `/api/cron/refresh-insights-cache` ruled `ok = okCount > 0` because "a single board timing out is EXPECTED". Measured over 869 ticks: `deals` failed **59.5%** of ticks (longest streak **34 ≈ 2h50m**), and `okCount > 0` was satisfied almost entirely by candy-mlb at 95.6% — **the healthiest board vouching for the other four**. A tick's outcome structurally cannot express a cumulative quantity. Fixed by reading snapshot AGE, with the 2 h ceiling derived from the streak distribution (only 4 of 414 streaks reached it) rather than chosen.
- **Two questions CLOSED with arithmetic rather than left open.** Serializing the board warms is impossible: the six views' **mean** costs sum to **59,960 ms** against a `maxDuration` of **60 s**. And those views are **STARVED, not slow** — every max is ~29.x s (the 30 s ceiling) while means are 10–12 s, and `panini_squeeze_board` fails 51% of ticks on a **3.4 s mean**; each failure burns a full 30 s of DB time for nothing, ~90 s per tick, so the refresher feeds the saturation it exists to survive.
- **Also shipped:** the `MomentDetailModal` bio wiring, closing the half-feature this file had flagged — deliberately a **lazy fetch on open** rather than the filed "change both monolith queries", since the table holds hundreds of rows and a collector opens one.
- ⚠ **Process lessons paid for.** A `git checkout <file>` used to unwind a mutation test **silently reverted an entire retirement** — caught only by re-grepping, not by trusting the command; use a backup copy on a file you are mid-edit on. And `list_deployments` with a `since` in the FUTURE returns zero results, which reads exactly like "no deploy was triggered".
- **Left OPEN and filed, all needing lanes I stayed out of:** the `pinnacle_fmv_history` ASK_ONLY drop (migration); the entity-page **pool-acquire** family — `NEXTJS-1Z` and four siblings die at our 45 s bound while Postgres self-bounds at 30 s and the heaviest real call maxes at 13.6 s, so **a query-plan fix cannot close them** and two attempts already have not; and the six insights views needing to come under 30 s, with three candidate concurrency fixes each recorded WITH the reason it is a gamble.

### August 14, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — continuation of the CX/concierge thread: "keep going" ×3 → "make sure everything is addressed") — **the day's two biggest wins came from measuring a fix BEFORE shipping it and from asking what was eating the IO budget instead of optimising the page that hurt.** 6 pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path; `tsc` clean; primary 11,750 + component 1,670 green. 3 prod DB changes (1 migration, 1 `cron.alter_job`, 1 index drop), each verified live.

- ✅ **NARRATIVE SEARCH IS FIXED, on the THIRD diagnosis — and the first two were mine.** The defect was never the ranking: `rpc_search_catalog` required EVERY query token (`LIKE ALL`), so one word the prose never uses annihilated the query. `lillard buzzer beater` returned **0 rows** while `lillard buzzer` returned the moment at rank 6. Fixed (`audit_20260814_rpc_search_catalog_token_coverage`): a **3+-token query may miss ONE token**, scored by a `(tok_hit / v_n) * 0.60` coverage term so a full match still outranks a partial; 1- and 2-token queries still require every word (relaxing there degrades `lillard buzzer` into every Lillard moment). All three failures fixed, all three working ranks and every entity query unmoved. Canonical detail is in the `/api/search` bullet.
- ⚠ **BOTH "proper" fixes I had recommended in the filings would have FAILED, and I only know because I measured them instead of shipping them.** `websearch_to_tsquery` does **not** give OR-semantics — it emits `'game' & 'winner'`, keeping the exact AND that was the defect — and English stemming does **not** relate **winner↔winning** or **buzzer↔beater**, so FTS would have missed the precise two moments that motivated the whole thread.
- ⚠ **Part of the improvement was NOT the fix, and saying so mattered.** Description coverage moved **44.6% → 69.1%** overnight on the catalog cron. The fix is a **no-op for 1- and 2-token queries by construction**, so every better bare-phrase result is coverage growth. **Any narrative measurement older than a day is a dated sample.**
- ⚠ **I went to ship my own filed fix for the top user-facing error and it was a REGRESSION — the planner agreed with me and was wrong.** Full detail now lives in the `fmv_snapshots` section; the short version is that "add `collection_id` to reach the covering index" does exactly what it promises and still loses, because the covering index is 113 MB with 15.6k lifetime scans against a 61 MB one with 38.9M, and its `Index Only Scan` still did **1,292 heap fetches of 1,531**. What shipped instead was `and fs.computed_at <= now()` — runtime partition pruning of two provably EMPTY partitions, **9,131 → 6,308 buffers on the same hot index**, semantically a no-op. ⚠ **It does NOT close `NEXTJS-1Z`**: the same query is 30 ms warm, 5.1 s evicted, 12.9 s while FMV recalc dirties pages. The mechanism is buffer thrash on a 2 GB instance.
- ⚠ **The #1 disk reader on the whole instance was a backfill asking the wrong question — 113 GB, and the fix was one cron argument.** Found by ranking `pg_stat_statements`, not by chasing the pages that were timing out. Live ticks **2:13 → 3.5 s**. Also dropped a 72 MB index unused in 63 days. Both are written up under "General rules", including the eleven unused indexes I deliberately did NOT drop and why.
- ⚠ **A tidy hypothesis nearly cost a working job.** The obvious story for that backfill was zombie rows that could never satisfy its join; a 200-row sample said **200/200 would be updated**. It had real work left and was simply being asked the wrong question. **A cheap sample beats a plausible mechanism.**
- **The player-bio columns landed and populated on their first cron tick** — 9,082 / 9,082 / 9,037 of 13,208 canonical Top Shot editions — so the `Play.stats` field placement was right and the 422 trap was dodged. ⚠ **But three of `classifySerial`'s eleven quirk kinds were structurally unreachable in production** because both callers passed only `circulationCount`. The concierge half is now wired (fail-soft, mutation-proven); `MomentDetailModal` is not. **Wiring a capability to its data is a separate act from shipping either one** — the second instance of that in two days.
- **Also verified rather than assumed:** the catalog walk's `sets_faulted: 11` reproduced **byte-identically** on the next night's run (9,396 / 541 / 258 / 11), which is strong evidence the fault set is STABLE, not flaky — filed with the cross-run query, since `pipeline_runs` retention is ~73 h and the `errors_sample` set ids vanish with it. And `refresh_wmc_fmv_changed` (112 GB, #2 reader) was characterized as **not a defect** so nobody spends a session on it.
- **Left for an operator, unchanged:** All Day prose/bio is still **0%** behind a WAF 403 that is almost certainly the one-line `ALLDAY_PROXY_URL` → `/allday-consumer` env change ([inbox 2026-08-14T0500Z](docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-14T0500Z-allday-waf-403-is-a-one-line-env-fix.md)). An env change needs a REAL rebuild — `vercel.json`'s `ignoreCommand` skips a docs-only or empty commit.

### August 14, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "improvements to the RPC profile page and trophy case, and how trophy cases get shared to Twitter/X" → "default the RPC username to their Top Shot username, keep going on the rest" → "keep going" ×6) — **the ask was flair and pretty link previews; the measurement said it was a FUNNEL problem — 16 of 20 collectors had no public profile at all, so there was nothing to flair and nothing to share.** ~20 pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, both gates green on every push, every deploy checked PER COMMIT.

- ⚠ **THE FRAMING FIND, from counting before building.** `profile_bio` held 20 rows but only **4 with a username** — and *a username is what creates `/profile/<username>` at all*. Every downstream feature (flair, sharing, follows, the OG card) was dead by default for 80% of signups. Closed forward (handle defaults from the Dapper/Top Shot username) and backward (16 collectors backfilled, Trevor-authorized): **4/20 → 20/20**. Canonical detail is in the Route-structure bullet.
- ⚠ **A false `$0` sat in the unfurl of every profile for ~2 months** — `generateMetadata` published "Portfolio: $0 FMV across 0 moments" on any failed read, and self-fetched its own API to get there. **Unnoticed because metadata output is invisible in the browser and only ever seen in someone else's timeline.** That is the FIFTH layer of the failed-read-renders-as-an-answer class and is now a row in the honesty table, along with the `openGraph`/`twitter` shallow-merge gotcha.
- **Shipped, in the order the thread found them:** all **43** OG cards branded + cached behind one loader; **trophy notes**, the flair field that already existed in schema, RPC and renderer with no UI to write it (0 of 16 trophies had one); a **shareable `/profile/<u>/trophy-case`** page + card; `/profile/edit` live preview; the unequip path; duplicate-pin prevention; and the picker **confirm step** that names the trophy it will replace.
- ⚠ **The rewards shop would sell a cosmetic the app cannot draw, and take the credits** — found by *assessing* a product decision ("should cosmetics be data-driven?") rather than answering it. Detail in the `lib/cosmetics.ts` bullet.
- ⚠ **THREE investigations ended in findings I could not ship, and filing them was the deliverable.** (1) The board's **top user-facing error by user count** (`pack detail bundle unavailable`, 40 users) is NOT a missing bound — deadline and error-vs-absent split were both already there; the RPC is 18,951 ms cold vs 406 ms warm. ⚠ **A later session took my filed fix, MEASURED it, and it was a regression** (the index it unlocks is 1.85× larger and effectively cold; its "Index Only Scan" does 1,292 heap fetches because delete-then-insert keeps pages non-all-visible) — **and my filing's "prunes the other partitions" claim was wrong.** Filing a hypothesis AS a hypothesis is what made that catch possible. (2) The **Pinnacle FMV drift guard compares `pinnacle_catalog` to ITSELF** — deal rows and comparison rows are the same table and columns, so it cannot fail for the reason it exists; it went tautological when the FMV source moved off `pinnacle_editions` and nothing announced it because nothing failed. **54 pages, none of them possible.** (3) **Two schedulers run the same wmc FMV propagation** over the same rows — **92 lock timeouts** beside 108 statement timeouts — and the heavier caller is invisible to the `pipeline_runs` logging added specifically to make that observable (logged max 34.9 s vs 364 s observed live).
- ⚠ **Method, and I paid for each.** A guard that fires is not a guard that is right: the drift-guard investigation killed **five** hypotheses, including one that looked conclusive (397 of 2,561 catalog rows really do have untrimmed `set_name`, and the leak strings show it) before reading `tripleKey` and finding it already trims. A `pg_stat_activity` query matching on a function name **matches itself** — that became a wrong "2 concurrent instances, confirmed live". And **`get_deployment.state` lags**: `BUILDING` across four polls over ~45 min on a deploy that was live and aliased, and I produced two confident wrong diagnoses before checking a second field — *a real adverse condition was underway, which is exactly what made the false story plausible*.
- ⚠ **Two of my own tests were vacuous and mutation caught both**: a font test that passed with fonts stubbed to null (the loader fails soft, so "a PNG came out" asserts nothing — assert a BYTE DIFFERENCE), and a flair test that varied fonts *and* flair, so the difference could have come from the typeface. **Hold everything but the one variable.**
- **Left open deliberately:** per-slab styles (needs a `slab_style` column plus a design decision on the finishes), and the drift-guard retire/re-point call. `tierAccent` has no case for Pinnacle's `STANDARD` tier — consistent grey, so a missing design decision rather than a bug.

### August 13, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "implement a TODO" → "keep going" ×8 → "don't stop until you've exhausted everything") — **started on a drained TODO backlog and ended by finding that the platform's biggest user-facing error is something WE cause every time we ship a migration.** ~10 pushes to `main`, every one CI-green, each ledgered with a revert path. No DB, migration, cron, auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or FMV/pricing-MATH change all session — the DB lane was a concurrent session's and I stayed out of it deliberately.

- **The in-code TODO backlog really is drained** (re-verified exhaustively, 0 markers outside `docs/`), so every item below came from measuring something instead: a guard's blind spot, a disk-read ranking, or the Sentry board.
- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE, and it corrects my own earlier claim in the same session.** `PGRST002 "Could not query the database for the schema cache"` was the largest real user-facing error — `NEXTJS-1Z`, **81 users / 84 events since 2026-07-18**, plus edition + player. I classified it transient (`4f303102`) and reported it fixed. **Then I went back to verify and it wasn't that simple**: every schema-cache event in 24 h fell inside ONE 11-second window, 10-21 s after `schema_migrations` shows a migration applied. **We cause these 500s ourselves, one ~20 s burst per `apply_migration`.** And `rpcWithRetry` is 3 attempts at 50 ms + 200 ms ≈ **250 ms** — entirely inside the first quarter-second of a twenty-second outage, so the classification is right in kind and **does not close NEXTJS-1Z**. Canonical rule + the filed options now live under the `apply_migration` bullet.
- ⚠ **The leak guard's greatest strength was exactly its blind spot.** `anon-api-no-driver-message-leak-guard` EXECUTES `isPublicPath` so it can never miss an anon route — and that same derivation **fixes its scope to the anon wall**. Sweeping the routes `proxy.ts` GATES found **120 more leaking sites across 64 files**: the collector's own profile / wallet / alerts / watchlist / portfolio. One (`/api/profile/trophy/reorder`) is rendered **straight into a toast**, so a statement timeout was shown to a collector as the reason their trophy case did not save. Fixed by codemod on exact `(file, line)` matches (never a broad regex — this repo has a 351-file incident), with 13 contract-key sites done by hand. **Two codemod regressions were caught by existing tests**: a 502→500 flattening in `breaks/*` and a silently dropped `rows: []`.
- ⚠ **`safeApiError` only knows Postgres, so blanket-applying it DESTROYS an actionable error** — four routes throw their own "Could not resolve `<username>`", and classifying that as `internal` replaced the one thing the user needed with "Something went wrong." at a **500 instead of a 400**. Hence `isUnresolvedIdentifierError` / `unresolvedIdentifierResponse`.
- **59 existing tests asserted the leak**, two titled *"500s and **surfaces the message**"* — a title that makes a defect read as the contract.
- ⚠ **Two guards were fabricating verdicts out of their own failed reads.** The Pinnacle FMV drift guard (`NEXTJS-14`, 54 events since May) built its comparison set from `pinnacle_catalog` and never destructured that read's `error` — a failed fetch emptied the set, so **every priced row "leaked"** and it hard-paged. Verified false against the DB: every named row was present with a matching FMV. Fixed to `couldNotRun`, with an empty-but-successful read deliberately still a real leak.
- ⚠ **A stale canonical claim nearly made me DELETE a live tripwire.** CLAUDE.md said the sentinel's `Sales Ingest (2h)` check was "superseded by the per-collection arm"; reading the live RPC showed it keys on **`sold_at`** while the sentinel keys on **`ingested_at`** — different questions. Kept it, and made it cheaper instead: bounding the **partition key** prunes 6 of 8 partitions (`EXPLAIN` **212,454 → 107,025**) and makes it STRICTER, since a lone history backfill used to satisfy it while every forward indexer was dead.
- **Perf/cost, code-only:** `count_insider_detector_candidates` was **44.7 GB / 27 GB-per-day of pure telemetry** with no production consumer (12 calls per hourly tick, 4× re-scanning the same 24 h window) — sampled to every 6th UTC hour, ~22 GB/day recovered. ⚠ It needed a new `candidates_status` field rather than reusing `null`, which already meant "the RPC errored" — otherwise a broken instrument becomes indistinguishable from a deliberate skip.
- **Bounded the edition page's last two unbounded table reads** (`withQueryDeadline`), picked up as a clean handoff from a concurrent session's filing — and **corrected its spec**: it suggested a second primitive, but `withDeadline` is already shape-agnostic, and extracting those two does NOT satisfy the server-page ratchet (it keys on the `@/lib/supabase` import, still present for two `rpcWithRetry` sites).
- **Measurements that closed flagged items:** the roadmap's **headline metric** was re-measured post-sweep — **Top Shot 17.3 → 54.5%**, **All Day 6.3 → 27.7%**, Pinnacle 30.3% (never measured before) — with the correction that §6's target MOVED, since All Day quadrupled while Top Shot tripled. And **Golazos was re-tested rather than accepted**: it is genuinely market-limited (only **14 of 575** editions have ≥3 sales in 30 d; the busiest traded **6 times in a month**), so its ceiling is ~2.4-2.8% and **there is no All-Day-shaped win there**.
- ⚠ **Sentry triage method:** my own first sweep filtered `firstSeen:-3d` and **hid the biggest issue on the platform for being a month old**. Sort by frequency and users; verify every "stale" verdict against the live DB. Two verified-stale reds resolved; `NEXTJS-28` deliberately left open (fired 2 h ago).
- ⚠ **A mutation test that stops the suite PARSING proves nothing** — my first attempt at one spliced a file by string index, broke it, and reported `no tests`; redone as a clean one-token change. Every new assertion this session is mutation-proven, several in both directions (the naive `"schema cache"` substring reds the PGRST205 cases, i.e. the obvious implementation is demonstrably wrong).
- **Concurrent-session discipline:** rebased onto their commits five times, took one clean handoff, corrected one of their specs, and stayed out of the DB lane entirely — every DB-side fix I found (`get_series_detail` at **20 s** on 12 public pages, the one-pass insider RPC, the `STALE`-share arm) was FILED with measurements rather than taken.

### August 13, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive, cont. — Trevor: "Game winner isn't just for the win set moments… think about the Damian Lillard Archive and Run It Back Legacy Moments") — **a domain expert reading the OUTPUT caught in one sentence what three green gates, 11k tests and my own live query could not: "narrative search" returns a SET ROSTER, and I had verified the defect and reported it as a success.**

- ⚠ **THE CORRECTION.** I shipped `search_catalog` as "the only tool that can answer a NARRATIVE query", cited `lillard game winner` → "Damian Lillard — **For the Win**" as live proof, and built a **"Find a game winner" pill** on top of it. **"For the Win" is a SET NAME containing the query words.** I confirmed set-name matching and called it prose matching — three times, in a tool description, a system prompt, and the canonical docs.
- **The measurement that settles it:** `game winner` (Top Shot, limit 30) returns **0** actual game winners — a For-The-Win set roster, **`Block` variants included**, which is exactly how the defect announces itself to anyone who knows the catalog. `buzzer beater`, `series winner`, `game winning shot`, `walk off winner`, `lillard game winner`: all **0**. ⚠ The two most famous Blazers game winners are missed entirely — the 2014 0.9-second series winner (**Archive Set `48:1652`**) and the 2019 37-footer (**Run It Back: Legacies `121:4255`**) — **though BOTH carry descriptions**, one containing "game-winning" verbatim, the other "buzzer". Single-token `buzzer` returns *Buddy Hield* on a fuzzy NAME match instead.
- ⚠ **Root cause established by CONTROL, not inference — and it is not what it looks like.** The prose arm is **not broken**: `weber state`, a phrase present only in description text, resolves to exactly the right row. Matches are scored with trigram **`similarity()`, which is LENGTH-NORMALIZED**, so a 200-character paragraph containing the phrase verbatim scores BELOW the short set name "For The Win". **Prose only wins on a distinctive proper noun; every generic narrative phrase loses to a set name.** `similarity()` is the wrong scorer for a long text field — fix is a containment boost or full-text ranking, **NOT** raising the `via_prose` 0.12 constant (the gap is structural, not an offset).
- ⚠ **THE METHOD LESSON, and it is the one I keep paying for.** My verification asked *"did it return rows?"* instead of *"are the rows RIGHT?"* — the identical failure mode I had filed an inbox note about earlier the same session (a probe that passes during the outage it exists to catch). **For a relevance or ranking feature, the check must be a known-correct ANSWER, not a non-empty result** — and the cheapest source of a known-correct answer is someone who knows the domain.
- **Shipped:** pill REMOVED (live ~40 min demoing set-name matching as narrative search) + a test blocking re-add until the ranking is fixed **and verified against those two slugs**; tool description and prompt section rewritten to stop promising narrative search, name the concrete failure, and require the bot to READ results before presenting — if hits all share one set name and don't match what was described, say so rather than pass a roster off as an answer. Ranking defect FILED (`inbox/2026-08-14T0310Z-…`), not taken: `rpc_search_catalog` is a concurrent session's function shipped the same day.
- **Verified:** `tsc` clean; primary **11,563 green**; component **1,614 green**.

### August 13, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "CX and UX improvements? Also expand the concierge's capabilities and feed it additional intent abilities") — **the two new intents came from asking what the PLATFORM can answer that the BOT cannot; both UX finds were things that had never worked rather than things that broke.** 4 waves to `main`, each ledgered + deployed READY (checked per commit); both gates green; `tsc` clean.

- **Concierge 29 → 31 tools.** `search_catalog` (the catalog INDEX, searching `editions.description` as well as names — ⚠ **but see the correction entry above: it does NOT deliver narrative search**, and I shipped it claiming otherwise) and `get_price_history` (ACTUAL SALE PRINTS back to 2020, the only path past the ~4.5 months `fmv_snapshots` covers). Canonical detail + the honesty constraints now live in the AI Concierge section, not here. The gap existed because the site shipped narrative search EARLIER THE SAME DAY and nobody wired the assistant to it — worth remembering that a new capability does not reach the bot for free.
- ⚠ **A capability with no route to discovery is half-shipped** — but the route matters. Narrative search is the least guessable thing the concierge does (nobody thinks to ask a support bot to find a moment by what HAPPENS in it), so it needed surfacing. I first put it in the **welcome copy** and then reversed that in the same session: it is the first thing every user reads, the prompt's own rule is "one tight, human line, not a menu dump", and most users are on mobile — a clause there is read by everyone and acted on by almost nobody. It is now a **pill** ("Find a game winner"): no reading cost, one tap, and it DEMONSTRATES the capability by running it. ⚠ **Top Shot pages ONLY** — prose is ~45% there and **0%** on every other collection, and `search_catalog` scopes to the active collection, so the same pill on an All Day page would demo a coverage gap instead of the feature. Pinned + mutation-proven; do not copy it to other collections until their coverage is non-zero.
- ⚠ **The chat panel ignored the light theme, and had since the theme shipped.** Hardcoded `#0d0d0d`/`#141414`/`#ccc` while `ThemeToggle` flips ~20 tokens: a light-mode collector got a black slab over a white page. **Not a regression — it never worked**, which is why no test and no monitor ever said anything.
- ⚠ **Every `/insights/*` link the bot handed out was inert text**, for the same reason: the bubble rendered the message as one raw string and the repo has no markdown renderer. The prompt tells the bot these boards are "the most shareable thing RPC has" and the UI made them un-clickable.
- ⚠ **THE MOST TRANSFERABLE FIND — a regex I shipped would have killed the whole chat on older Safari, and every gate was green.** `rich-text.ts` used an ES2018 lookbehind. **`tsc` does not transpile regex literals**, so the pattern ships verbatim and is a `SyntaxError` at **PARSE** time on Safari < 16.4 — the module never evaluates, `SupportChat` never imports, the concierge vanishes. CI passed because the entire toolchain runs on Node (lookbehind since v8.3). **A Node test suite is structurally incapable of catching a browser-support defect**; only a source guard can. Fixed in a follow-up (`a0944b6c`) with that guard.
- ⚠ **TWO of my own tests were vacuous, both caught by mutation, both for the same underlying reason — the environment could not produce the failure.** (1) The focus-restore assertion passed with the fix REMOVED, because jsdom happily focuses a `display:none` element; rewritten to assert the MECHANISM (the launcher must not be hidden after close). (2) The pill test asserted two states in sequential `waitFor`s, which a plain overwrite satisfies at DIFFERENT TIMES — page pill before the fetch lands, server pill after. **Two assertions individually true at different moments do not prove they are ever true together; when state arrives asynchronously, pin the ORDER of the waits.**
- ⚠ **My own source guard tripped on its own comment** (the file explains why it refuses to render HTML, naming the banned API) — third instance in this repo, after `pack-dist-contents-not-streamed` and `collection-analytics-failed-vs-empty-guard`. **Any guard that searches source for a string must strip comments first.**
- **Also fixed:** the header ✕ and the launcher shared the accessible name "Close chat"; focus restoration silently no-op'd because the launcher hides itself while an input is focused; the chat was the only overlay of six with no `role="dialog"`/Escape/`aria-live`; and `PAGE_DEFAULTS`' 35 per-page pill sets were **dead code on every session**, overwritten ~200ms after open by the context route's generic list.
- ⚠ **Could NOT verify by HTTP round-trip:** the sandbox proxy blocks `rippackscity.com` (403 CONNECT), so production evidence is the direct Supabase RPC checks (`rpc_search_catalog`, `get_edition_sale_history`, `edition_description_coverage` all queried live) plus the suites — not a real request through the deployed routes.
- **Verified:** primary gate **11,397 tests green, 91.57 st / 78.91 br**; component gate **1,590 green, 90.03 / 81.42**; brand-token guard clean. Commits `9d383d6e` (tools) · `7114dc9e` (UX) · `a0944b6c` (Safari) · `43c14350` (pills + discovery copy).

### August 13, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "analyze test coverage → propose" → "do all you can that makes sense, don't stop until you've exhausted your options") — **measured first, and the measurement said breadth was already closed: 452/452 API routes and 250/254 `lib/` files are referenced by a test. So every find below came from the STRUCTURAL blind spots — and there turned out to be TWO of them, together ~75k LOC.** 6 waves to `main`, each ledgered; both gates green; `tsc` clean throughout. Primary gate 91.18→**91.54** st / 78.02→**78.90** br, ~360 tests added.

- ⚠ **The blind spot: `app/**/page.tsx` is 48,325 LOC measured by NEITHER gate** — comparable to the entire component gate's surface (45,875 LOC) — and **79 of those pages query Supabase directly**. The repo's existing answer was `server-pages-error-vs-absent-guard.test.ts`: hand-written source assertions covering **2** pages, i.e. a per-file allowlist for a class with ~79 members. Now also held by **`server-page-data-access-ratchet.test.ts`**, which freezes the count at **18** (36 when first landed → 35 `/moment/[id]` → 34 edition page → **26** when EIGHT `/insights` board pages moved to one shared `lib/insights/board-page-fetch.ts` → 25 when a DEAD import was deleted → 24 the analytics sets-detail page → 23 the allday-scarcity page and its API route on ONE shared query → **18** when FIVE more `/insights` board pages (squeeze, trophies, set-squeeze, offer-spread, pinnacle-scarcity) each moved onto a shared `lib/insights/<board>-board.ts` with its own route — a ratchet is lowered by the change that earns it, in the same commit). **Down to 8 as of 2026-08-16** (18 → 14 → 13 → 12 → 11 → **8**, each lowered in the commit that earned it). ⚠ **AND THE HONESTY SWEEP BEHIND IT IS NOW COMPLETE: all 8 remaining pages already distinguish a failed read from an absence** — verified page by page (`insights/cross-collection` carries `ok` on all three legs, `insights/market` a `loadError`, `insights` and `admin/flowty-errors` degrade by OMISSION, `challenges`/`hot-floors` carry an `errored` flag consulted *before* their empty state, and `pack/[id]` + `analytics/wallets` are pinned by the source guard). **What remains on the ratchet is structural debt with no known defect**, which is a materially different thing from where this started; the next conversion buys coverage, not a fix. ⚠ Extraction remained the way to REACH the defects right up to the end — every one of the 2026-08-16 finds below was invisible until the code moved somewhere a test could drive it, and each was already accompanied by a comment asserting the right behaviour. **The comment is not the check.** ⚠ When a page duplicates its route's query, **share the QUERY, not the POLICY**: return supabase-js's `{ data, error }` untouched so the route keeps `boardUnavailable()` and the page keeps `ok:false`, and let their `limit` defaults stay different. ⚠ And copy the route's ordering EXACTLY, **including secondary sorts** — a first draft dropped the squeeze route's `squeeze_pct` tiebreaks, which would have silently reordered equal-valued rows between the server HTML and the client's first refetch. A ban would have shipped a 35-entry allowlist (theatre); a ratchet forces NEW pages through `lib/` where a gate watches them. It carries a not-vacuous check and a **no-slack** assertion, because a ratchet with headroom silently licenses the next N additions. ⚠ **A NOT-VACUOUS CHECK MUST BE SATISFIABLE AT A POPULATION OF ZERO — ours was not, and it went RED on its own success (2026-08-16).** It asserted `pages.length > 10`; the population reached 8 and the guard failed. That is precisely the failure its own next comment warns about for naming a specific page — *"the guard would punish its own success"* — expressed as a threshold instead of a name, which is why nobody spotted it while writing the warning two lines below. **Assert that the ENUMERATOR still sees the tree** (the walk finds >50 `page.tsx` files), not that many of them are still dirty; the dirty count is the thing you are driving to zero, so it can never also be the evidence that the check works.
- **Extraction as the remedy** (`lib/trophy-case/pdf-image.ts` precedent): the pack-dist page's 11 fetchers → `lib/pack-dist/fetchers.ts`, injectable `db`, uniform `{ data, ok }`. ⚠ **It immediately exposed a live defect**: `fetchTopPulls` returned `[]` on a query ERROR and the caller renders an empty list as *"Drop-pool contents aren't indexed for this distribution yet"* — **a claim about OUR INDEX manufactured from OUR outage**, on packs whose pool is fully indexed. `fetchExhaustedCount` returned a literal **`0`** that a section header printed as a measured count. ⚠ **"Not applicable" is NOT a failure**: a Top-Shot-only panel on an All Day pack reports `ok: true`, else every All Day pack page carries a permanent degraded banner — the cry-wolf outcome `board-status.ts` warns about.
- ⚠ **The OG cards were the sharpest find, and the copy could not possibly have been true** — 15 of them printed `Loading the live board…` into a **static, edge-cached PNG**. Full detail in the honesty-layer table under "Key files"; it is now the FOURTH row of that table.
- **The three worst-covered files in the primary gate were all OG cards** (og/moment 21.4% br, og/edition 25.5%, og/pack 36.0%) because the existing sweep is deliberately breadth-only. New `__tests__/helpers/og-capture.ts` stubs `next/og` and asserts the rendered element tree, so the tests pin **honesty logic, not layout**: the closed-market FMV suppression, and the pack card's **survivor-bias guard** (a depleted TS pool prices at 40–86×, and a green **+EV** badge on an unfurl is a buy signal reaching people who never open the page). ⚠ Complementary to the sweep, not a replacement — the stub cannot see a satori render failure.
- ⚠ **My own push ERRORed the production deploy, and only a per-commit check caught it.** See the second bullet in "Vercel tool behavior" — same class as first-mint, different page, and the build failure and the honesty defect turned out to be the same bug.
- ⚠ **The SECOND blind spot, found only because I asked what a passing guard could not see.** `insights-gate-include-completeness` requires every `"use client"` `page.tsx` to be named `*Client.tsx` so the component gate catches it — a good guard, and it **walks `INSIGHTS_DIR`**. So it is silent about client pages anywhere else *by construction*, however often it runs green: **33 of them, 27,016 LOC** (`dashboard` 2,299 · `[collection]/sniper` 1,748 · `[collection]/analytics` 1,706). Held now by `client-page-gate-ratchet.test.ts`. **Third time in one session that a guard's own derivation fixed its blast radius** — the others being the anon driver-message guard and the server-page guard's hand-written 2-of-79 list.
- ⚠ **The same error-vs-absent defect turned up a FOURTH and FIFTH time, and the fourth is the worst of them.** `/moment/[id]` — the platform's most-shared URL, where every link posted into Discord or a DM lands — returned a bare `null` for both "no such moment" and "the RPC failed" and answered `notFound()`, so a timeout told a collector who had *just shared the link* that their moment does not exist, and handed a crawler a hard 404. ⚠ **It has TWO `ok`s that must never be merged**: the RPC's own `ok` means "I looked and there is none" (an ANSWER, still 404s); the envelope's means the read worked. **Both merge directions are mutation-proven**, because collapsing them either way is a defect — one makes a timeout 404, the other makes every genuinely-missing moment render "unavailable". Its metadata failure branch is `noindex, follow` so an outage cannot de-index a real moment. Fifth was `/analytics/sets/[set_id]`, above.
- ⚠ **Two OG cards could 500 into a BLANK unfurl.** `og/pack` and `og/pack/lifecycle` had no try/catch: **supabase-js RETURNS a Postgrest error but THROWS on a transport failure**, and an uncaught throw escapes `GET`. Both files' headers already promised "never 500" — it held only for the error-return path. Swept the family: `og/deal`/`og/collection` also lack a catch but are param-only and do no I/O.
- ⚠ **I inferred instead of reading, twice more, and the tests caught both.** Writing the `og/pack/lifecycle` suite I took `fmtUsd`'s threshold and the stat labels from the SIBLING `og/pack` card — but this file rounds at ≥100 (`$120`, not `$120.00`) and its ripped path labels the anchor `PAID`, not `LAST BOUGHT`/`RETAIL`. **Reading the sibling is not reading the file.** Same class as the `ufc-strike` slug earlier the same day.
- ⚠ **Four things I did NOT do, recorded so nobody re-derives them.** (0) `app/api/cron/pinnacle-listings-reconcile` is the worst-covered file in the gate at 28.6% **because `ASK_UNIFY_RETIRED = true` short-circuits it** and everything after is unreachable rollback code — testing it inflates coverage for nothing. (0b) `og/{set,team,series}` lack the closed-market FMV suppression `og/moment`/`og/edition` have, but **their own pages don't suppress either**, so aggregate FMV on a dead market is a product decision, not a test fix. (1) Extracting the 5 forward sales indexers' batch-insert retry (~529 uncovered branches, the largest remaining cluster): `sales-batch-insert-23505-guard.test.ts` is source-level **by design** and says so, extraction would break it, and **rewriting a guard to accommodate a refactor of production ingest with a permanent-data-loss failure mode is a bad trade for branch percentage**. (2) The `/analytics` overview loaders — they gate every total on `> 0`, so a failed read renders a status line, not a manufactured `$0`; I had estimated a defect there and the code was already correct.
- ⚠ **A test I deleted rather than tuned to green.** An OG "with rows" case needed a fixture per card's real row shape; one generic fixture satisfied 10 of 15. Passing would only have meant **my invented shape happened to fit** — the impossible-fixture trap. Replaced with a spelling-independent structural assertion, and ⚠ **my first structural attempt was also too narrow** (`.length === 0`; `market` uses `heads.every(h => h.median == null)` and correctly red it).
- ⚠ **A pre-existing guard had to be REPOINTED, which is a distinct and legitimate move from weakening one.** `pack-dist-contents-not-streamed.test.ts` asserted the literal `Promise<EditionTile[] | null>` — a TYPE SIGNATURE that legitimately moved to `lib/`. It now pins the **property** (the shell mapping `ok` back to the `null` the panel branches on). **A guard that pins an implementation detail reds on a correct refactor and teaches people to edit guards to make builds pass.**
- **Every new assertion mutation-proven** (removed the market-closed suppression → 1 red; disabled the survivor-bias guard → 3; reverted the two pack-dist swallows → 2; flipped one card's `fetched` → 2; dropped the Data-Saver skip and the packs-surface gate → 4). Ratchet raised 90.4/77.0/92.2/92.6 → **91.2/78.3/93.0/93.35**; see the CI-ratchet bullet for why that raise was ~0.9 rather than an increment. ⚠ Also: **UFC's registry slug is `ufc`, not `ufc-strike`** — I inferred it, and an unrecognised segment falls back to the last-visited collection, so the wrong slug silently tests Top Shot; the paired positive assertion caught it.

### August 13, 2026 (Claude Code, docs, SECOND pass of the day — "analyze repo → update CLAUDE.md to current state") — docs-only maintenance on tip `48369a45`: re-verified all 13 canonical-reference facts (**every one CURRENT — the earlier pass today had just corrected them**), so the work was entirely in the four CODE commits that landed since `df0779fb`. Three carried durable lessons documented nowhere canonical, and one of them **invalidated a claim in the canonical sections rather than merely extending it**. No product/DB/prod change; direct to `main`. Ledger entry 2026-08-13.

- **The headline: a canonical bullet was asserting a scope that was wrong, and the wrongness is the transferable part.** `lib/api-error.ts` was documented as "the REQUIRED failure path for every route an **ANONYMOUS** visitor can reach". `7e6a4a2c` swept the routes `proxy.ts` **gates** and found **120 more leaking sites across 64 files** — the collector's own `/profile`, `/wallet`, `/alerts`, `/watchlist`, `/portfolio`. ⚠ **The anon guard's greatest strength is exactly why it could never have found them:** it keeps no route list *because* it executes `isPublicPath`, which is what makes it impossible to miss an anon route — and that same derivation **fixes its scope to that wall**, putting everything behind sign-in outside it by construction. The bullet now leads with the correction rather than quietly swapping the word, because a future reader who trusts a scope claim will not re-derive it. Fifth mis-filing of this one defect.
- **Recorded because the remedy nearly caused its own harm:** blanket-applying `safeApiError` (which only knows Postgres) to four routes that throw their own "Could not resolve `<username>`" replaced an actionable **400** with "Something went wrong." at a **500** — deleting the one thing the user needed. Hence `isUnresolvedIdentifierError` / `unresolvedIdentifierResponse`. Also `aafd014d`: `/api/pack-listings` was publishing **Dapper Studio's** wording — the leaked text is not always ours or Postgres's — and two codemod regressions worth knowing (a 502 flattened to 500, losing the "did WE break?" signal; `rows: []` dropped beside a 500, which is correct but must be deliberate).
- **Added the fourth commit's lesson to the conflation family** (`48369a45`): sampling the insider-detector telemetry needed a **new field**, not a reused `null`, because `candidates_evaluated: null` already meant "the RPC errored" — overloading it makes a broken instrument indistinguishable from a deliberate skip. Its operational half is worth the space too: that pure telemetry was **~27 GB/day, ~3% of all disk reads** on the IO-throttled tier, with no production consumer.
- **No correction due on `788a5605`** (the sentinel `sold_at` bound) — the concurrent session's own `bee9e7c3` had already landed it in the `sales` bullet accurately, including the part that matters most: the check was **not** deleted as redundant, and the two probes answer different questions.
- **No tail roll due.** Zone read before converting (`date '+%Z'` → **PDT**; 12:59 PT / 19:59Z) — same PT calendar day as the earlier pass, so Recent sessions still spans Aug 13 · Aug 12 · Aug 11 and the archive pointer stays "August 10 → August 1". Two docs passes on one day is not duplication: the first was **facts drifting under stable claims**, this one is **a claim invalidated by new work**.

Also recorded why it calls the handler **in-process** rather than self-fetching (absolute-base-URL fragility, a second lambda, fails closed behind preview SSO) and why it logs nothing itself (the walker already writes the row; two would double-count).
- **Addition 1 — `wmc.fmv_confidence`** (`6bd15560`), undocumented in the wmc schema section. wmc is the portfolio store and ~34 DB functions sum `wmc.fmv_usd`, but the table carried the VALUE with no LABEL, so confidence was structurally unavailable at the point a portfolio total is computed and `/share/[wallet]` emitted FMV with no confidence field at all. Captured the two non-obvious decisions a future editor would otherwise re-litigate: **deliberately unindexed** (the cohort index already INCLUDEs `fmv_usd`; HOT ratio 1.8%), and the force-path change-detection **widened to fire on a differing label** — without which already-correct rows keep a NULL label forever.

It ran long enough to reach Top Shot 7% exact-match / AllDay p95 14.2× against `fmv_current`. Generalized into the sections rather than left in a commit message: **a 202 route and a succeeding wrapper do not make the work inside observable.**
- **Also recorded, in the `editions.description` bullet: the catalog walker's "empty vs refused" fix** (`9909f27d`) — `fetchEditionsPage` returned `… | null`, collapsing "this set has no editions" with "we could not ask", which is *why* the 422 regression logged `ok: true`. Now a discriminated `PageFetch` carrying the upstream fault (whose body NAMES the offending field), a `sets_faulted` counter, and — deliberately — `ok` does NOT redden on a single fault, because a chronically-red pipeline trains the operator to skim past it, the exact cost already paid for with `ufc_fmv_stale_hours`.

- **Relationship to the entries below:** those are shipping logs; this is the docs-maintenance log. The distinction is load-bearing — **dated entries are frozen history and roll off to `docs/sessions/` within ~3 days**, so a fact left only in a session log stops being found by the canonical sections a future session actually reads. That is exactly how all five figures above drifted. Two open items in the dated Aug-13 overnight-pass entry have since been fixed by other sessions and are noted here rather than by editing that frozen entry: `compute-pinnacle-pack-ev`'s deterministic `ON CONFLICT` self-collision (`bd53bb3a`) and the `allday-pack-opens` unreachable-tip early return that sat outside the `try` and so wrote no row (`033c0c15`).

### August 13, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — continuation of the Aug-12 honesty thread: "keep going" ×3 → wrap-up) — **the turn's most useful finds were a defect I shipped 40 minutes earlier and two errors in my own ledger.** 3 pushes to `main` (`7f48e928` → `b32c7363` → `6db740b3`), both gates green on each, all deployed READY. No DB, migration, cron, auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or FMV/pricing-MATH change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — a disk-IO saturation spell can FAIL THE WHOLE PRODUCTION BUILD, and an ERRORed deploy is easy to miss.** Found by sweeping deploys PER COMMIT: `dpl_FwbnxURHqSbbYRqCQus44Cxxgyhc` (`6960281c`) was state **ERROR** — `/insights/first-mint` exceeded Next's 60s per-page export budget ×3 → `npm run build` exited 1. The next push superseded it and went READY, so the tip looked healthy. **Check the deploy state of the commit you shipped, not the state of the tip.** Fixed by `BOARD_LIVE_TIMEOUT_MS` (8s) — full detail in the Vercel bullet under "Vercel tool behavior".
- **⚠ DURABLE — the stale rung existed and could not be reached.** first-mint's snapshot was ~85 min old at build time, present and usable. `readBoardOrLive` only fell back when the live query **ERRORED**, and a query that is merely SLOW errors nowhere. **Slow and broken are equally unservable; only broken was modelled.** That exact shape then turned up twice more in the same subsystem (panini's `partial` flag covering only the error exit, and `summarizeDegraded` reading `partial` only inside its `!ok` branch), which is why the third fix went into the PRIMITIVE rather than a third call site.
- **⚠ I SHIPPED A DEFECT AND CAUGHT IT BY ASKING A DIFFERENT QUESTION.** `f02362c7` made panini's page-cap exit return `partial: true` — correct — but the status it passed to `summarizeDegraded` was `{ ok: rows.ok, partial }`, and a capped read is `ok: true`, which that function skipped. Result: an EMPTY board with NO notice, strictly worse than the silent truncation it replaced. **The root cause was the test:** it asserted `res.ok` and `res.error` — what the FUNCTION RETURNS — and never `payload.degraded`, what the READER SEES.
- **⚠ DURABLE — a mutation test caught a test of mine that asserted nothing.** The obvious "does not time out a fast query" case passes **even with the budget set to 0 ms**, because `Promise.race` settles an immediately-resolving microtask before the `setTimeout` macrotask runs. **When you test a timeout, the happy-path case must take REAL TIME or the assertion is vacuous.**
- **The board-warm telemetry shipped 08-12 paid off within hours.** The failures now name their views: `deals` → `cross_collection_deals_board` timing out, `first-mint` → BOTH its views. With `service_role`'s `statement_timeout=30s` and `topshot_first_mint_trophy_stats` measured at **2,047 ms** on a quiet instance (its covering index alive and used, 591 scans), that is a **~15× throttling multiplier, not a plan defect** — so the lever is the shared materialize-latest-FMV item, NOT another index. Filed: [inbox 2026-08-12T2330Z](docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-12T2330Z-board-view-timeouts-now-named.md).
- **Two tidy stories died on measurement, both recorded so nobody re-derives them.** (1) The warm cron's ~32s-per-5-minutes duty cycle looked like reclaimable waste — but the pages are ISR `revalidate=300` with `s-maxage=300`, so the cadence already matches what a consumer can observe. (2) Sweeping the estate for more blind instruments found exactly one candidate, `promote_unmapped_sales`, which on inspection is a *working* silent-no-op detector whose condition IS its reason.
- **⚠ DURABLE — TWO ledger errors of my own, both corrected in `6db740b3`.** (a) The session resumed across a day boundary and I carried the previous turn's date forward: two headings said 08-12 for commits stamped **2026-08-13 07:30 / 07:48 PDT**. A concurrent session hit the identical class the same day. **Read the zone, never assume it** — `date '+%Z'`; this sandbox is **PDT**, so the reflexive "subtract 7h from `date -u`" lands a day EARLY. (b) **`cont. N` in the ledger is NOT session-unique** — two sessions increment the same counter and 08-12 alone carries duplicate 16/19/20/21, so a `cont. N` cross-reference is ambiguous by construction. **Cite the sha.** I corrected only my own headings and references; renumbering another session's entries would be the whole-file rewrite the ledger's own rules forbid.

### August 12–13, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "analyze test coverage → propose" → "do everything" → "keep going" ×8) — **a long coverage program that kept turning into a DEFECT program: every real bug below was found by writing the test for code that already looked fine.** ~14 pushes to `main`, all CI-green; both gates green on every push; each change ledgered with a revert path. No DB, migration, cron, auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or FMV/pricing-MATH change.

- **Gates moved, but that was the by-product.** Primary 89.3→91.2 st / 75.1→77.9 br (1,150 files / 10,966 tests); component 79.0→~90 st / 67.0→81.3 br (185 files / 1,560 tests). Closed the `.tsx` blind spot (44 `route.tsx` + 2 libs, ~8k LOC measured by NEITHER gate), the `app/**` vs `app/api/**` sitemap gap, and a coverage glob that was **valid and matched nothing** (`app/(collections)/**` — route-group parens are picomatch extglob syntax), now rot-guarded.
- **User-facing defects found and shipped** (each mutation-proven, each with a positive mirror so the fix cannot be "always say couldn't load"): the mobile moment table had **no empty state at all**; a public profile could report **"↑ 50000.0% / 30D"** from a `|| 1` divide-by-zero guard; the same moment showed **a different P&L on a phone than a desktop**; the Candy board **silently sliced a ranking**; `TeamChecklist` claimed an empty catalogue **and disabled its own retry**; and ~10 client components reported a failed fetch as a market finding ("No recent whale trades.", "No Flowty marketplace activity in this window.", and — sharpest — *"try a longer time range or lower min FMV floor"*, advice to fix a filter that was never the problem). All four durable lessons are recorded in **Key files** above.
- **⚠ Tests that ASSERTED the defect were a recurring find, not a one-off.** ~35 existing tests pinned a leak or a false claim across the session, two titled *"500s **with** the message"*, and one ran on a `costBasisLabel` that **does not exist in the vocabulary** (`"Pack pull"`; the map emits `"Pack"`), so it exercised a path real data never reaches — the D9 impossible-fixture shape, hit independently twice. **A green test proves nothing if its fixture is unreachable; check a literal against the map that produces it.**
- **⚠ My own errors, all caught before CI except one.** I pushed once with a failing `tsc` because commit/push were chained without `&&` — reddened `main` ~4 min. A mechanical test-repair regex hit **351 files instead of 18** (reverted, redone by exact `(file, line, string)` targeting). I twice INFERRED a shape instead of reading it (a sort key, a prop envelope). And `echo "TSC=$?"` after a pipe into `head` reports HEAD's exit — it printed a green `0` over a real `TS2698`; only `TSC=$?; [ "$TSC" -ne 0 ] && exit 1` works.
- **⚠ Concurrent-session collision is now a real cost on this repo.** A parallel session shipped the anon-API driver-message sweep while I had an equivalent one ready; **theirs was strictly better** (43 route sites *plus* 33 concierge tool-result sites no HTTP-body grep can see, a guard that EXECUTES `isPublicPath`, `Cache-Control: no-store`), so I deleted mine unpushed — the same call as dropping my `edge-fn-no-hardcoded-gate-keys` fix earlier. What survived was the cross-check: their guard had **four** leak patterns where the older one had five, and the missing fifth was still live on 12 sites. Later rounds deliberately stayed in the component layer while they worked `lib/insights`/API/DB, and the two halves of the truncation fix (my client `DataTable`, their fetch layer) **composed** rather than collided. Cost across the session: one full duplication and ~6 rebase conflicts.

### August 12, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "implement a TODO" → "fix the error.message leak in those six routes too, along with anything else unresolved" → "keep going" ×N → "do what you think is best") — **the driver-message leak has now been filed FIVE times, each at the size of wherever the last person happened to be looking; this pass measured the boundary instead of guessing it.** Multiple pushes to `main`; `tsc` clean, both coverage gates green, each wave ledgered. No DB, migration, cron, auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or FMV/pricing-MATH change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — the scope history IS the finding, and it is the reason the remedy is not another list.** D3 filed it as `/api/sets`. 2026-08-11 filed it as six `/api/public/insights` routes, then found all **29**. This pass swept the routes **`proxy.ts` actually lets anonymous visitors reach** and found **43 more sites across 33 files** — plus **33 concierge tool-result sites** invisible to any HTTP-body grep. Five filings, five under-counts, every one honest at the time it was written. A hand-maintained route list cannot catch the route nobody thought to add.
- **The guard EXECUTES the security wall rather than describing it.** `__tests__/anon-api-no-driver-message-leak-guard.test.ts` imports the real `isPublicPath` from `proxy.ts` and runs it over every route file on disk, so widening the public surface widens the guard **in the same commit**. Mutation-proven (re-introduced the leak in `/api/market`; it reds). It excludes `/api/admin/**` + `/api/cron/**` — proxy-bypassed *precisely so* their own bearer-secret check can BE the gate — and then **asserts each excluded route really carries that gate**, so the exclusion can never quietly come to cover an ungated admin route. That assertion immediately flagged 5 routes my first detector called ungated; all 5 delegate to shared helpers (`verifyAdminRequest`, `runStudioHistoryDrain`), each verified to 401 before the predicate was widened.
- **One primitive, not a fourth peer helper.** `apiErrorResponse(err, tag, fallback?)` moved into `lib/api-error.ts` and `boardUnavailable` is now a thin alias of it — so the CLAUDE.md three-layer map (route / server page / client dashboard) still has exactly three entries. Behaviour-preserving for the boards (its 8 tests pass untouched).
- **⚠ The concierge surface is structurally invisible to an HTTP-body sweep.** 33 sites in `support-chat/route.ts` returned `JSON.stringify({ status: "error", message: err.message })` as **tool results** — the model reads them and can quote them, so Postgres's own wording reaches a visitor *through the assistant* with no response body ever carrying it. Now `safeApiError(err, "<tool> failed").error`, which also gives the model actionable copy ("the database is under heavy load") instead of driver text it has to paraphrase.
- **⚠ 29 existing tests asserted the LEAK** — `expect(body.error).toBe("db down")` — across 21 files, plus 2 more the route→test name heuristic missed (`api-moment-id`, `api-public-media-and-detail-routes`; one was even titled *"500s (with the RPC message)"*). **A test that pins a defect makes the defect look like the contract**, and a rename does not remove that: each was rewritten to `not.toContain(<driver text>)`, which asserts the property that actually matters.
- **The status half matters as much as the body.** These all returned a bare **500**; a statement timeout is now **503 + `Retry-After`**, keeping transient capacity out of the hard-5xx budget that pages on genuine breakage, with `Cache-Control: no-store` so a momentary blip is not edge-cached into a sustained outage alongside these routes' `s-maxage` success responses.
- **Two routes kept their shapes deliberately**, spreading the classified error in rather than being converted: `/api/ready` (`status: "error"` is its contract with the monitoring consumers named in its own header) and `/api/subscribe` (`success: false` is its contract with the two email-capture components). `/api/moment/[id]` went the other way — its `{ ok: false }` **is** the RPC's "no such moment" verdict, so reusing it for a database failure made an outage indistinguishable from a genuine miss.
- **Also fixed at the client layer:** `TrophyPickerModal` reported ANY non-ok moment lookup as *"Couldn't find a moment with that ID"*, so a 503 told the user their moment does not exist — a claim about the catalogue manufactured from a database outage. Now branches on `res.status >= 500`, with a companion test that fails if the branch is removed.

### August 12–13, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive, cont. — "Do everything you can" → descriptor discovery → "keep going") — **NARRATIVE SEARCH IS LIVE: "Damian Lillard game winner" now returns the For the Win moments.** The thread's goal, reached — and the two things that nearly stopped it were both MY OWN diagnostics lying. Multiple pushes to `main`, 1 column + 1 view + 2 CONCURRENTLY indexes, each verified live and ledgered.

- **⚠ DURABLE — Top Shot `description` is on `Play`, NOT `PlayStats`, and getting that wrong FAILS SILENTLY WITH `ok: true`.** An invalid field invalidates the WHOLE GraphQL query; Top Shot answers **HTTP 422**; `fetchPage` returns null; `backfill-topshot-catalog` walks every set and upserts **ZERO** editions while logging success. **The only tell was `gql_calls` EXACTLY equalling `sets_processed`** (257 == 257 — one call per set means every call died on its first page; a healthy walk needs multiple pages, and the fixed run showed 327 > 258). Two live runs wrote nothing before this was caught. Blast radius was limited only because that route is on **no cron**.
- **⚠ DURABLE — a diagnostic must have a CONTROL, or a total failure is indistinguishable from a total absence.** The descriptor probe's v1 reported `exists:false` for EVERY field on both leagues — including `classification` and `dateOfMoment`, which our live ingest queries daily. Two transport failures (wrong AllDay endpoint → 404; `setID: null` → Top Shot 422) had been rendered as schema facts. v2 made `status` `yes|no|unknown` (a transport failure is NEVER `no`), probed CONTROL fields we already use in production, and reported the arm INCONCLUSIVE with `absent: []` when they fail. **v2 then immediately paid for itself**: it refused to answer and its captured error BODIES named both causes exactly. v3 fixed the causes; **v4 added `on: "PlayStats"|"Play"` to every finding** because the flat `found` list was what let the `Play`-vs-`PlayStats` mix-up happen at all.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the live Top Shot `searchEditions` contract** (copy it from `backfill-topshot-catalog`, do not improvise): `filters` is **REQUIRED** (`bySetIDs`, **UUID-format set ids only**) plus `searchInput.pagination`, and the response path is the **double-`data` inline-fragment** `searchSummary.data.data[]` (`... on Editions { data { ... on Edition } }`). The flat `searchEditions { data { … } }` shape does not exist. Also: `headline` exists but is just the player name — **NOT** the moment page's editorial title ("…stuffs stat sheet in historic 5x5"), whose source is still unlocated — and sibling fields return **SENTINELS not nulls** (`draftYear: 0`, `draftRound: "N/A"`, `quarter: "NA"`), so `lib/topshot/play-description.ts` exports `isSentinel()` to encode that.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the sandbox cannot reach the collectible upstreams AT ALL** (measured: `curl` to both returns `status=000`) and holds no secrets, so GraphQL discovery MUST run as a prod route. That is why `/api/admin/discover-moment-descriptors` exists.
- **Shipped:** `editions.description` + capture in the Top Shot catalog walker (normalized via `lib/topshot/play-description.ts`: null never empty-string, whitespace collapsed for trigram consistency, sentinel-only → null); `idx_editions_description_trgm`; `rpc_search_catalog` searching prose with a `via_prose` boost (**33ms → 23ms**, faster because the extra selective index narrows earlier); the `edition_description_coverage` view; `/api/search` `meta.coverage`/`meta.note` read LIVE; `GlobalSearch` rendering it. Coverage at ship: **5,885 / 13,197 canonical Top Shot (44.6%)**, 0 elsewhere; **76 Top Shot moments describe a game-winner**.
- **⚠ Two tests asserted copy that had become FALSE** ("Moment descriptions aren't in the catalog") and were rewritten, not deleted — a test pinning outdated copy makes the copy look like the contract. Also fixed `meta` missing from `GlobalSearch`'s `useMemo` deps, which would have stopped the panel re-rendering when coverage arrived.
- **⚠ `/api/admin/backfill-topshot-catalog` is on NO cron and a naive Vercel cron would 401 every tick** — `verifyAdminRequest` accepts ONLY `RPC_ADMIN_TOKEN`, while Vercel cron sends `CRON_SECRET`. Same trap that killed the `pinnacle-sync` entry. Scheduling it needs a `/api/cron/*` wrapper that checks `CRON_SECRET`, not a `vercel.json` line. Until then description coverage only grows when someone runs it by hand. ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-13 (`7a8d7f2a`): the wrapper was built and it IS on a cron now** — `/api/cron/topshot-catalog-backfill` at `12 2 * * *`. The diagnosis above was right; only the "until then" is stale. Current detail lives in the `editions.description` bullet under the editions-table section.

No DB, migration, cron, auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or FMV/pricing-MATH change.
