- **Ship 1 — honest degradation on the 6 remaining single-view boards** (squeeze · trophies · offer-spread · pinnacle-scarcity · allday-scarcity · set-squeeze). `lib/insights/board-status.ts` was built 2026-08-09 for one named failure and wired into only **2** of 8 boards; the other six still had the exact `if (error) return []` the module's own header names, so a 57014 rendered as an EMPTY board at HTTP 200. ⚠ **Invisible to both existing instruments**: it is a 200 (5xx metrics blind) and the board-liveness probe times `SELECT count(*)`, which the planner prunes.
- **Ship 2 — the D3 driver-message leak was SURFACE-WIDE: all 29 `app/api/public/insights/**` routes, in THREE spellings** (see the `lib/insights/board-error.ts` bullet under "Key files"). The filed follow-up had scoped it to six. `pack-reality` additionally published a per-source `errors[]` of driver text into `meta.errors` on its **200** — its only consumer reads `e.source` and never the message, so the leak bought nothing.
- **⚠ DURABLE — a pre-authorized deletion whose PREMISE WAS FALSE.** `app/api/topshot-listing-cache/route.ts` carried a 2026-06-07 note: the wallet-verification matcher "is effectively a no-op — cached_listings is frozen (Flowty shut 2026-05-13)… safe to delete." Measured live: `cached_listings` holds **309 rows, newest `cached_at` minutes old** — the Flowty *frontend* shut, `api2.flowty.io` is alive, and **this very route writes that table**. Deleting on that note would have dropped a working backstop. It has never fired for a *different* reason (all 9 challenges resolved `expired`/`gql_on_demand` — the on-demand GQL route gets there first). Comment corrected to KEEP. **Re-measure a "this is dead" annotation before acting on it; the annotation is not the evidence.**
- **⚠ DURABLE — `workers/rpc-mcp-proxy` was telling every LLM agent that a WORKING tool is broken.** `get_badge_data`'s description claimed the backing RPC "currently raises an `unaccent` error… returns badges:{}". Verified live: `get_edition_badges_unified` carries `search_path=public, extensions, pg_temp` and a real call returns `gaps: []` with actual badges. Tool descriptions are agent-facing config — stale text there actively routes agents away from working tools. ⚠ Committed but **INERT until an operator `wrangler deploy`**.
- **Smaller corrections, each verified not assumed:** `backfill-badges-from-sets` reported `matchedMissing: wanted.size` (keys *sought*) while the real count was dropped with `void matched`, so a dry run could never show a set matched **nothing**; `/api/fmv` carried a provably-unreachable `if (fallbackTier !== "rpc_fmv")` in both handlers (API contract left untouched, 87 tests green); `RTRClient`'s "stub until the Odds API is wired" was stale; one genuinely-unused import kept alive by `void`.
- **⚠ Method notes.** `main` was **CI-red on arrival** (`edge-fn-no-hardcoded-gate-keys` failing on clean HEAD — proven by stashing my diff); I fixed it by widening the regex, then a concurrent session landed a **strictly better behavioural fix** (it EXECUTES the real `gateKeyOk` under set/unset secrets and pins `executed === 8`) and I **dropped mine and took theirs**. Every new test here is **mutation-proven** — reverted the fix and confirmed the test reds — because the repo's own lesson is that a test which cannot fail asserts nothing. ⚠ **`io.open(p,"w")` in Python truncates the file BEFORE the argument expression is evaluated**, so a `write(transform(...))` whose transform raises leaves an EMPTY file (it silently emptied a route; restored from git). Compute every transform first, then write. ⚠ Run the two coverage gates into **separate `--coverage.reportsDirectory` dirs** — a stray concurrent run collided on `coverage/` and failed with a bogus `ENOENT coverage/.tmp/*.json` that reads like a real break.


### August 11, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — assessed the filed candy-treasury finding → "work through the rest of the tree, leave it clean") — **found and fixed a 24h silent ingest outage that five consecutive monitor runs had misdiagnosed.** 7 pg_cron jobs repointed + 2 prod migrations, each verified live and ledgered. One regression introduced and closed in the same session. No hot-wallet, auth/`proxy.ts`, FMV/pricing-MATH, or destructive-SQL change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — `cron.job_run_details` "succeeded" on a `net.http_get` means DISPATCHED, not executed; and a 403'd edge fn writes NO `pipeline_runs` row, making it INDISTINGUISHABLE from a completed no-op walk.** The 2026-08-09 D2 change de-hardcoded 8 edge-fn gate keys to env vars and warned in its own ledger entry: *"Do NOT clear it by redeploying without first setting the secrets, or 9 pg_cron jobs (15, 16, 20, 42, 44, 55, 56, 83, 84) start 403ing."* On 08-11 ~03:09–03:22Z the pg_cron `?key=` values were rotated but the fns were never redeployed and the secrets never set — **half a rotation**. `ingest-allday-pack-opens`, `ingest-pinnacle-mints` and `ingest-topshot-pack-opens-history` all 403'd for 24h. **Real loss:** AllDay pack opens (the forward walk wrote 8 rows on its first repaired run) and Pinnacle mints (~17 rows/tick forward, 8–35 per 2 min backfill).
- **⚠ DURABLE — the tell was a dispatch-layer success plus a silent pipeline; that is an AUTH hypothesis BEFORE it is an upstream hypothesis.** Settled with two `mode=probe` (read-only) calls to the SAME live function: the cron's rotated hex → **403**, the deployed gate literal → **200 OK**, scanning to tip `161083198` and resolving a live pack open. **Flow/spork reachability was perfect the whole time** — the spork-proxy / mainnet24 theory in the `0610Z`/`1512Z`/`1809Z`/`2112Z`/`0309Z` monitor files is wrong; do not re-open that lane.
- **⚠ DURABLE — a real alarm was suppressed by a stale "known false positive" annotation.** `detect_stalled_pipelines()` DID fire and was dismissed five times as *"documented no-op-walk false positive."* That annotation is real but belongs to the **TopShot** walk (cursor 61,808,846, genuinely below the 65,264,619 spork floor → `done:true`); it was **misapplied to AllDay**, whose cursor at 85,940,403 sits ~20.7M blocks ABOVE the floor, mid-walk. The 0309Z run even cited `cron.job_run_details` as its verification — the one instrument structurally blind to this. **A benign annotation on a shared symptom is a diagnostic hazard: scope it to the pipeline it was measured on.**
- **Shipped the missing instrument.** Nothing in the estate read `net._http_response` — 100 of 194 responses were 403 and no check looked. New `check_edge_fn_http_failures()` flags 4xx on pg_net-dispatched edge fns, wired into `get_pipeline_alerts()` (the live Telegram/email path) by **RENAME + thin wrapper** (`get_pipeline_alerts_core`) so its load-bearing 11,383-char body was never retyped. **Scoped to 4xx only** — 5xx and pg_net's 55s timeouts are routine noise and would have made it permanently red. It fired on the live outage (109 × 403, `critical`) and reads `[]` now that all 7 jobs are fixed. ⚠ `net._http_response` retains only ~1.6h and `net.http_request_queue` is **pruned on completion**, so a response cannot be joined back to its URL — status code + body are the only attribution available.
- **⚠ DURABLE — `REVOKE … FROM PUBLIC` is NOT enough, and it bit me.** `CREATE OR REPLACE` on the name freed by the RENAME picked up Supabase's default `anon`/`authenticated` EXECUTE grants, widening `get_pipeline_alerts()` from its original postgres+service_role-only ACL. Caught on the same-session verification pass and closed in a second migration with `FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated`. **Always re-check `has_function_privilege('anon', …)` after a RENAME + recreate — the new object does not inherit the old ACL.**
- **Candy treasury finding closed, with its key number corrected.** Both argmaxes re-verified in agreement (`BhA2Bfd8…APe2`; wmc 2,610 vs 1,367 = 1.9×, packs 2,243 vs 21 = 107×). ⚠ **The filed "76.8 ms" is wrong — `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` cold is 2,854 ms**, which inverts the doc's own conclusion and rules out a live trust arm. `check_candy_treasury_divergence()` is therefore **standalone**, deliberately NOT in `get_pipeline_alerts()` (45s budget, already taken down once by one heavy arm). **Board stays at 38 arms.**
- **Also corrected:** the `1512Z` claim that the D34 precompute needs "per-leg budgets below the outer 600s" — **every per-leg budget is already below 600s** (60/90/120/180/180/240/300/480). The `CALL` is one statement and `statement_timeout` does not re-arm per `COMMIT`, so `cron_heavy`'s role-level 600s bounds the TOTAL; the split worked (committed legs persisted, only the two expensive tail legs were lost — which IS the separately-reported `public_board_slow_count` freeze). Not a live defect (18:58Z 494.7s, 00:58Z 73.6s both succeeded); nothing changed.
- **⚠ STILL OWED (operator).** The 7 jobs now use the PRE-rotation literals, which are **public** (in this public repo's git history — exactly why D2 rotated them). No NEW exposure (the deployed fns already accept them), but the rotation must be completed as ONE window: set the 8 `*_GATE_KEY` secrets → deploy the repo (env-var) fns → repoint the pg_cron `?key=` together. Any subset reproduces this outage. **Not fixed, different cause:** jobid 44 `rpc-compute-golazos-pack-ev` has a literal key yet `compute-laliga-pack-ev` is silent ~22h.

### August 11, 2026 (Claude Code, docs — "analyze repo → update CLAUDE.md to current state") — docs-only maintenance on tip `d410be5e`: independently re-verified every checkable canonical-reference fact against the repo (all CURRENT, no factual correction due) and made **three genuine ADDITIONS** — a whole new public product surface that had landed from a concurrent session and was documented nowhere in the canonical sections. No product/DB/prod change; direct to `main`. Ledger entry 2026-08-11.

- **Added the global catalog search to Route structure + Selected API endpoints.** `/api/search` + `rpc_search_catalog` + `components/search/GlobalSearch.tsx` (in `GlobalSiteHeader`) shipped in `98a9d4a2` — **the site's first real search**; everything prior searched ONE column with an unindexed `ilike`. Recorded the three durable constraints a future editor would otherwise re-break: it MUST apply the **canonical-edition predicate** (TS moments are stored under two key conventions, so without it every TS hit doubles — Lillard 95 vs the canonical 65), it MUST join `AND c.is_active` (unpublished collections have no routes → hits 404 on click), and it MUST **503 rather than 200-with-`results:[]`** (an empty array is byte-identical to "nothing matched", so an outage would render as "we have no such moment"). Also recorded that the catalog holds **no descriptive prose**, so narrative queries correctly return zero and the copy says so — do not "fix" that by implying coverage the data lacks.
- **Added both new `proxy.ts` public-path carve-outs** (the security-relevant one) — `/api/search` and GET `/api/profile/follows`, both GET/HEAD-only EXACT-path (no subtree). The prose now carries the ⚠ that made the follows carve-out safe: **`isPublicPath` never sees a query string**, so BOTH the `?username=` probe and the bare listing form bypass the proxy, and the route's own `requireUser()` is what protects the listing — keep that guard when editing the route. POST/DELETE writers stay gated.
- **Added the Follow button** to the `/profile/[username]` bullet (`c91a5aea`) — the follows table, CRUD route and dashboard friend-activity feed had existed since Phase 4 with **zero callers**, so follows sat at 0 rows and the feed showed its empty state permanently.
- **All 13 canonical reference facts re-verified current on this tip, no edit due:** 37 Vercel crons · 122 DB-invariant pins (raw `grep -cE 'fn:'` = 123, one two-fn pin counted twice) · both launch flags (`CANDY_MLB_PUBLIC` / `PANINI_PUBLIC`) `true` · 8 collections defined / 5 published (the interface's own field decls inflate a naïve grep to 9/6) · 17 workers · 20 GHA workflows (17 scheduled / 3 unscheduled) · 8 CI jobs · concierge **29 tools** on `claude-sonnet-4-6` · primary coverage thresholds 89.3/75.1/91.5/91.6 · component thresholds 79.0/67.0/78.8/83.2 (the search commit added `components/search/**` to the gate's `include` allowlist but did NOT move the thresholds).

The two entries are complementary, not duplicates: **theirs is the shipping log, this one is the docs-maintenance log**, and the durable value here is that the facts were moved into the CANONICAL REFERENCE sections (Route structure, Selected API endpoints, the `proxy.ts` bypass set, the `fmv_snapshots` schema bullet). ### August 11, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive, cont. — Trevor sent Top Shot moment-page screenshots: "this is the type of additional descriptors I'm hoping we can pull") — **the encyclopedia gap is REAL but SMALLER than this session first concluded, and one instance of it was a defect, not a gap.** 2 commits (`f3480a43` region), 1 prod migration. No hot-wallet, auth, or destructive-SQL change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — we have been FETCHING descriptive prose and throwing it away.** `ALLDAY_RELAY_QUERY` in [lib/editions-hydrate.ts](lib/editions-hydrate.ts) has selected **`play { description }` since the query was written**, but `AllDayEditionMeta` never carried the field, so every AllDay hydrate discarded the only descriptive text any of our ingests receives. Now captured through `hydrateAllDayEditions` → `toUpsertRow`. ⚠ **Clobber-safety is the subtle part:** `toUpsertRow` spreads the row straight into the upsert, so a present-but-`undefined` `description` key would write NULL over a good value on every Top Shot hydrate and on every AllDay row with no prose — the key must be **omitted entirely**, and `HydratedEditionRow.description` is OPTIONAL so `emptyRow` and the Top Shot path never emit it. Same class as the `save_user_wallet` COALESCE-never-null-out rule.
- **⚠ `editions.description` now exists (nullable, catalog-only ALTER) but is DELIBERATELY NOT SEARCHED.** `rpc_search_catalog`'s edition arm is index-bound; an un-indexed `description ILIKE '%q%'` OR forces a seq scan of all ~27k editions on EVERY query and would undo the **162ms → 33ms** the `idx_editions_team_name_trgm` index bought. Correct order: **populate → measure coverage → trigram index → then extend the arm.** Wiring it at 100% NULL buys zero recall for real latency. Do not "finish the job" by adding the predicate before the data is there.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the sandbox CANNOT reach the collectible upstreams at all.** Measured 2026-08-11: `curl` to `public-api.nbatopshot.com` and `public-api.nflallday.com` both return **`status=000`** (connection refused by the agent proxy), and `TS_PROXY_SECRET` / `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN` / `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are all **absent** from the sandbox env. So GraphQL schema discovery CANNOT be done from a dev session — it has to run where the secret lives. Same class as the documented `supabase.co` (403 CONNECT) and `jsr.io` blocks.
- **Shipped the probe rather than guessing: `/api/admin/discover-moment-descriptors`** (admin-gated POST, read-only, writes nothing). Introspection first; falls back to **FIELD PROBING** — ask for one candidate field and read the error, since GraphQL answers `Cannot query field X on type Y` for an unknown field, so the error text IS the schema. Walks Top Shot `PlayStats`/`Play` (headline / prose / box-score / bio candidates drawn from what the moment page visibly renders) and AllDay `Play`. It returns **sample values, not just field names**, because "field missing" and "field exists but empty" imply opposite next steps. **Operator action — one call:** `curl -X POST .../api/admin/discover-moment-descriptors -H "Authorization: Bearer $RPC_ADMIN_TOKEN"`.
- **What the screenshots establish:** the Top Shot moment page renders a **headline** ("Victor Wembanyama stuffs stat sheet in historic 5x5 vs Lakers"), a **prose paragraph**, and a **full per-moment box score** (MIN/PTS/REB/AST/STL/BLK/TOV/+-, FG, 3P, FT + quarter scores) plus player bio (height/weight/hometown/draft). Our Top Shot ingest selects only `play { stats { playerName, playCategory, playType, dateOfMoment, teamAtMoment, … } }` — no descriptive field at all. So the earlier session conclusion "the catalog holds no descriptive prose" is correct about OUR DB but must NOT be read as "the data is unobtainable upstream".


### August 11, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "some ideas I've been thinking of" → assessment → "Do all you can") — **shipped 3 of the 7 ideas end-to-end: the follow button, the site's first catalog SEARCH, and long-horizon price charts.** 8 commits to `main` (`84710248` → `9d6e2408`), 5 prod migrations + 1 CONCURRENTLY index, each verified live + ledgered with a revert path. No hot-wallet, FMV/pricing-MATH, or destructive-SQL change.

- **The encyclopedia idea is DATA-blocked, not search-blocked — measure before building.** `editions` has **no `description` column**; `editions.name` is literally `"Damian Lillard — Base Set"`; `badges`/`reward_indicators` are **empty on every row of all 5 collections**; `play_type`/`play_category` are shot mechanics (Rim, 3 Pointer, Dunk). So **"Damian Lillard Game Winners" is unanswerable** — the concept exists nowhere in the data. The richest untapped corpus is `badge_editions.play_tags` (Tomahawk Dunk, Alley-oop, Hot Streak, MVP Year, Hall of Fame) which `app/api/badges/route.ts:138-152` deliberately DISCARDS — but it covers only **3,839 of 19,735** Top Shot editions (**19%**), so a search built on it would silently answer for a fifth of the catalog. Catalog scale is tiny (27,143 editions / 3,841 players / 914 sets / 171 teams) — search was never an infra problem.
- **⚠ DURABLE — `editions` stores Top Shot moments under TWO key conventions for the SAME moment** (int `setID:playID` **9,436** rows vs UUID-pair **10,299**), and the UUID twin carries no thumbnail and **no sales**. Any new surface reading `editions` un-filtered returns every Top Shot result TWICE with ~doubled counts (Lillard read 95; canonical **65**). Use the platform's existing predicate — Top Shot rows must match `'^[0-9]+:[0-9]+(::[0-9]+)?$'` — the same one `rpc_fmv_confidence_share` and the trust-health precompute legs use. Sales attach ONLY to the canonical rows, so sale-derived history needs no such filter.
- **⚠ DURABLE — `fmv_snapshots` begins 2026-03-31 (~4.5 months); `sales` begins 2020-07-28 (3.11M TS rows).** So the edition chart's "365d" chip had NEVER shown a year. Any 1Y/all-time price view must be sale-derived. New `get_edition_sale_history` returns median (not mean — the Sept-2020 `5:145` bucket is median $50 vs high $800) with **grain returned per row** (day/week/month) so the axis label is measured, not assumed.
- **⚠ DURABLE — a plpgsql `RETURN QUERY` type-checks on EXECUTION, not at CREATE.** `rpc_search_catalog` applied cleanly and only failed when first CALLED (`42804`, `collections.slug` is `varchar(50)` not `text`). **Applying a migration is not verification — you must call the function.** Also: `count(*) FROM check_secdef_anon_exec_drift()` reads **1 when clean** (it returns one row containing an empty array) — read the contents, not the count.
- **Shipped.** (1) **Follow button** — the `follows` table, its full CRUD route, and the dashboard friend-activity feed had existed since Phase 4 with **zero callers** (grep returned only the route file), so it sat at 0 rows behind a permanent empty state. Added `FollowButton` + a single-edge `?username=` probe; then found by probing PROD that `/api/profile/follows` was proxy-gated, so anon got 50KB of login HTML instead of the `{authed:false}` the route was written to return (the button survived via its catch — a waste/honesty fix, not a broken UI). (2) **`/api/search` + `rpc_search_catalog` + a header search bar** — player/set/team/edition, multi-token (longest token anchors an index-backed ILIKE, rest via `LIKE ALL`), entity arms resolve from the table the PAGE resolves from so a hit always has a working route, zero-edition rows dropped, `is_active` gate (candy/panini links would 404). **One new index** `idx_editions_team_name_trgm` (CONCURRENTLY, out of band): **162ms → 33ms**. (3) **1Y/ALL sale-print charts** + fixed a pre-existing bug where a FAILED fetch rendered "too few sales to chart" — a claim about the market, from a network blip.
- **Honesty constraints are structural, not decoration:** `/api/search` returns **503 on failure, never 200-with-empty** (indistinguishable from "nothing matched"); `meta.note` and the empty state both state that narrative queries can't match; the FMV and sale-print series are **never merged into one line** (different quantities). Verified: primary gate **1117 files / 10496 tests**, component gate **170 / 1342**, `tsc` clean, security invariants 0, secdef drift 0, anon-write 0; search confirmed live in prod anonymously.
- **Left for Trevor (exposure decisions, deliberately not taken):** there is still no collector DIRECTORY, so following requires already knowing a username — a public `/collectors` index is a new public surface. And `GlobalSiteHeader` is only rendered by the `(collections)` layout, so the search bar is absent on `/dashboard`, `/insights`, `/analytics` and `/`; unifying those headers is a separate layout change.

On an **idle** DB the inherited 120s cap is ample: a 270MB / 955k-row partial covering index built in **35.8s**. Schedule at a **specific minute** (`24 2 * * *`), not `* * * * *`, so it fires ONCE (next occurrence 24h out — no double-fire); `cron.unschedule` by JOBNAME only AFTER the build completes (unscheduling a RUNNING pg_cron job KILLS it).
- **⚠ DURABLE — the Small-compute instance can RESTART under sustained IO saturation.** Mid-session the DB returned `57P03 "not accepting connections / hot standby disabled"` for ~1-2 min after a multi-hour IO-depletion spell (a 256s stuck autovacuum on `wallet_moments_cache` + rolling `fmv_snapshots` reads). **Build prod DDL ONLY in a genuinely quiet window** — gated on `datafileread_waiters<=2 AND long_running_gt10s<=1 AND io_waiters<=4 AND 0 idle-in-txn` and polled via `send_later` across ~40 min until it cleared. `hypopg` is available (not installed) for hypothetical-index proofs, but its hypothetical index + the `EXPLAIN` must run in ONE MCP call (each MCP `execute_sql` is a fresh session; hypothetical indexes are session-scoped).

### August 10, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "implement a TODO" → drained the deep-audit register → "keep going"×N → shipped the D34 prerequisite → wrap-up) — **SPLIT the load-bearing precompute + shipped the D34 arm it unblocked.** ~9 DB/docs commits to `main` (`0a0458da` region → `61bb92aa`), each verified live + ledgered with a revert path. One defect introduced and caught from its own first tick. No hot-wallet, auth/`proxy.ts`, FMV/pricing-MATH, or destructive-SQL change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh` is now SPLIT; this SUPERSEDES the frozen "single 569s function / only 3 EXCEPTION handlers / a leg timeout rolls back all 18 metrics" claims in the Aug-8/9 dated entries below (and register D34).** It is now **8 per-leg SECDEF functions `rpc_thp_leg_*()`** (each own `statement_timeout` + `EXCEPTION→999`) driven by a thin **INVOKER procedure `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh_p()`** that `COMMIT`s between legs; **pg_cron jobid 287** (`58 */6`) `CALL`s it. A saturated-tick kill now loses ONE leg (a loud 999 for its own arm), never the whole board. Monolith `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh()` left defined (unscheduled) as the one-line revert. Migrations M1 `20260810225549` (legs) → M2 `20260810230106` (orchestrator) → M3 `20260810230704`+cron cutover → M3b `20260811010305` (grant fix) → D34 `20260811012334` (8th leg). Plan+§5 in [docs/audits/precompute-split-plan-2026-08-10.md](docs/audits/precompute-split-plan-2026-08-10.md).
- **⚠ DURABLE — a `cron_heavy`-owned pg_cron job needs EXECUTE granted to `cron_heavy`, and an INVOKER orchestrator needs it on EVERY function it PERFORMs (EXECUTE is required to CALL a fn even when it is `SECURITY DEFINER`).** The split's first natural tick (00:58Z, jobid 287) **FAILED `permission denied for procedure`** because M1/M2 revoked the new objects to postgres+service_role only — but job 287 runs as `cron_heavy` (its owner), and the retired monolith carried an explicit `cron_heavy=X` grant (that is why it ran for months). Fixed in M3b. **Check `has_function_privilege('cron_heavy', oid, 'EXECUTE')` before cutting a cron over to new objects** — the M1/M2 revoke-to-service_role pattern (correct for SECDEF fns only reached by `supabaseAdmin`) is WRONG for a fn a `cron_heavy` job calls.
- **⚠ DURABLE — cron cutover privilege recipe.** `apply_migration` can't touch `cron.job` (`42501`) — do DDL via migration, the cron via `execute_sql`. And even as `postgres`, **`cron.unschedule(bigint jobid)` and `cron.alter_job(...)` both `42501` permission-denied**; only **`cron.unschedule(text jobname)` works, and only when run as the job's OWNER role** (the jobname overload silently scopes to the current role's jobs). For a `cron_heavy`-owned job: `SET ROLE cron_heavy; SELECT cron.unschedule('<name>'); SELECT cron.schedule('<name>', '<sched>', $$CALL …$$);` in `execute_sql`. `CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE/FUNCTION` PRESERVES existing grants (the M3b cron_heavy grant survived the D34 orchestrator replace).
- **⚠ DURABLE — a procedure that does `COMMIT` must be INVOKER-rights, NO `SET` clause, single-statement `CALL` (schema-qualify everything); a SECDEF proc or a SET clause → `2D000 invalid transaction termination`. `statement_timeout` does NOT re-arm per `COMMIT`, so the per-leg budget lives on the SECDEF leg FUNCTIONS' `SET` clauses, never the procedure.** (Same family as the `reconcile_all_saved_wallet_stats` finding.) Validated the whole mechanism with throwaway one-off pg_cron jobs (schedule → observe `cron.job_run_details` → unschedule).
- **⚠ DURABLE — set a per-leg budget ABOVE the leg's SATURATED cost, not its healthy cost.** M3a raised the `impossible_parallel` leg 300s→480s because in the cheapest-first order it runs LAST (most connection-contended). Vindicated live: a full 8-leg one-off run under heavy saturation took **319s total with the impossible_parallel leg at 289s** — a 300s cap would have written a false 999 and PAGED (breach_at 3). EXPLAIN-characterized §5: that leg is index-optimal; its cost is ~16k cross-partition `edition_id` probes + a heap fetch per matching sale to test `serial_number > circulation_count` (no sales index carries `serial_number`) — the two real fixes (covering `(edition_id, serial_number)` index / recent-`ingested_at` window) are both gated (operator / owner-semantic).
- **D34 RESOLVED (`20260811012334`):** `rpc_thp_leg_pinnacle_fmv_share()` computes `pinnacle_fmv_high_med_share_pct` (**28.7** live) from `pinnacle_fmv_history` (DISTINCT ON render_id over the `(render_id, computed_at)` PK, ~0.7–1.5s) as the 8th leg. **TRACK-only like the other 5 `*_fmv_high_med_share_pct` metrics — NOT a `v_rpc_trust_health` arm (board stays 38); a thin market is a fact, not an alert.** Read via `rpc_fmv_confidence_share()`/dashboards straight from `rpc_trust_health_precompute`.
- **Also shipped this thread:** pruned 2 orphaned `public_board_liveness_state` rows (47→45, `20260810190101`); characterized D37 (AllDay `unmapped_sales` backlog — 63% is correctly-parked price-0 rows, no cheap fix). **Verified benign:** a concurrent session's board-liveness decouple (`public_board_liveness_probe()` is now a cheap state read + a separate `public_board_liveness_sweep()` on jobid 288 `28 */6`) makes the split's board-liveness leg ~86s→~0ms — de-risked before the tick.

### August 10, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — assessed a filed Cowork finding, then "keep going / do all you can / clean tree") — **refuted the finding's conclusion with a live re-measure, then shipped the fix it said was unnecessary.** 1 prod migration + 3 docs commits, all pushed, ledgered with a revert path. No hot-wallet, auth/`proxy.ts`, FMV/pricing-MATH, or destructive-SQL change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — you CANNOT time a query by wrapping it in `clock_timestamp()` CTEs. The planner evaluates them independently of the work.** A filed finding declared `public_board_slow_count` a false alarm on the strength of "I re-measured `allday_scarcity_board` live: **4,044 ms**, 49% of budget." I reproduced that method (`MATERIALIZED` CTEs bracketing a `count(*)`) and it returned **0 ms** for the same 6,190-row board — impossible, and the same class of artifact that produced the 4,044. **`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE)` is the only trustworthy timing here.** By that measure the board was **32,809 ms cold / 38,574 ms warm** against an 8,300 ms budget, and `topshot_first_mint_trophy_stats` **17,308 ms** vs 5,400 — **both WORSE than the "stale" snapshot the finding was dismissing.** Warm being *slower* than cold also rules out cold-cache. The arm was a **TRUE positive**; acting on the finding would have dismissed a real breach on a public board and its ⛔ "don't re-tune `max_ms`" would have been kept for exactly the wrong reason.
- **⚠ DURABLE — a `count(*)` health probe can be silently PRUNED into meaninglessness, and my own fix did it.** `public_board_liveness_probe` times `SELECT count(*) FROM <view>`. The board's old `LEFT JOIN LATERAL (… ORDER BY computed_at DESC LIMIT 1)` was **not provably unique, so it survived into the plan and `count(*)` measured the FMV work by accident.** Replacing it with `DISTINCT ON (edition_id)` **proves** `edition_id` unique → PG **removes the LEFT JOIN entirely** for `count(*)`. Net: probe reads **2,377 ms** while the page pays **15,172 ms** — the arm will read **GREEN for a board still ~183% over budget**. Users got a 2.4× faster board and the instrument watching it went blind in the same commit. This is the recorded *"the probe times `count(*)`, which the planner prunes"* caveat graduating into an active false-negative. **Do not treat this board's arm as authoritative until the probe is fixed** (queued, `docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-10T1900Z-*.md`, with the candidate one-line fix AND the slow-vs-empty conflation that must be fixed alongside it — an errored probe counts as EMPTY, and at 15.2s this board would exceed its `clamp(max_ms*1.5)` = 12,450 ms cap and mislabel as "renders blank").
- **The ship (`931a52db`, migration `20260810185031`):** the LATERAL ran once PER EDITION — 6,190 random probes into partitioned `fmv_snapshots` (~5.2 ms each, ~2,950 heap fetches). One `DISTINCT ON` CTE filtered on `collection_id` rides the existing `fmv_snapshots_<year>_collection_id_edition_id_computed_at_idx` (Merge Append → Unique, no sort). **33–38 s → 15.2 s.** Verified **output-identical BEFORE applying**: `EXCEPT` diff in BOTH directions = 0 rows over all 6,190 editions, and **0 editions tie at `max(computed_at)`** so neither form is order-dependent. Post-apply 6,190 rows / 5,210 priced, `security_invoker=on` and all three grants unchanged, `check_public_security_invariants()` 0, route test 3/3, live page 200 with **142,881 bytes of real DOM**. ⚠ **Deliberately reported as PARTIAL** — still 183% of budget; the durable fix is the shared materialize-latest-FMV item, which would also close `/api/market` and `cross_collection_deals_board`.
- **⚠ The finding was wrong on two more points, both checked rather than assumed.** (1) *"It does not use the existing 999 sentinel"* is **FALSE** — the board arms are precomputed rows and `v_rpc_trust_health`'s `pre` CTE **already** maps `computed_at < now()-24h` → 999. The guard exists at 24 h; the snapshot was 11.2 h old, so it simply had not tripped. The proposed fix is a **tightening 24h→8h**, not a missing guard. (2) `trust_precompute_max_age_hours` (breach 13) fires **earlier** than that 999 mapping, so precompute staleness was already attributable — it tracked correctly all session (9.98 → 11.97).
- **Missed by the original sweep: 2 orphaned rows.** `public_board_liveness_state` holds **47** rows against **45** active watchlist entries — `candy_deals_board` and `topshot_underpriced_serials_board` are `is_active=false` with rows frozen at **2026-08-02 (208 h)**. They do **NOT** inflate any arm (the probe derives `n_slow`/`n_empty` from its own loop over active rows, never from the table), so it is a hand-reading hazard only — and it is what made the original table read confusing. Left in place deliberately; cleanup buys no signal.
- **Method:** the mechanism half of the finding was fully CONFIRMED (single caller `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh`, jobid 222 killed at 600.0 s on the 12:58Z tick two days running, probe writes rolled back with it) — **a correct mechanism does not make the conclusion correct.** Corrected the filed inbox artifact in place rather than writing a new one, keeping the original text struck-through so the record shows what was claimed. Ledger discipline held: re-read fresh, `grep -c '^### '` 1185 → 1186 (+1 exactly), line 9 intact — and the first splice landed my entry BELOW its same-day sibling, so it was redone against `head -9` to keep newest-first.

### August 10, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "work through everything pending in the tree to ensure we have a clean tree afterwards. Address any issues you encounter") — landed **three nights of NO-PUSH overnight output** and fixed the one job in the estate that had *never once succeeded*: `rpc-reconcile-saved-wallet-stats` (pg_cron jobid 259). 2 prod migrations, both verified live end-to-end, each ledgered with a revert path. No hot-wallet, auth/`proxy.ts`, FMV/pricing-MATH, or destructive-SQL change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — a PL/pgSQL procedure that does transaction control can be NEITHER `SECURITY DEFINER` NOR carry a `SET` clause.** Both put the call in an atomic context and `COMMIT` fails `2D000 invalid transaction termination`. Measured with three one-minute throwaway pg_cron probes: SECDEF-without-SET **FAILED**, invoker-with-`SET search_path` **FAILED**, plain invoker **SUCCEEDED**. This matters because the queued profile doc explicitly prescribed keeping both ("FUNCTION→PROCEDURE is an object change: grants reset — re-`REVOKE`/`GRANT` in the same migration"), which would have shipped a job that `2D000`s on its FIRST tick — strictly worse than the timeout it replaces. So the procedure is invoker-rights with no `SET` clause and **schema-qualifies every reference** instead; a runtime `SET search_path` was also rejected because it would persist on a pooled connection after the call returns. Privilege is unchanged in practice (pg_cron runs it as `postgres`, only other grantee is `service_role`) and the actual column writes still go through the SECDEF `aggregate_saved_wallet_stats` / `log_pipeline_run`.
- **⚠ DURABLE — `statement_timeout` does NOT re-arm per `COMMIT`.** The doc's stated mechanism — "the timer re-arms after each COMMIT, so it effectively gets the budget *per wallet*" — is false. A probe set the session GUC to 2500 ms then ran three 3.0 s legs; **all three survived**, each reporting `statement_timeout = 2500ms` at its own commit boundary. Only the timer armed at top-level statement start counts (the same reason a function-level `SET statement_timeout` is inert here). A per-wallet `COMMIT` therefore buys **durable partial progress**, not a per-wallet budget — the soft deadline is what bounds the run. Related: the cron command **must be a single statement**, since `SET ...; CALL ...` is an implicit transaction block and the COMMIT fails 2D000; the `SET statement_timeout='300s';` prefix added on 08-09 is removed.
- **⚠ DURABLE — `saved_wallets.cache_updated_at` is a LAST-CHANGED stamp, not a last-verified stamp, and every staleness figure filed against this job has been measuring the guard.** Live: 21 of 99 rows read older than 12 h — and **all 21 are (wallet, collection) pairs holding nothing whose three `cached_*` columns are already correctly `(0, NULL, NULL)`. Zero rows are wrong on screen.** Pass 2 deliberately skips a row that already holds the right values, so its stamp can never advance. The inbox doc's "p50 age 9.4 h / max 33.5 h / 21 rows past 12 h" is that artifact — do NOT re-file it as a data-quality defect.
- **⚠ The fix's own new telemetry caught a defect I had just introduced, on its first run.** It committed **4 of 21 wallets / 13 rows in 106.5 s** and logged `truncated: true` — the first progress this job has ever made (2 of 2 prior scheduled attempts committed *nothing*: killed at 120.0 s, then at 300.4 s after the budget raise). That immediately exposed two things: (a) stalest-first was ordering on those *perpetually-old zero-holding rows*, so it spent the whole budget on wallets chosen by a timestamp that can never move and would pick the same ones on every future run — **a livelock dressed as a priority queue**; and (b) `IF NOT v_truncated` gated the cheap, always-correct pass 2 behind completion of the expensive pass 1, so the one genuinely-wrong-on-screen case (a wallet that sold out of a collection keeps showing its old count forever) was skipped in exactly the saturated conditions that cause it. Second migration runs **pass 2 first and ungated**, restricts pass 1's list *and* ordering to pairs that actually hold moments, and adds a 6 h freshness skip.
- **Also measured:** the soft deadline is checked BETWEEN wallets only and **cannot preempt a single long wallet** — one wallet exceeded 100 s in the 16:00Z saturation window (the same set takes 37 s for all 21 on a quiet instance). A kill then costs only that wallet. And `cron.unschedule` **cancels a RUNNING job** — it killed one of my verification runs mid-flight at 29.0 s.
- **NOT done, deliberately:** no third budget raise (refuted twice); no schedule move off 13:33Z (that slot is not structurally congested — jobid 215 exceeds 3 min on only 13% of runs); no `tier` covering index on `wallet_moments_cache` (INCLUDE columns block HOT exactly like key columns on a 2.2 M-row table the wallet walks write constantly — not worth it for display-only data); `aggregate_saved_wallet_stats` untouched (still the single definition of these three columns, shared with the route); the array_agg fold declined (the doc measured it at only −21%, because removing the subplan knocks the aggregate off its covering index).
- **Tree landed:** the 08-10 nightly pass's ledger entry + `metrics-latest.json` + `docs/handoff-2026-08-10-overnight-pass.md`, the weekly `docs/health/PROJECT_HEALTH_2026-08-10.md`, and the two 08-10 inbox files, all of which had been written to the mount UNPUSHED across three consecutive shell-down nights. **Daytime-monitor `1509Z` dispositions:** its item 1 (Sentry `JAVASCRIPT-NEXTJS-26`, edition-page pool-acquire timeouts, 8 events/1 user) is saturation collateral with no new code lever, as filed; item 2 (`pinnacle-sync` missed its 10:07Z tick, last ran 08-09 10:07Z) is confirmed real but LOW — `pinnacle-catalog-backfill` ran today 09:37Z ok/2,457 rows, so catalog freshness is covered by the 08-08 Vercel backstop and Pinnacle FMV is on separate pg_cron jobs. It is an external cron-job.org tick, operator-side; not escalated on one miss.

### August 9, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — drained deep-audit addendum-2 → "clean tree afterwards + explore how to re-enable shell in Cowork") — **2 green ships to `main` (`b83435c4` docs → `ae78fb84` code)** closing D11 plus two siblings of the same class it uncovered, and a root-cause diagnosis of the wedged Cowork sandbox. Clean tree, `tsc` clean. No DB, hot-wallet, auth/`proxy.ts`, or FMV/pricing-MATH change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — a new named defect class with a standing probe: "a failed read served as HTTP 200".** A route returns `{error:"…"}` with status **200**; the caller's idiomatic `if (!res.ok) throw` passes it; the error object is stored as data; it is **truthy**, so every field falls through its `?? 0`. `catch` never runs, so the page's error banner never fires either. **A database timeout renders to users as a measurement.** Same family as D12/D3 ("failure renders as data") but a distinct mechanism — the lie is in the *status code*, not a swallowed fetch.
- **⚠ D11 was NOT AllDay-specific and NOT a data gap.** `/api/collection-stats` showed `TOTAL EDITIONS 0 / PRICED 0% / $0` for a collection with **6,190** editions; any collection whose stats RPC times out did the same, AllDay was just the one saturated during the sweep. **The page was already correct** — its null path renders an em-dash, it already had a written error banner ("Couldn't load collection stats right now") that had never once fired, and its `CollectionStats` type already declared `error?: string` that nothing checked. Only the honest status was ever missing. Fixed at the source (503 + `Retry-After` via `lib/api-error.ts`, the D3 precedent — driver message logged, never published) **and** at the consumer, so no future 200-with-error-body can resurrect it.
- **⚠ The sweep found two more, and the second is the sharpest instance yet.** `/api/platform-stats` is a byte-identical copy with no runtime consumer — a landmine awaiting its first caller, not a live defect. `/api/support-chat/search-deals` returned `{deals: []}` on failure — **byte-identical to its own genuine "no deals matched" response ten lines below** — so a caller reports "no deals found" for a database outage, a fabricated answer manufactured from a failure; it also published `error.message` (the D3 leak). It now **omits `deals` entirely** on failure, since an absent field cannot be misread as "none".
- **⚠ The tell is NOT the 200 — it is whether the failure shape is INDISTINGUISHABLE from a legitimate success.** Most 200-with-error returns here are deliberate and must not be "fixed": cron routes' `{ok:false, skipped:"throttle_error"}` (the caller is a scheduler and the real status goes to `pipeline_runs`), `bundle_not_supported` on the two pack-EV routes (a successful determination), `track-funnel` invalid-event (telemetry must not fail the client), and `wallet-cost-basis`/`wallet-hold-time` returning `rows:[]` **with an explicit `reason`** for a non-TopShot collection (an honest gap). Recorded with its probe in the register's VERIFIED-CLEAN section.
- **⚠ I claimed `/api/platform-stats` had "zero consumers" and was WRONG** — a route test existed that my grep missed, and the full suite caught the break. Corrected the in-code comment, and applied the lesson immediately by checking for a test *before* touching `search-deals`. A grep proving "no callers" does not prove "no tests".
- **Cowork shell — root-caused, and it is not fixable from this repo** ([docs/handoff-2026-08-09-cowork-shell-recovery.md](docs/handoff-2026-08-09-cowork-shell-recovery.md)). It is a disk-capacity failure on the hosted `/sessions` volume: `useradd` runs during provisioning **before any user code** and must create `/sessions/<session>/`, so at zero free bytes the shell never starts. ⚠ **The proven `/tmp` fallback (which restored full git on 08-05 and 08-07) is now structurally unreachable** — using `/tmp` requires a shell, and the failure moved upstream of the shell, which is why 08-09 failed identically on resume, create and re-resume. ⚠ **The "repo is too big" theory is disproven by measurement: a fresh clone is ~82 MB** (33.4 MB tracked per `git ls-files` + 48 MB history) — it is **`node_modules` at 811 MB**, ~10× the clone. ⚠ **Do NOT measure this with `du -sh` on a working tree — it overstates a clone by ~18×** and appears to confirm the wrong theory: plain `du` reports 1.1–1.5 GB here, but **1 GB of that is a single gitignored file** (`panini-capture.jsonl`, the residential Panini runner's local capture, `.gitignore:122`) plus 362 MB of per-worker `node_modules`, none of it tracked. Use `git ls-files`, not `du`. Inferred: each session makes a fresh clone under its own `/sessions/<name>/` home (the deploy-split rule) and any session running tests adds ~811 MB, so the checkout is ~9% of a session's footprint and the dependency install ~91% — ≈0.9 GB per session, apparently never garbage-collected. **Recovery: operator deletes old Cowork sessions** (the only step that can break the deadlock, since nothing in-sandbox can run until space exists), then verify with `df -h /sessions` + `du -sh /sessions/* | sort -h` — nobody has yet seen the actual breakdown.
- **⚠ Local-suite tally correction: the documented "3 known local-env files / 7 tests" is now stale.** `worker-test-completeness` joined the class and is **INTERMITTENT** — it passes in **3.66s alone** but times out at vitest's 5,000 ms default under full-suite contention, and it both passed and failed across runs in this one session; its walk grows with the worker count (17 dirs since `atlas-proxy`). Proven decomposition: `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN=""` clears `api-ingest-backfill`, `--testTimeout=120000` clears `worker-test-completeness`, and the irreducible remainder is exactly `worker-moments-hydrator-handler` (2) + `worker-pack-events-ingest` (3) = 5 tests. Count failing FILES against the list; do not trust a fixed total.
- **⚠ METHOD — vitest SKIPS the coverage report and threshold check entirely when any test fails**, so a red local suite yields *no* threshold signal at all rather than a passing one. To get a real ratchet reading locally you must first neutralize the local-env failures (the two env/timeout levers above, then `--exclude` the two irreducible worker files).

- **Indexer dark paths (ufc + golazos, the two lowest-branch indexers):** the Cadence-borrow-SUCCESS path (resolves a wmc-miss edition on-chain, writes `nft_edition_map` — the self-healing production path) + the V1 `cached_listings_v2` price/seller hit. ⚠ **DURABLE: golazos `runScript` decodes `JSON.parse(res.text())` (bare base64 string) while ufc reads `res.json()`** — a shared borrow fixture must return the RAW base64 string to satisfy both; a `{value:…}` object only satisfies ufc.
- **Concierge `check_wallet`:** the live-walk fallback (`source: live_walk_first_page`, asserted NOWHERE before) + username recovery via the live resolver on a cache-RPC miss. Deliberately stopped there — a status-string sweep confirmed all arm-level statuses are already covered; the rest is the ~450 deep per-tool fixtures the config flags as low-value to force.
- **Pack-EV engine extracted to a tested lib (the one deferred item, later done on request):** `computeTopPulls()` in `lib/pack-dist-odds.ts` (now in the primary gate), lifted byte-identical out of the 2,760-line, gate-unmeasured `pack/dist/[distId]/page.tsx` — owns the denominator choice (cached `totalUnopened`→full-pool weight→null, never the inflated top-50 sum, Pack B2), the `FMV × drop_weight/denom × slots` EV (reconciles with the Gross EV KPI, Pack D3), and the sort. +7 unit tests; the page now fetches rows and calls it. - **Component gate — the 8 biggest gaps driven** (via subagents, each output verified green + `tsc`-clean before commit): CollectionMomentTable (FMV-alert popover + acquisition chips), WalletProfile (position-transfers subtree), TrophyPickerModal (manual-entry tab + pin), PackSniperClient (filters/sorters/mobile — `window.matchMedia` must be stubbed in jsdom), SetsDashboard, ListingsDashboard, FastBreakClient (save() toast matrix), SqueezeBoardClient (refetch/drill-down effect), PaniniSqueezeClient (the whole coverage-banner disclosure chain — dark because `renderBoard` passes `coverage=null`), CandyBoardClient (Spread `belowAskPct` sort legs). PopularOnCollection is an async server component (can't render in jsdom) → only its `distinctSlugLinks` helper is testable.
- **Method reconfirmed:** run the two gates into SEPARATE `--coverage.reportsDirectory` dirs (they collide on `coverage/`); a jsdom-tagged `.test.ts` (not `.tsx`) lands a browser-branch test in the PRIMARY gate; `git push | tail` masks the exit code; ledger is concurrent-write-heavy (rebase-on-reject, splice the freshly-read top, `grep -c '^### '` up by exactly the delta — held across ~4 rebases here); for a test-only push commit tests first + ledger as the docs-only TIP to suppress the Vercel deploy, but for a real source change (the extraction) commit ledger FIRST so the code commit is the tip and deploys. **Left as the deliberate not-forced tail:** deep per-tool support-chat fixtures + the live Flow-GraphQL/Cadence inline route bodies (base topshot/allday indexers, fmv-recalc, ingest).

Verified TS 254 sets / AllDay 363 / UFC 256 — the last two match `analytics_sets_summary` exactly. ⚠ The intended `lower()` functional index from migration `20260704000200` **does not exist in prod** — repo↔DB drift — which is why the predicate had nothing to use. **11 more prod functions still carry it (D3b).**

Accept EITHER secret, mirroring `/api/cron/warm` (the canonical pattern: `(cronSecret && auth===Bearer cronSecret) || (ingest && auth===Bearer ingest)`). My snapshot cache stayed empty for 11 min until this was fixed.
- **⚠ DURABLE — multi-section board warm gates differ by failure semantics, and the choice is load-bearing.** `candy-mlb` (10 views via `Promise.all`) gates the warm on the **PRIMARY Market section only**, NOT all-10 — live evidence showed that under sustained saturation even single-view boards warm ~1/tick, so an all-10-healthy gate would almost never fire exactly when the board most needs a cached copy; a degraded peripheral section rides honestly in `payload.degraded`. `panini-squeeze` (PAGINATED `.range()` over `panini_squeeze_board ORDER BY fmv_usd DESC`) gates on a **COMPLETE page-set** (`ok = rows.ok && !partial`) — STRICTER — because a truncated fetch is a *corrupted ranking* (top-N rendered as the whole board), not merely short, so a partial is never cached. Both verified warming live (candy 125, panini 4,472).
- **⚠ DURABLE — `deals` + `rookies` public boards are I/O-BOUND, not bad plans; they 500/time-out first under disk-IO saturation.** Profiled (safe read-only `EXPLAIN`): `topshot_2025_rookie_index` cost **~79,595**, ~76k in ONE node — the max-mint-#1 lookup scans **all 8 `sales_20xx` partitions** filtering `serial_number=1`, and `sales_2020–2025` LACK the `idx_sales_YYYY_serial1` partial index that `sales_2026/2027` have (cost 52–248/partition vs 15). `cross_collection_deals_board` cost **~20,149** (per-edition `fmv_snapshots` probes + 8,449-row `cached_listings_v2` scan). **Ready fix queued in `docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-09T1941Z.md`**: `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY … serial1` on 2020–2025 (mirrors the proven 2026 index) — but ⚠ **CONCURRENTLY only** (historical partitions still take daily history-backfill writes), **SQL-editor/quiet-window only** (can't via MCP; and building under active saturation worsens the very throttling). deals needs a materialized latest-FMV-per-edition (the standing `/api/market` precompute item). Do NOT bump statement_timeout / upgrade tier / serialize the cron (per-view cold I/O, not contention — panini's 4,472-row fetch succeeds while deals' 37-row fails in the same tick).
- **The cache demonstrably did its job under a multi-HOUR saturation spell:** `deals`/`rookies` sat ~60 min stale but their pages served honest last-good data (`fetchedAt`-stamped, `meta.cache_stale`) instead of live-timing-out or rendering a truncated ranking. Non-regressive by construction. Cron run status reads `ok` when ≥1 board warms (per-board detail in `extra`) so routine single-board saturation doesn't log a chronically-red `pipeline_runs` row. **Deliberately NOT built:** API-route/concierge snapshot fallback — `fetchPublicInsight` already fails safe (returns `{status:"error"}`, no crash) and route default-params mostly don't match the cached page-defaults, so it's bespoke risk for modest gain.

- **⚠ The handoff's NO-PUSH blocker did NOT apply here.** It was a *cloud-sandbox* git-proxy 403 ("repo not in this session's authorized set"). Trevor's Windows box carries the PAT in `remote.origin.pushurl`, so pushes worked normally. **Check the actual remote before believing an inherited "cannot push" claim** — the two migrations had been applied to prod ~18h earlier and were sitting uncommitted purely because of that.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the "compute-for-all-then-filter" anti-pattern claimed a THIRD victim, and it was the top public-page 5xx.** `/[collection]/pack/dist/[distId]` threw **81 hard 500s/24h, every one `/nfl-all-day/`**. `v_allday_pack_info` LEFT JOINs a `DISTINCT ON` subquery over `pack_ev_latest`; the `dist_id` predicate lands **above** that `Unique` so it cannot push down, and PG cannot prune the join (LEFT JOIN removal needs a base relation with a unique index, not a subquery). Every AllDay pack page materialized the latest EV for **all 4,245 packs from 119,591 `pack_ev_history` rows** and discarded all but one. **The tell: not one column any caller reads came from that node** — all four consumers read only `mv_allday_pack_ev_corrected` + `allday_pack_supply`. New lean `v_allday_pack_detail_ev`: planner cost **1,195,280 → 7.54**, verified output-identical by a 0-row `EXCEPT` diff over all 3,052 AllDay dists, and the 8 previously-500ing dists now return in **36–883 ms** (live probe: 200 with 132–262 KB of real DOM). When a view is slow, check whether the expensive node feeds any column the caller actually selects before rewriting anything.
- **⚠ DURABLE — a fail-soft `if (error) return []` renders a TIMEOUT as an empty section at HTTP 200.** Measured live: one `/insights/candy-mlb` render logged **six simultaneous 57014s** and still served 200. It is **triply invisible** — the 5xx metrics can't see a 200; the board-liveness probe measures `SELECT count(*)` on the view (a query the planner prunes) so `public_board_slow_count`/`empty_count` both read **0**; and the user just sees a blank section. This is the 2026-08-06 "probe understates by up to 8,300×" finding **confirmed in production for the first time**. New `lib/insights/board-status.ts` + `DegradedDataNotice` make the boards say so; `null` when healthy, so a good render is byte-identical. **A board that SUCCEEDS with zero rows is deliberately NOT flagged** — an empty board is an honest answer.
- **⚠ CORRECTED the handoff on two points.** (1) It said "panini-squeeze and first-mint do not appear in any failure log" — **`panini-squeeze` fails repeatedly** (`backing view error: canceling statement…`), and its variant is the sharper one: it pages 10×600 `ORDER BY fmv_usd DESC`, so a page-3 failure renders the top ~1,800 rows **as though they were the whole ranking**. (2) The daytime monitor's prescribed build fix targets code that is **already fail-soft** (`loadSet` try/catches to `null`; `generateStaticParams` try/catches to `[]`) — so the throw that reds the build is elsewhere, and implementing its suggestion would close the ticket without fixing anything.
- **`/api/market` profiled, NOT rewritten (queued).** Worst timeout route (51×504/24h); shares `get_topshot_sniper_deals` with `/api/sniper-feed`. Measured **13,989 ms cold / 9,768 ms warm** against a 30 s statement timeout AND `maxDuration = 30`. ⚠ **Its plan is already index-optimal** (all index scans, cost 8,029, partition pruning working) — this is NOT the compute-for-all shape, so a rewrite would find nothing to remove; the cost is ~1,645 random probes into partitioned `fmv_snapshots` that 512 MB `shared_buffers` can't hold. Also note the fn has **no `listed_desc` arm**, so the route's default sort falls through to `discount_pct DESC` — **fmv-dependent**, which is why the 08-08 LIMIT-pushdown trick does not transfer. Lever is precompute/caching, not SQL.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the trust board is COLD-CACHE-bound, and this CORRECTS a claim I put in the ledger earlier the same day.** I had recorded as "reusable" that `SELECT … FROM v_rpc_trust_health WHERE metric IN (…)` "returns instantly — Postgres prunes the UNION ALL branches". The pruning half is true; **"instantly" is not** — I had happened to test two cheap arms. Measured properly: the SAME 10 arms cost **19,002 ms cold and 80 ms warm (237×)**. So per-arm cost is near-zero once resident and the whole cost is physical I/O (12 GB DB, 512 MB `shared_buffers`, depleted IO budget) — corroborating the standing "trust-health cost is not time, it's I/O" note. **Consequence: chunking `/api/sentinel`'s whole-board read would NOT bound its time** (a cold chunk still costs seconds), so I measured that idea and discarded it rather than shipping it. Precompute remains the only lever — but still cannot be done naively, since `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh` already runs at ~569 s of its 600 s budget and rolled back all 18 metrics on hitting the ceiling at 12:58Z that day.
- **Shipped the containment for the push-outage class: `migration-parity` (daily 07:40 UTC, reporting-only).** Prod migrations that never got a committed file — the exact harm behind this session's opening state. ⚠ **It matches on NAME, not version**: `apply_migration` stamps its own version, so my own migration landed in prod as `20260809155541` while the committed file is `20260809170000_…` — a version-keyed check would flag it missing forever. ⚠ **The window is bounded on purpose**: prod holds **2,478** migration rows vs **402** committed versioned files, so an unbounded check reports ~2,000 findings and becomes noise; at 14 days it found **114** (a real pre-existing backlog, now queued), at 3 days exactly **2**. It reports rather than fails for the same reason a permanently-red trust arm is harmful. ⚠ Windows footgun: `process.exit()` mid-stdout-flush trips a libuv assertion and the shell sees **127** — use `process.exitCode`.
- **Method notes.** `git push | tail` masks the exit code — and so does `npm run test:coverage | tail`: the first ratchet run reported "exit 0" while 7 tests were failing (it was `tail`'s status). Re-ran unpiped → real exit 1, then **proved the 3 files / 7 failures pre-exist my diff by stashing and re-running** (5× 5 s timeouts + 2× `expected 401` = the documented local-env class, green in CI). Both coverage gates green otherwise (component ratchet exit 0, 982 tests). ### August 8, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — drained the Cowork handoff `docs/handoff-2026-08-08-stale-saved-wallet-cached-counts.md`) — **2 green ships to `main` (`bd0eb975` ledger, `991291be` code)** plus a prod data reconcile: every user's displayed collection size was stale. CI-green + ledgered with revert paths. No auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, FMV/pricing math, or destructive-SQL change.

- **The bug: `saved_wallets.cached_moment_count` / `cached_fmv_usd` / `cached_top_tier` — what `/dashboard`, `/profile/<username>`, `/share` and the profile OG card render — had exactly ONE writer, the signup/wallet-association path.** Nothing refreshed them afterwards while `wallet_moments_cache` kept growing via the scheduled walks. Measured live: **99 rows, 21 of 21 users affected, 45 NULL, 41 drifting >5** (Edogg1976 Top Shot NULL vs 12,552; Rigged 33,374 vs 38,097; ThunderHour 50 vs 1,474 — its shallow first-paint value frozen since signup). Reconciled to **0 NULL / 0 drift at any tolerance**.
- **⚠ DURABLE — `aggregate_saved_wallet_stats` structurally CANNOT zero a collection the wallet no longer holds, and that is the sharper failure than the NULLs.** It runs `UPDATE … FROM agg` where `agg` is a `GROUP BY` over that wallet's wmc rows, so a `(wallet, collection)` pair with **zero** wmc rows yields no `agg` row, the join matches nothing, and the stored value survives **forever** — a wallet that sells out of a collection displays its old count indefinitely. Any future "recompute the cards" work must carry an explicit zero pass. NULL→0 is display-neutral (every consumer already coalesces: `?? 0` in ProfileClient / the collection profile page, `Number(…) || 0` in the OG route + `CrossCollectionHoldingsCard`), but it makes `cache_updated_at` a real freshness stamp.
- **Two durable writers (migration `20260809050000_audit_20260809_reconcile_saved_wallet_cached_stats.sql`).** (1) SECDEF `reconcile_all_saved_wallet_stats()` → pg_cron **jobid 259 `rpc-reconcile-saved-wallet-stats`, `33 13 * * *`** (06:33 PT; hour 13 UTC is the only hour carrying no other pg_cron job, deliberately off the `:17`-past and `*/6` pileups that contend for the depleted disk-IO budget). It **loops the existing per-wallet RPC instead of re-implementing the aggregate** — that RPC *is* the definition of these three columns (tier ladder, "0 fmv → NULL"), and a second set-based copy would be a second source of truth; the loop is over the **user** count (21), not the moment count. Verified live: `{"wallets":21,"rows_refreshed":78,"rows_zeroed":0,"elapsed_ms":37241}`. (2) `wallet-backfill-multicollection` re-stamps the wallet's cards at the end of its `after()`, **after** the COMPLETE telemetry row (that row is the load-bearing dispatch-gap signal — ordering is mutation-proven by a test), **gated on the wallet belonging to a signed-up user** because the route also runs for hundreds of unclaimed seeded wallets per 12h sweep and the aggregate is a full per-wallet wmc scan.
- **⚠ FOUND EN ROUTE — `docs/overnight/ledger.md` had been silently corrupting its own newest heading since `af3a30d4`.** A splice welded a `### ` heading onto the **tail of the line-9 "Dates are Pacific" note** rather than its own line, truncating that note mid-sentence (486→344 chars) and leaving the heading not matching `^### ` — so `grep -c '^### '` undercounted by one and the ledger guard's heading-set comparison could not protect that entry. Every later session spliced onto the same broken line, making it self-perpetuating. Line 9 restored verbatim from `HEAD~3`; count went 1106 → **1108** (+1 mine, +1 recovered), no heading lost. **When splicing the ledger, insert BEFORE line 9's newline — never onto it.**
- **Also corrected a FALSE comment** in `resolve-and-associate` claiming "the hourly wmc-fmv-populate sweep and the next dashboard load reconcile it upward" — neither did, and that comment is precisely why nobody looked for ~3 months. A comment asserting a backstop that does not exist is worse than no comment.
- **NOT done (deliberate):** no SQL invariant pin for `reconcile_all_saved_wallet_stats()` — pinning needs a fixture copy of `aggregate_saved_wallet_stats`, whose body calls `auth.uid()`, which is non-trivial stubbing for a display-only function whose worst failure a nightly re-run self-corrects. The handoff's optional "add the drift check to `rpc-pending-signups-watch`" was skipped as moot once the nightly reconcile landed (the handoff says so itself).

### August 8, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — drained the Cowork handoff `docs/handoff-2026-08-08-allday-locked-moments-invisible.md`) — **2 green ships to `main` (`4b354e0e`, `e0805f0c`)**: the AllDay LOCKED-moment fix, plus a CI unblock found along the way. Both CI-green + ledgered + verified live in prod. No auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, migration, or DB-schema change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — NFL All Day has NO on-chain locking contract, so a "locked" moment is INVISIBLE to every wallet walk we own.** Only `AllDay` + `PackNFT` are deployed at `0xe4cf4bdc1751c65d` (verified live) — unlike `TopShotLocking`, which flags the NFT *in place* so `getIDs()` still returns it. On All Day, Dapper custodies the moment and it is **absent from the holder's account entirely**. `GET_UNLOCKED_MOMENT_DETAILS` borrows `/public/AllDayNFTCollection` → `getIDs()`, so it structurally cannot see one, and a wallet whose AllDay moments are all locked scans **`ok=true / rows_found=0`** — indistinguishable from an empty wallet. That was the report: `0xdcd41c74d2dd0a66` (ThunderHour) held 5, RPC showed 0.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the handoff's prescribed fix (`searchMomentNFTsV2`) is IMPOSSIBLE; do not re-attempt that lane.** Both AllDay GQL surfaces are dead, re-verified 2026-08-08 **from Trevor's residential IP** (so it is upstream, not a worker-egress block): `public-api.nflallday.com/graphql` → **nginx 404** (path gone), `nflallday.com/consumer/graphql` → **403** block page. Confirms [[allday-consumer-gql-cf1009-blocked]] still holds 45 days on.
- **The fix (`4b354e0e`):** new `lib/chains/flow/allday-studio-holdings.ts` walks the **Dapper studio-platform** `searchAllDayNft` — the same unauthenticated, Origin-header, Vercel-egress-OK endpoint `allday-studio-sales-history-backfill` already uses — and `runAllDayDetailsBackfill` **UNIONs** it with the chain result, opted in per-collection via `config.studioCustodyHoldings` (AllDay only, so Golazos/Pinnacle are byte-identical). Fired in parallel with the chain read (≈no added wall-clock) and handed down to the paginated mega-wallet path, written once at `resumeFrom === 0`.
- **⚠ DURABLE — `owner_address` on studio is BARE HEX.** A `0x`-prefixed query returns `totalCount 0` — a **silent empty, not an error**, so it fails exactly like "this wallet owns nothing". `stripFlowPrefix()` is the single chokepoint; mutation-tested.
- **⚠ DURABLE — studio's `owner_address` CAN BE STALE, so this AUGMENTS and never REPLACES the chain, and never DELETEs a wmc row.** Verified counter-example: AllDay moment `1557801` is held **on-chain** by `0x11859edcf2f53edd` while studio still names a 2022 owner. Chain wins on nftId conflict. Live proof it holds: Levy re-backfilled to **33, not 32**.
- **⚠ The blast radius is platform-wide, not one user: Trevor's own wallet reads 24 on-chain vs 3,707 in studio.** wmc already held 3,706 for him, so **wmc has long been right and the chain walk is the undercount** — it is *new* wallets, whose only writer is that walk, that render empty. Verified before wiring that the two sources agree on keys: across the 32 moments present in BOTH for `0x11859edcf2f53edd`, studio `edition.id`/`serial_number` matched on-chain exactly (32/0).
- **Honesty preserved:** `on_chain_count` still means chain truth, union total is the new `holdings_count`, `studio_*` fields alongside; `rows_found` keeps its "raw on-chain scan count" meaning (the union passes malformed chain tuples through verbatim — filtering them silently redefined that field and the existing pinned test caught it). A degraded walk logs `studio_custody_walk_degraded` rather than passing as "no locked moments". `fetchAllDayStudioHoldings` never throws, so a studio outage leaves the on-chain backfill exactly as it was.
- **Live verification (prod):** ThunderHour `rows_processed 0 → 5`, all 5 wmc rows enriched, and the three moments match the report exactly incl. mint counts (`#1/4999`, `#59/59`, `#355/499`); telemetry read `on_chain=0 / holdings=5 / studio_added=5`. Regression: Levy 33→33, RipPacksCity 1→1, zero NULL `edition_key`/`player_name`, 0 new Sentry.
- **⚠ FOUND EN ROUTE — `main`'s CI had been RED for 5 straight commits and it was nobody's diff (`e0805f0c`).** `17738436` deleted `components/SignInWithDapper.tsx` + `components/auth/ConnectButton.tsx` but left both in the protected lists in `scripts/check-brand-tokens.mjs`; the guard fails on a missing protected file, so every push after it failed. **The step lives inside the `TypeScript` job, so the red job name pointed at `tsc`, which was passing the whole time.** Deleting a protected component means deleting its guard entry in the same commit. All 8 jobs green after.
- **NOT done (deliberate):** no bulk re-backfill was triggered — existing wallets self-heal on their next scheduled scan. Whether Golazos/UFC/Pinnacle have the same custody gap is **unverified**; the flag is AllDay-only.

### August 8, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "work on the Sentinel WARN" → observability → "keep going / do what you think is best" → tier decision → session-close) — **7 green ships to `main` (`2fd756e4` → `ece298d0`)**: a Sentinel-WARN fix, a multi-collection observability build, three saturation-resilience fixes, and the platform's #1 disk-IO query fix (index built by Trevor via SQL editor). Each CI-green + ledgered with a revert path. No auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or destructive-SQL change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — the DB is DISK-IO-BUDGET-bound, NOT compute-bound. Do NOT upgrade the compute tier to fix saturation.** The intermittent "everything 504s" spikes (`/api/market`, `/api/sentinel`, price-snapshots, entity pages, 6 pg_cron MV refreshes all timing out at once) are Supabase's **disk-IO burst-credit model** depleting → throughput throttles to a **22 MB/s baseline** (confirmed on-screen by Supabase's own "about to deplete its Disk IO Budget" banner). Diagnosis signature: `pg_stat_activity` shows the DB **idle** (2–3 of 90 connections active, CPU idle, 0 lock waits) with backends in **`DataFileRead`** I/O-wait; 12 GB DB vs 512 MB `shared_buffers` on the Small tier. **We are on SMALL** (2 GB RAM / 2-core, `max_connections`=90 — CLAUDE.md's old "Micro" was stale). **Medium is the SAME 2 cores** (only +RAM) for **4× the cost** — you don't get more cores until XL. So a bigger box would run the *same* wasteful queries with more cache; the fix is **killing the expensive queries**, which is free + permanent. Cheapest infra lever if ever needed: provision disk throughput/IOPS independently (not a compute bump). **EXPLAIN (no ANALYZE) is safe to profile even a saturated/IO-throttled DB** (planning only, no execution/IO) — used it throughout.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the "compute-for-all-then-LIMIT" anti-pattern + the heap-fetch-storm MV pattern, both fixed, both reusable:** (1) `get_allday_market_editions` ran a per-edition fmv LATERAL over ALL ~4,300 listed editions then `LIMIT 60` → 7s warm/12s cold, 504ing ~19% of the **public** Market tab. Fix (`abbf7fb5`, migration): for fmv-INDEPENDENT sorts (`price_asc`/`price_desc`, the default) sort+LIMIT on the aggregate FIRST, fmv-lookup only the page → **72ms** (byte-identical output, 0-row EXCEPT diff). (2) `mv_topshot_perfect_mint_premiums_board` refresh burned **2,417 s/day** because its `ed_med` median CTE used `(edition_id, price_usd)` then **HEAP-FETCHED ~406k rows** to filter `collection`/`sold_at`. Fix (`ece298d0`): partial covering index `idx_sales_2026_ts_edition_median (edition_id, sold_at DESC) INCLUDE (price_usd) WHERE collection='nba_top_shot' AND price_usd>0.50` → validated live **`Index Only Scan`, zero heap fetches**. The other 5 timing-out MV refreshes are the SAME class (queued, inbox `2026-08-08T1945Z.md`; `topshot-edition-median` likely shares the exact `ed_med` shape).
- **⚠ DURABLE — `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` cannot be run from the Supabase MCP** (can't run in a txn → not `apply_migration`; and a full-partition build exceeds the ~60s MCP tool cap, worse when disk-throttled — a plain `COUNT` already timed out at 25s). It aborts on client disconnect leaving an **INVALID index** on the hot partition. Path: the **Supabase SQL editor** (no cap) or a quiet-window psql. This one Trevor ran himself in the SQL editor; committed a reference migration (`20260808200000_*`) for repo↔DB parity (re-run is a no-op via `IF NOT EXISTS`).
- **⚠ DURABLE — the Sentinel itself was 504ing ~88% of runs (failing BLIND — no report, no digest, no alerting).** Its ~8 sequential health-check DB reads blew `maxDuration=60` under saturation. Bumped `60→180` (`f3ebcd8e`; GHA caller waits 5 min, Pro cap 800s). Same class fixed on `price-snapshots` `maxDuration 15→60` (`e2ff80cd`) — it was silently dropping hourly OHLC buckets (7 of 24 missing); safe because its RPC only writes the prior hour + is idempotent (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). A cron/health route near its cap under saturation is a candidate for this whenever the work is idempotent.
- **Multi-collection Sentinel (`e47fefef`, migration):** the aggregate `Sales Ingest (2h)` check was ~92% Top Shot, masking a per-collection ingest death, and Pinnacle sales (`pinnacle_sales`) weren't even watched. New `sentinel_ingest_watch` config + service-role `sentinel_sales_ingest_health()` (per-collection + per-source lane health, **index-bounded per collection** because **there is NO index on `sales.ingested_at`** — every sales index is `sold_at`/`collection_id`, so query recent sales by `sold_at` + `collection_id`). Two new checks page TS/AllDay, warn the rest, off for UFC (market closed). +9 tests. Also the `pinnacle-catalog-backfill` WARN that opened the thread → Vercel-cron backstop (`2fd756e4`, vercel.json 35→36) for the daily catalog whose sole cron-job.org tick had dropped.
- **Method:** 504-triage via Vercel `get_runtime_logs` `group_by=requestPath`/`statusCode`; separate transient-saturation from chronic by pre/post-fix timing + `deploymentId`. Ledger is concurrent-write-heavy (several rebases this session; splice the freshly-read top keeping BOTH entries, `grep -c '^### '` must go UP). Scheduled self-check-ins via `send_later` (self-bind) for live verification; cancelled the pending one at session close since the thread is being archived.

### August 5, 2026 (Claude Code, docs/hooks — "update CLAUDE.md to current state" → "how do we stop the stale-main divergence" → "do what else makes sense") — verified the file was already fully current (no factual edit due), then FIXED the SessionStart hook's mirror-lineage self-heal, which the 08-03 filter-repo root re-hash had silently defeated. `main` @ `950cdcf4` (hook + ledger, pushed clean). Hook-only; no product/DB/prod change.

- **⚠ DURABLE — the "stale local `main`" self-heal was BROKEN and nothing caught it, because the guard is repo-vs-repo.** `.claude/hooks/session-start.sh`'s realign fallback gated on `merge-base(main, origin/main) == origin's ROOT commit`. The 2026-08-03 `git filter-repo` purge re-hashed origin's root (`55f5fe4f`), so a frozen pre-purge provisioning mirror now shares only a DEEPER non-root ancestor (`90c508a4`) with origin/main → the gate mismatched → the hook printed "left as-is" and stranded local `main` days behind (the July-29 lineage this thread opened on). **A gate that hard-codes any historical SHA is defeated by the next history rewrite — key on a CONTENT property instead.**
- **The fix (`950cdcf4`): purge-proof `is_mirror_lineage()`** — realign when local `main` carries no commit unique by patch-id (`git cherry origin/main main` shows no `+` — provably lossless) OR is ≥100 commits ahead (mirror-shaped magnitude; genuine unpushed work on a commit-directly-to-`main` repo is never hundreds, and this catches the purge-orphan case where a stripped commit leaves a lone `+` so the lossless signal alone misses). Kept every prior guard: ff-only tried FIRST, clean tree required before `reset --hard`, genuine small branch-off left untouched. Validated: `bash -n` clean; the real July-29 mirror `d7fda5a0` (ahead 4023) → realign, a synthetic 3-commit branch-off → left alone.
- **Root cause is UPSTREAM PROVISIONING (out of repo), now handed to Cowork:** the web/Cowork container seeds each session from a frozen, days-old, re-hashed mirror of `main` rather than a live clone. The hook only heals it after the fact — and only once provisioning ships a snapshot containing the fixed hook (the same chicken-and-egg that delayed the first 08-05 attempt `aa453cc6`). Permanent cure = seed from live `origin/main` at provision time. **Manual instant heal any session (clean tree): `git fetch origin main && git reset --hard origin/main`.**
- **⚠ METHOD TRAP (cost me my first hook Write): a `git reset --hard origin/main` run to clean test pollution ALSO discards uncommitted working-tree edits.** I made synthetic test commits ON `main` (wrong — use a throwaway branch) then `reset --hard` to undo them, which reverted my un-committed hook edit back to origin's version. Commit hook/code changes BEFORE any reset; never make test commits on `main`.

- **⚠ DURABLE — "Edge Requests Nx spike + external API failures" Vercel alert = SCRAPER → Supabase POOLER exhaustion, not a code regression and not raw-Postgres saturation.** Diagnose: `get_runtime_logs group_by=requestPath` (finds the flooded public page — here `/nba-top-shot/collection`, all `cache=MISS`/200, ~5 req/s bursts, bare URLs) + `get_runtime_errors` (the "external API failures" are PGRST003 "Timed out acquiring connection from connection pool" + "canceling statement due to statement timeout" across every DB route). `pg_stat_activity` can read CALM (raw PG fine, e.g. 4 active/90) while the **pooler** pool is what tips over — it's bursty/self-limiting (recovers between bursts; entity pages "degrade to empty"). **The fix is edge rate-limiting the scraper — Vercel Firewall per-IP rate limit (operator dashboard; the Vercel MCP has NO firewall/WAF write tool) — NOT a code change.** Entity pages are already ISR-cached (`revalidate=600`), so the load is volume-driven (thousands of distinct catalog URLs each a first-render cache-MISS); no caching win exists in code.
- **⚠ DURABLE — statement_timeout convention (role defaults + per-function caps).** Role defaults: **anon 3s / authenticated 8s / service_role 30s** (postgres unlimited). Public **entity pages query under `service_role` via `supabaseAdmin`** — so an anon-role timeout change is a NO-OP for them. The team deliberately per-function-caps the entity read RPCs so a slow read fail-fasts and frees its pooler connection: `get_edition_detail/_recent_sales/_fmv_history`, `get_player_*`, `get_team_activity`, `get_set_detail`, `get_series_detail` = **8s**; `get_team_detail` **25s**; `get_pack_detail_bundle` **30s** (pack EV is genuinely heavy — do NOT cap it low). This session closed a gap: `get_edition_market_bundle` / `get_edition_insight_links` / `get_edition_special_serials` inherited the 30s default while their same-page siblings freed at 8s → capped them at 8s (migration `audit_20260805_cap_edition_read_fn_statement_timeout`, warm 132/41/<1000ms so ~60× headroom, only bites under saturation). `ALTER FUNCTION … SET statement_timeout` is config-only (preserves body + search_path). **Revert:** `RESET statement_timeout` on the three fns.
- **Method reconfirmed:** ledger is concurrent-write-heavy (re-read fresh before every splice; rejected push → `git pull --rebase`, resolve the top-of-ledger conflict by keeping BOTH entries newest-first; `grep -c '^### '` must go UP); a11y toggle/nav pattern = `role="button"`+`tabIndex={0}`+`onKeyDown` guarded by `e.target===e.currentTarget`; TZ-forced tests bite where a UTC CI runner can't (`process.env.TZ` is honored at runtime for `toLocaleString`); dead-code deletion re-verifies zero-importer live (only refs were `check-brand-tokens.mjs` guard lines) and re-runs BOTH coverage ratchets before push; `actions_list` MCP result overflows the token cap → parse the saved JSON file with python.
