- **🔒 Audited all 121 DB-invariant pins against LIVE production and fixed 2 genuinely STALE ones (`e8e410ed`).** The in-CI drift guard is repo-vs-repo and structurally CANNOT catch a pin whose live definition drifted; only a live check can (`scripts/check-db-pin-staleness.mjs`, which needs `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` the sandbox lacks). **DURABLE METHOD — replicate it from the cloud sandbox via the Supabase MCP:** `execute_sql` runs elevated, so pull `pg_proc.prosrc` (or `pg_get_functiondef`, base64-encode big bodies with `encode(convert_to(...,'UTF8'),'base64')` to dodge JSON-escaping) for the pinned fn set, then compare to each pin's committed-migration body under the script's own normalization (collapse `\s+`, plus a comment-stripped variant — live prosrc is often comment-stripped vs the file). Found `get_wallet_collection_snapshot` + `holdings_summary` STALE: both wallet-total RPCs were redefined 2026-08-03 for UFC market-closure (`collections.market_closed_at` excludes closed collections from grand totals) and **applied via MCP with NO committed migration**, so pin+test+guard all stayed green while validating a body that no longer runs. Fix = author snapshot migrations byte-identical to live (no-op if applied), repoint PINS, update the embedded DDL, AND (per CLAUDE.md) re-check the ASSERTIONS — added a `market_closed_at` fixture column + a closed-market assertion to each test (moments still counted, value excluded). Validated end-to-end: drift guard 122/122, both now match live, both SQL tests pass on a throwaway `postgres:16`, new assertions mutation-proven to bite. Other 119 clean. **⚠ The cloud sandbox CAN stand up `postgres:16`** for `run-db-tests.sh`: bins at `/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin`, `useradd pgtest` + `su pgtest -c "initdb -A trust -U postgres"` + `pg_ctl -o '-k /tmp/pgrun -p <port>'` + `CREATE EXTENSION unaccent` — but on RE-init in the same session the server can fail to start (`global/pg_filenode.map` missing); use a FRESH datadir/port and `select 1` to confirm connectivity before trusting a "test failed" (a down server fails on connection, not assertion — a false mutation-test pass).
- **🔒 Pinned the worst-incident edge fn's cursor safety (`8ad588c6`).** `ingest-allday-pack-opens` (the pack-opens walker behind the 2026-07-25 cursor-reset that re-walked ~31.4M blocks + the 2026-08-01 budget-stop drop) had NONE of its inline cursor safety pinned, unlike its topshot twin. New `__tests__/edge-ingest-allday-pack-opens-cursor.test.ts` source-drift-pins the getCursor discriminated-union (read error ≠ reseed), the forward `after = err||rerr ? start-1 : end` hold, and the descending-backfill arithmetic vs the tested `nextBackfillCursor` mirror; each mutation-proven. **Edge/worker/component layers are otherwise saturated on current `main`** (only `sync-nba-games`, a retired 410 stub, is genuinely untested — a path-reference scan over-counts because tests reference edge fns by NAME in arrays like `THROW_PATTERN_WALKERS`, not by import path).
- **🔧 Session-start hook now self-heals the mirror-lineage nag (`aa453cc6`).** ⚠ **DURABLE — web sessions here are seeded with a re-hashed MIRROR lineage of `main`**: local `main` shares ONLY the root commit with `origin/main` (merge-base == root, ~4000 commits each side, identical commit MESSAGES under different SHAs), frozen days behind. The Stop hook counts `origin/main..HEAD` and nagged "N unpushed commits" every turn; `.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` used `git merge --ff-only` which fails on this (non-ff) and treated the mirror as precious unpushed work. Added a realign arm to both hook branches fenced by *non-fast-forwardable AND merge-base == root AND (on-main) clean tree* → `git reset --hard`/`git branch -f`; genuine branch-offs (recent merge-base) or dirty trees fall through untouched. **Manual one-liner if it ever recurs before the hook runs: `git fetch origin main && git reset --hard origin/main` (clean tree only).** Root cause is upstream provisioning (out of repo); the hook makes it harmless. **Editing that hook is classifier-gated** (it adds `git reset --hard` to an auto-run file) — needs explicit user approval.
- **Method reconfirmed:** `npm install` then `git checkout package-lock.json` (Node 22 vs CI 24); ledger is concurrent-write-heavy (rejected push → `git fetch` + `merge --ff-only` + re-splice the freshly-read top, `grep -c '^### '` must go UP); large MCP/Actions tool results auto-save to a file — parse with python (`json.load(...)['result']`, slice the `<untrusted-data>` array), never trust an unauthenticated `curl` to the GitHub API (proxy-blocked → `None`).

### August 4, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — drained the "Golazos shell drain stalled" handoff → "keep going" ×3 → verified) — 🩹 **ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED the Golazos wallet-scan silent-failure that stranded 3,822 wmc shells, then VERIFIED it worked end-to-end (shells 3,822 → 0, sales undercount 134 → 87).** Commits `922fd2c1` (borrow fix + honesty guards) · `2c18f7ff` (pagination gate) · `9cb057ca` (verification); each its own ledger entry (2026-08-04) + revert path. Code+test only; no migration, no prod-DB mutation.

- **The bug — a nil Cadence borrow masquerading as an empty wallet.** `wallet-backfill-golazos`'s details scan (`runAllDayDetailsBackfill` + `GET_GOLAZOS_MOMENT_DETAILS`, live since 07-31) borrowed the NARROW `&{NonFungibleToken.Collection}`, which returns nil for wallets whose capability is published only as `CollectionPublic` → the script returned a genuine `[]` → logged `on_chain_count:0 / no_more_moments / ok:true`, indistinguishable from a real empty. **Root-caused from telemetry, NOT a guess:** 7d of golazos runs showed ONLY `no_more_moments` + `pagination_failed` (never `error`/`timeout`), so `fcl.query` RESOLVES `[]` (it rejects on script failure) → a nil borrow, not a throw. Provenance clinched it: canonical wallet `0x4ba45c2312086820` had `max_onchain_ever=0` across every scan yet held 1,832 wmc rows all written in one 07-25 18:16 bulk pass — the live route had NEVER successfully read it.
- **The fix — borrow the BROADER `&{NonFungibleToken.CollectionPublic}`** (both public-path attempts). Verified against `onflow/flow-nft` `NonFungibleToken.cdc` via WebFetch: `CollectionPublic` declares BOTH `getIDs()` and `borrowNFT()`, and `Collection` inherits it, so CollectionPublic resolves for the currently-working wallets PLUS the nil-borrow ones (the sibling ID-only `CADENCE_GOLAZOS` already proved that borrow resolves for these wallets). ⚠ **DURABLE — Flow REST is 403 from the cloud sandbox and no Cadence MCP was connected, so on-chain testing was impossible; the safe path was (1) verify the contract INTERFACE from the public standard source, (2) ship self-verifying (a failing script → visible ok:false, never silent/corrupting), (3) scheduled self-check to confirm.**
- **Honesty guards (so `on_chain_count:0` can never again silently mean "the scan failed"):** a non-array `fcl.query` result → `ok:false non_array_scan_result` (universal); an empty scan for a wallet that STILL has cached wmc rows → `ok:false empty_scan_but_cached_holdings` (+ `cached_row_count`), gated by new config flag `flagEmptyWithCachedHoldings` set ONLY on the Golazos route so AllDay's verified-healthy empty path is untouched. Both skip the clean-refresh stamp so the wallet is retried. Plus a pagination-misroute gate: the comp-limit/access-500 catch branches routed EVERY caller into the AllDay paginated path (`mode:"allday"`) — there's no Golazos range script — so gated on `detailsMode==="details_allday"` (AllDay byte-for-byte unchanged; Golazos surfaces an honest failure).
- **✅ VERIFIED ~2.5h post-deploy via a scheduled self-check:** canonical wallet `0 → 1,832`; **shells 3,822 → 0**; runs returning moments ~23% → **66%**; **0** `empty_scan_but_cached_holdings` (nothing masked). Downstream fired AUTOMATICALLY as traced — `promote_unmapped_sales` **Path 4** (resolves via `wmc.edition_key` → `editions.external_id`, called in `golazos-sales-indexer`'s finally block every run): **shell-blocked unmapped sales 45 → 0, unmapped_open 134 → 87.** Closes Gate 1 item 3's Golazos shell drain. DURABLE: enriching a shell is SUFFICIENT to unblock its sale — no map rebuild — because Path 4 reads wmc directly.
- **⚠ ENVIRONMENT — local `main` was a stale DIVERGENT line (4023 vs 4469 commits, merge-base = initial commit), not just behind.** Prod deploys `origin/main`; the recent Golazos work (`b60e2833`, `925b54f5`) was only on origin. `git checkout -B main origin/main` FIRST or you fix the wrong code and push non-fast-forward. Method notes reconfirmed: `npm install` then `git checkout package-lock.json` (Node 22 vs CI 24); ledger is concurrent-write-heavy (rejected push → fetch, re-splice the freshly-read file, `grep -c '^### '` must go UP).

UFC value stays in `wmc.fmv_usd` (for the count/display); it is simply no longer summed.

### August 3, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "implement a TODO" → "keep an eye out for issues" → "keep going" → "wrap up for memory") — re-confirmed NO safe live TODO, then a full health sweep found ONE real bug and shipped it: the smoke gate's retry helper was a silent no-op on timeouts. `main` @ `c741e338` (deploy READY, CI green); ledger entry 2026-08-03 + revert path. Smoke-route only; no product/FMV/ingest/DB.

- **SHIPPED — `smokeFetchRetry` reused the caller's one-shot `AbortSignal.timeout(N)` on its retry, so the retry aborted INSTANTLY on any timeout — a silent no-op on the exact class it exists to tolerate** (`app/api/smoke-test/route.ts`, `c741e338`). `isTimeoutOrTransient` caught the abort and fired the retry, but the retry reused the same already-fired signal → immediate re-abort. This weakened EVERY caller (sniper-feed, og, wallet-search, public-profile), and the two `resolve-and-associate` probes had never been migrated onto the helper at all (bare `smokeFetch` = zero retry) — which is why Sentry `NEXTJS-K` flapped **43× over 3 months** on "operation was aborted due to timeout". Fix: `smokeFetchRetry` now builds a **fresh** `AbortSignal.timeout(SMOKE_RETRY_TIMEOUT_MS=15000)` on the retry when `init.signal?.aborted` (reuses `init` otherwise, so a non-timeout transient keeps its live budget); + converted the flapping Phase-4.1 correctness probe onto it (left the 4.2 latency probe on bare `smokeFetch` — a retry would muddy its `<3s` assertion and it wasn't flapping). +2 regression tests (`__tests__/api-smoke-test-html-contains.test.ts`); the fresh-signal one was **proven to fail against the pre-fix helper**. **DURABLE: the existing unit tests passed the whole time because they mock `fetch` with no real signal — a mocked-fetch test can't catch an AbortSignal-lifecycle bug; when a helper's contract is about signal reuse, assert the signal identity per attempt.** `checkUrl` was always correct (it builds a fresh `AbortSignal.timeout` inside each of its 3 fetch blocks); the inline `smokeFetchRetry` was the odd one out.
- **DURABLE — a Sentry statement-timeout cluster (57014) on entity pages is almost always SATURATION collateral, not a slow query; profile WARM before "optimizing".** Four issues (`NEXTJS-1Z` pack /16 users, `1Y` team, `23` player, `24` set) all first-seen Aug 2 = the fmv-recalc sweep-cursor stall (`484d08d7`) that re-priced ~1,000 editions every 20 min and saturated the pool. Proof it was collateral, not code: `get_pack_detail_bundle` warm = **16.8ms** (a timeout needs ~8000ms), the team/player RPCs already carry their slug expression indexes, and every event predated the fix's 20:42Z deploy with zero recurrences since. Resolved all four (Sentry auto-reopens as a regression if they recur — so resolving a confirmed-collateral cluster is correct, it makes a genuine recurrence stand out instead of hiding in the pile). CLAUDE.md already says this ("A pool-acquire timeout is a SATURATION symptom… profile warm vs cold first") — this extends it to *statement* timeouts on the read RPCs.

Fixed in all 4 listing + all 4 sales indexers, in **three structural variants**: (a) cursor-written-after-loop → cap to `firstFailedChunkStart-1` (allday/topshot/golazos/ufc-listings, allday/golazos-sales); (b) **ufc-sales advances the cursor PER-CHUNK inside the loop** → a mid-range failure lets a later chunk LEAPFROG the gap, so it `break`s on first failure; (c) **topshot `sales-indexer` uses `fcl.send` (THROWS on failure, vs the REST helpers returning `[]`)** with two per-fetch catches + two cursor sites → track across both, cap both via a shared `cursorTarget`. Re-scan is safe (all writes idempotent — sales dedup on `transaction_hash`, listings upsert). +2 regression tests proven to bite. ⚠ **CRUCIAL NUANCE: `fetchEventRange` DEGRADES an HTTP 500/429 to `[]` ON PURPOSE** (the dormant Flowty-fork legs 500 by design; the "survives a 500" tests encode it) — only the THROW path is the bug; do NOT "fix" the intentional HTTP-degrade.
- **DECLINED with reasoning (own judgement): do NOT make a live-leg HTTP-500 hold the cursor.** It would wedge every FMV indexer at once during a broad Flow-REST 500 storm; distinguishing live vs dormant legs is fiddly; the safer bounded-retry needs prod 500-rate + run-duration metrics the sandbox lacks. Measure-first, not a blind fix. (Logged as a DECIDED ledger entry.)

When you refactor a pinned pattern, the grep guard breaks even though intent holds — update the guard, don't revert the fix.

### August 3, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — drained the FMV-sweep-cursor-stall handoff) — 🩹 **UNSTUCK THE FMV SWEEP: it had been re-pricing the same ~1,000 editions on every run, leaving 74% of the traded catalogue never recomputed** — plus a 126× fix to a slow PUBLIC board. Commits `a989aebc` (ledger) · `484d08d7` (fix) · `3655cf44` (board); each its own ledger entry (2026-08-03) + revert path. CI-green, deploy `dpl_4K43mhkkCbseaJTqgJautqoJaLSh` READY.

- **🩹 SHIPPED — `/api/fmv-recalc` cursor never advanced (`484d08d7`).** It asked `fmv_recalc_edition_page` for a **2,500**-edition page over PostgREST, which clamps RPC results to `db-max-rows`=**1000** and returned 1,000; `hasMore = pageEditionIds.length === limit` (1000 !== 2500) → false → `cursor_after=NULL` → **every run restarted at offset 0, `ok=true` throughout**. Verified live before shipping: 20h of runs all `cursor_before='0'`/`cursor_after=NULL`; the page fn returns **2,500** called direct in SQL vs **1,000** through PostgREST; population is **11,606** editions with a sale in the 30d window. Fix: `DEFAULT_LIMIT` 2500→**500**, new `MAX_PAGE_LIMIT=900` ceiling on `body.limit` (was 5000 — a manual call could re-trigger the truncation), `hasMore` `===`→`>=`, a cap-hit tripwire, `page_size`/`has_more` telemetry, and a `Number.isFinite` guard on the limit parse (`Math.min(NaN,x)` is NaN — the same class already swept out of `/api/edition-history` and `/insights`). **VERIFIED IN PROD: first run on the new code went `cursor_before=0 → cursor_after=500`, `has_more=true`** — the first cursor advance since the cursor was introduced. **✅ AND THE ACCURACY PAYOFF LANDED: by sweep position 6500/11606 (56% of one pass), editions publishing FMV >2× their own 30d realised median fell from the roadmap's 264 TS / 194 AllDay to `18` / `12` — 463 → 30, −94% — with p90 ratios 1.889→1.167 (TS) and 3.000→1.214 (AllDay), collapsing toward the never-floored `drain_fmv_cold_tail` control (1.060), median holding 1.000.** This closes the 08-02 dust-floor question: the removal (`3809425b`) was right all along and simply could not REACH the catalogue while every run restarted at page 0. **Revert:** `git revert 484d08d7` (no DB unwind; the sweep just returns to page 0).
- **⚠ DURABLE — chose 500, NOT the handoff's 900, because of a risk the handoff never measured: fmv-recalc runs average 181s against `maxDuration=300` and *95 of ~402 invocations in 72h (23.6%) are KILLED at the wall* before writing a terminal row.** A killed run logs no `cursor_after`, so the next run retries the SAME offset — harmless while the cursor was pinned at 0, but once it advances a systematically-slow page could re-stall the sweep. 900 sits inside the band where a quarter of runs already die. **QUEUED (not shipped — cost decision): `maxDuration` is 300 but the Vercel Pro cap is 800, so the route's own comment calling 300 "the Vercel Pro maximum" is WRONG.** Raising it would likely eliminate the kills.
- **⚠ DURABLE — the guard for this class already existed one layer away and nobody noticed.** `__tests__/invariants-fmv-recalc-chunking.test.ts` enforces the identical 1000-row cap on every `.in()` chunk site *and its header comment states the cap outright* — it just never covered the page fetch. Extended it there (+5 assertions), mutation-tested: restoring `2500` and `===` reds exactly the two intended assertions. **When you find a silent-truncation bug, grep for an existing guard on the same constant — the fix is usually to widen its scope, not to write a new file.**
- **SHIPPED — `candy_special_serials_board`, a PUBLIC board: buffer touches ~397,000 → ~42,000 (**~9×**) (`3655cf44`, `audit_20260803_candy_special_serials_board_union_arms`).** ⚠ **DURABLE — do NOT quote wall-clock speedups on this Micro instance without a like-for-like load comparison.** I first recorded "7,388ms → 58ms (~126×)"; re-measuring the SAME fixed view under load returned **28,174ms** with an identical plan and identical buffer counts. Wall-clock here swings ~500× with I/O contention, so a quiet "after" against a contended "before" measures the load, not the fix. Like-for-like under heavy load: 82,137ms → 28,174ms (~2.9×). **Buffer accesses are the load-independent metric — use them.** It was **2.73×** over its liveness budget and one of the 3 boards holding `public_board_slow_count` in BREACH. ⚠ **The 3 breaching boards are NOT the two that arm's own `catches` text names** (`topshot_perfect_mint_premiums_board`, `topshot_pack_reality_dist` — both since re-budgeted); the live three are `candy_special_serials_board` (2.73×) plus `topshot_first_mint_trophy_stats` (1.04×) and `topshot_first_mint_trophies` (1.02×). Cause: a four-way OR on `serial_number` that Postgres can't satisfy from an index, so each of 125 editions scanned ~203 serials and filtered 198 away (25,375 index entries / 6,623 heap fetches for 607 rows), with the correlated FMV join re-running **607 times (327,554 buffers)**. Split into three `UNION` arms inside a LATERAL → index probes, and the FMV join collapsed to one Hash Right Join (1,101 buffers). **`UNION` not `UNION ALL` is load-bearing** (a serial can satisfy two arms). Equality proven BEFORE applying: 607 rows both ways, **both directions of `EXCEPT ALL` = 0**.
- **⚠ DURABLE — two hypotheses tested and REJECTED before shipping; measure, don't pattern-match.** (1) The candy slowness looked like the 08-01 VACUUM/visibility-map win, but wmc measured **98.4% all-visible**, 4.5% dead, autovacuum 40 min prior — it was query shape, and a VACUUM would have decayed within hours on a table that hot. (2) For `topshot_first_mint_trophies` I tried the *same* restructure (restrict the `other_serial_avg` aggregate to the ~662 editions the inner join keeps, instead of all 11,164) — provably equivalent, and **5× SLOWER (39,071ms vs 8,216ms)**: at 73% selectivity one parallel seq scan beats 672 per-edition index probes. **Its existing plan is already optimal — do not "fix" it.** The two marginal boards are 2–4% over a budget set at 3× warm; that is a threshold question, not a query defect.
- **SHIPPED — closed-market disclosure finally reached UFC's two densest priced tabs (`53ff1fa5`), closing Gate-1 item 4.** ⚠ **The 2026-08-02 honesty pass shipped only the SEO half: `lib/market-closed.ts` is imported by `lib/seo.ts` and NOTHING else**, so titles/JSON-LD disclosed the closure while the rendered tabs did not. `MarketplaceStatusBanner` covered overview/collection/sniper/edition but **not `sets` or `analytics`** — and those are the two densest priced surfaces UFC registers (`pages: overview, collection, sniper, sets, analytics`): analytics renders `total_fmv` + per-tier/per-series FMV + a locked/unlocked split (a whole portfolio valued on a market whose last Flow sale was **2026-05-13**), and sets renders `lowestAsk`/`fmv` per missing piece (a cost-to-complete nobody can transact at). Per-row staleness can't catch this — UFC snapshots keep re-stamping `computed_at`, so freshness reads green on year-old evidence; the collection-level fact is the only honest signal. Additive (the banner returns `null` for healthy collections; verified `/nba-top-shot/analytics` unchanged). Guard `__tests__/invariants-closed-market-disclosure.test.ts` pins the mount on all 5 UFC tabs, **cross-checks the registry so a NEW UFC tab fails the guard rather than silently escaping it**, and asserts *behaviourally* that `isMarketClosed()` resolves both `ufc` and the `ufc-strike` alias (the alias is registered by assignment, not a literal map key — a source regex would pin syntax, not the guarantee). Mutation-tested.
- **⚠ DURABLE — measured, then declined: `topshot_fmv_pct_stale_30d` must NOT be re-baselined until a full sweep completes**, and the Gate-1 **pack-EV "actionable vs retired" item is a product decision, not an honesty defect** (the 08-02 pass already labels the 4,488 non-actionable rows; how a public board *defaults* its presentation is Trevor's call). Accuracy checkpoint recorded in the ledger for the next session: at sweep position 1500/11606, editions publishing >2× their own 30d realised median are **TS 263 / AllDay 200 (463)** vs the roadmap's 458 — **unchanged by design**, since the sweep pages most-recent-first while that cohort sits in the stale tail.
- ⚠ **Don't hand-trigger `fmv-recalc` to verify — it races the cron.** My manual POST landed 35s after a cron tick; both concurrent runs died at the 300s wall. The heartbeat row (`pipeline='fmv-recalc-heartbeat'`, written synchronously at `after()` entry) carries `edition_limit`, so it proves which code is live **without** triggering anything.

### August 2, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — FMV dust-filter decision doc) — ANALYSIS ONLY on this thread: measured the blast radius of the `DUST_PRICE_USD = 0.5` sale floor and recommended deleting it. Docs-only commit; **no code, no migration, no prod-DB change**. Ledger entry 2026-08-02. **✅ SUPERSEDED — the recommendation was ACCEPTED and SHIPPED 2026-08-02 (`3809425b`, Trevor/Cowork); see the Aug-3 entry above. The QUEUED status below is historical.**

- **The finding:** the absolute $0.50 floor at [lib/fmv-recalc-math.ts:8](lib/fmv-recalc-math.ts) / `:116` (step 1 of `dampenGrailSpike`, before any pricing math) discards **46.0% of Top Shot** and **76.3% of All Day** 30d sales, and `sales_count_30d` is the POST-filter count — so RPC under-reports volume as well as over-reporting price. Attribution is a clean dose-response: published FMV ÷ the edition's own raw 30d median is **1.03×** where the filter touches nothing, **1.55×** where it cuts some, **2.28×** where it empties the edition (All Day 1.00× / 1.97× / 3.38×). Mark-to-market, the affected editions transacted **$24,464** in 30d that RPC values at **$35,503 (+45%)**. Full option analysis, R1–R9 risks, exact diff, and verification checklist: [docs/fmv-dust-filter-decision-2026-08-02.md](docs/fmv-dust-filter-decision-2026-08-02.md).
- **DURABLE — `fmv_current` for actively-traded editions has TWO writers, and a platform-wide FMV scan HIDES this class of defect unless you split by `algo_version`.** Besides `/api/fmv-recalc` (`1.7.0*`), `drain_fmv_cold_tail()` (`cold-tail-1.0*` — 4,504 TS + 2,099 AllDay editions) writes a plain `PERCENTILE_CONT(0.5)` over ALL 30d sales with **no dust floor, no outlier removal, no grail guard**. Editions the dust filter empties fall silent, age 7 days, then get rescued at an honest price (ratio 1.000) — which is why the platform-wide average looks fine while the `1.7.0` rows are 1.55–2.28× wrong. Two side effects: that rescue is **7+ days stale by construction** (mean snapshot age 73.6h vs 41.7h), and `purge_fmv_snapshots_today()` deletes a same-day cold-tail row for a dust-emptied edition without fmv-recalc replacing it.
- **DURABLE — the constant was never derived from data.** `e3aee286` (2026-06-10) introduced it as step 1 of a 4-step guard aimed at the HIGH side (the "$9,000 Serial #1 Jokić" class); steps 2–4 are what the commit, the audit and all 40 unit tests actually describe. Its assumed downside protection already exists twice over — the `0.2 × median` band inside `wapWithoutOutliers` and steps 2–4 for the high side — so removing it does not remove a guard (with it gone, min published FMV is **$0.19** TS; only 72/8,481 editions land below half their own median). It is also **not** wash-trade defense: **zero** self-trades platform-wide in 30d, 1,094 distinct TS buyers below $0.50, and the price histogram is continuous through the cut (modal TS price = **$0.25 × 5,354 sales**, the marketplace minimum).
- **`fmv_apply_thin_sale_haircut` needs no fix** — a symptom, not a second defect (misfires on 691 editions but removes $127 total, and they stay ~2–2.8× their own median afterward); it stops selecting them once counts are honest. **Pinnacle is out of scope on both counts** (render-keyed in `pinnacle_fmv_history`, and fmv-recalc explicitly `.neq`s it); Candy 0 price changes; UFC 0 sales.

Real human denominator ≈ **8**, not 441; the single paste is Trevor's own wallet in a 36-event session. **Do not redesign the box** — the input is traffic.
- **Item 10 (409 "overstated" FMV editions)** — reproduced (432/4,412 = 9.8%) but the dollars kill the headline: **median sale price $0.30, median overstatement $0.55, total across all 432 = $339**, only 4 above $5 FMV and **zero above $20**. A ratio artifact on penny moments. **Do NOT move the 3× clamp to 2×.** If revisited, gate on **absolute gap**, not ratio.

**Durable operational lessons:**
- **A cadence watchlist keyed on SILENCE cannot see PARTIAL tick loss.** GitHub Actions was dropping **~83%** of `topshot-listing-cache` ticks (10–13/day vs 72) — measured from **GHA run history, not the DB**, which matters because the route is `after()`-based and a DB-only count would have been ambiguous. It *was* watchlisted (360 min) and never breached, because at 17% delivery it still fired ~every 2h. Moved it + `allow-list-reconcile` (~60% loss, **no watchlist row at all**) to Vercel crons at identical minutes. **Vercel Cron can only ever send `CRON_SECRET`** — both routes accepted only `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN`, so a `vercel.json`-only move would have 401'd every tick while still looking scheduled.
- **`after()` swallows observability, not just work.** `compute-laliga-pack-ev` has a daily cron and **zero `pipeline_runs` rows ever** because its `log_pipeline_run` lives inside `after()`. Added a synchronous invoked-marker so "never invoked" and "`after()` dropped" are distinguishable. Its header comment claiming the Golazos pool is empty is **false** — the pool now holds **1,957** pullable rows, so it has real work and its silence is a genuine gap.
- **Entity-page `statement timeout` Sentry issues (pack/player/team) are SATURATION, not slow queries** — `get_player_editions` is **161ms warm** on the heaviest player. Left alone deliberately; the structural-throw is an intentional honesty choice.
- Deliberately kept `exit 1` on the Atlas egress block rather than the handoff's `exit 0`: the exit code doesn't feed `v_pipeline_failure_rates` (`postRoute` does), so exiting 0 would only have turned a real outage green in the GHA UI. The win was cutting **24.2 min → ~1 min** per blocked run.
- **✅ FOLLOW-UP CLOSED (Claude Code, 2026-08-01 ~23:16Z): both new Vercel crons authenticate — NO revert of `2fefdad9` needed.** Confirmed by live `pipeline_runs` (not the rollup): `topshot-listing-cache` ticked `ok=true` at 22:55:10Z + 23:15:25Z (the new `15,35,55 * * * *` `:55`/`:15` slots) and `allow-list-reconcile` at 23:14:28Z (the new `14 * * * *` `:14` slot) — both post-deploy, no error. ⚠ **DURABLE — do NOT verify this class via `pipeline_runs_daily`:** that rollup runs pg_cron `11 */6 * * *`, so right after a cron move its today-row still reflects the PRE-deploy GHA rate and reads like a failure; check live `pipeline_runs` for a tick whose minute matches the NEW Vercel `schedule`. The original ZERO→`CRON_SECRET`-mismatch→revert trigger was never met.
- Item 13 (Panini) correctly **not** shipped: feed out 4 days vs the handoff's own >7-day gate, and Panini FMV is fresh (68 min), so no rendered number is stale. Re-check from 2026-08-04. Items 1/2/6/12 remain **operator-only**; 11/14/15/16/17 untouched.

The documented counter-case (hot not-all-visible pages, per the `fmv_current` note) did not apply.

### August 1, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — handoff drain, cont.) — SHIPPED the fix for the queued OPENS-HISTORY-BACKFILL-500 (edge fn `ingest-topshot-pack-opens-history`, repo + DEPLOYED v4 via MCP); found + fixed the same-class defect in the AllDay twin (repo-only, NOT deployed). Commits `a3817e14` (TS) + `8cbd1271` (AllDay); `docs/overnight/ledger.md` (2026-08-01) + revert path; direct to `main`.

- **The queued diagnosis was wrong on three counts (each evidence-backed).** (1) **`rows_written=0` across the green days is correct DEDUP, not silent loss** — the 03:56Z run found `rows_found=81`, `pack_rips` holds exactly 81 in that window; the miss was that **`rows_skipped` was never instrumented** on this route (a null instrument for a *re-walking* backfill). (2) The apparent "cursor-skip" was a **fast-forward to `MIN(pack_rips.block_height)`=61,930,346**, and coverage above it is dense/gapless — nothing stepped over. (3) The correlated `c4c4d3c9` is a **docs-only ledger commit** — spurious. (The ~272k low-range TS rips came from the one-day 2026-07-11 Dapper `searchPackNft` registry bulk load, not this scanner — which is why the fn re-verifies and writes nothing.)
- **THE ACTUAL DEFECT + FIX.** The fn scans a 25,000-block window as ~100 sequential 250-block `/events` queries and abandoned the **entire** window on the first failure (`after = anyTransient ? cur : start`). The deep-history spork is **flaky, not down** (the 41 failing runs died at query 1–5 in rotation), so a 100-query all-or-nothing window essentially never completes → cursor never moved, 96 runs/day burned. Shipped: `scanOpens` now walks **DESCENDING** (matching the cursor's direction) and returns a `scannedFloor` checkpoint; the cursor advances to `min(scannedFloor-or-resolvedFloor, cur)`, so progress is **monotonic under any upstream failure rate**. `rows_skipped` now populated; `ok` = progressed-OR-no-error. `SPORK_FLOOR = 27,341,470` (mainnet17 root) → ~34.6M blocks of reachable, un-indexed TS pack-open history below the registry floor; the fn should keep walking to it (TS genesis ~block 7M is permanently unreachable, documented gap).
- **DURABLE — scan direction MUST match the caller's cursor direction; getting it backwards is SILENT loss, not an error** (the unscanned hole lands on the side the cursor is about to claim). The AllDay twin `ingest-allday-pack-opens` carries the identical defect AND is **actively losing data today** — on a `MAX_TX=180` budget stop it advances the full 25,000-block window over opens it never resolved (measured 16/143 runs/24h). Fixed properly with `scanOpens(start, end, dir)` — `"desc"` for backfill, default `"asc"` for the forward/probe walker (which goes the opposite way), so **forward-mode behaviour is byte-identical**. **AllDay is repo-only, NOT deployed** (healthy high-volume pipeline; the MCP deploy inlines ~460 lines by hand = transcription risk — deserves its own deploy + tick verification). **Forward-mode tx-budget loss left OPEN (queued).**
- **Post-deploy verification (`3e0806c0`) CONFIRMED the fix and PROVED flaky-not-dead:** a chunk that 500'd at 14:57:19Z SUCCEEDED at 15:11:01Z (`ok=true`, `partial=true`, cursor advanced) — under the old code that run would have been zero-progress. ⚠ **NEW RISK the fix introduces — do not read it as a clean win.** At ~250–750 blocks/tick against 34.6M remaining this region is ~YEARS, and because the cursor now moves with `ok=true`, **neither `cursor_stalled` NOR the fail-rate alarm can ever fire for it again** — a silent crawl ([[green-pipeline-blind-to-its-own-work]]). Still strictly better than a paging hard-wedge, but the pipeline now needs a **rate** signal. **Queued:** raise the per-chunk retry budget in the spork events path (`j()` gives up after 3 tries — the same chunk succeeds minutes later), and add a backfill-rate/ETA arm to `v_rpc_trust_health`. **Deployed as a MINIMAL DIFF** keeping the inline `esm.sh` import (the documented repo↔deployed divergence). **Revert:** `git revert <sha>` for the repo files; to unwind prod, redeploy the v3 body (recoverable via `get_edge_function` history) — no DB/cursor unwind (forward-down + idempotent on `tx_hash`).

### August 1, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "find a TODO → also fix any bugs → keep going" → wrap up) — shipped 2 prod DB fixes clearing BOTH open trust/Sentry issues, then swept every monitoring surface clean. Each ship its own `docs/overnight/ledger.md` entry (2026-08-01) + revert path; direct to `main`.

Thread arc: the TODO sweep re-confirmed the standing finding (every live TODO is RESOLVED or launch-flag-GATED — nothing safe to implement) → pivoted to the "also fix bugs" directive → fixed the two open issues → swept Sentry/advisors/pipeline-fails, found nothing else actionable, wrapped.

- **SHIPPED — get_set_detail graceful FMV-timeout** (`audit_20260801_get_set_detail_graceful_fmv_timeout`, commit `6d0cc7e0`, DB-only). Killed Sentry **NEXTJS-22** "set detail unavailable: canceling statement due to statement timeout" on `/[collection]/set/[slug]`. Root cause (profiled): the per-edition latest-FMV rollup on TS **"Base Set"** (3,606 thumbnail editions) fans out to thousands of correlated `LEFT JOIN LATERAL … ORDER BY computed_at DESC LIMIT 1` probes across the `fmv_snapshots` partitions (~2s warm) and, cold under contention, blew the request statement budget and THREW — erroring the whole public page. Fix: wrap ONLY the rollup in `BEGIN … EXCEPTION WHEN query_canceled` → degrade the header stats to NULL (page renders "—", the separately-fetched editions grid still loads) instead of throwing. Also dropped the RETURN's `COALESCE(edition_count,0)` so a degraded read shows "—" not a misleading "0" beside a populated grid (happy path byte-identical; `COUNT(*)` never NULL). DB-invariant test re-pinned to the new migration (drift guard 57/57); Sentry resolved; overnight post-ship watch confirmed 0/24h.
- **DURABLE (cost a live break) — `statement_timeout` is armed at the TOP-LEVEL command boundary and CANNOT be re-armed mid-function.** A runtime `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` inside a STABLE fn THROWS *"SET is not allowed in a non-volatile function"* (my first apply broke `get_set_detail` on EVERY call for ~2 min until fixed); and even under VOLATILE the inner SET is a NO-OP for sub-statements (verified: `pg_sleep(1)` under `SET LOCAL '50ms'` completes; a function-level `SET statement_timeout` clause also does NOT enforce within the call). So you CANNOT give a slow sub-query its own inner budget — instead **catch the REQUEST/role timeout that's already firing** with `EXCEPTION WHEN query_canceled` and degrade; the `RETURN` still runs (the one-shot timer already fired, won't re-fire). Final ship is the clean STABLE + `BEGIN/EXCEPTION` wrapper, no SET. Proven deterministically on a local `postgres:16`.
- **DURABLE — for latest-per-edition FMV IN a DB query, KEEP the per-edition `LATERAL … LIMIT 1`; do NOT swap to a `fmv_current` DISTINCT-ON join.** Profiled on Base Set: the `fmv_current` join is **11× WORSE (22.5s vs 2s)** — it materializes the DISTINCT ON over all ~942k snapshot rows before filtering. The CLAUDE.md "use `fmv_current`" rule is ONLY for the JS-1000-row-cap correctness case (raw DESC + JS dedup), NOT in-DB per-edition reads. A covering `INCLUDE(fmv_usd,floor_price_usd)` index doesn't help either — the wanted row is always the newest = a hot, not-all-visible page → index-only still heap-fetches.
- **SHIPPED — v_fmv_sanity_flags own-sales corroboration** (`audit_20260801_fmv_sanity_flags_own_sales_corroboration`, commit `68aa236d`, DB-only view) — resolves the star-set FALSE-POSITIVE the overnight pass queued (§4). The view flagged any TS edition with FMV <12% of its **SET** median — a "cheap vs peers" heuristic that can't tell a genuinely-cheap role player (Champagnie `261:8714`, FMV $97 = **118% of his own $82 raw 30d median**) from a mispricing. Added corroboration against the edition's OWN RAW sales (`public.sales`, independent of the FMV pipeline): fire only when `fmv < 0.6 × own 30d sales median` on ≥4 sales with a >$50 gap. **STRICT SUBSET of the prior output** (only ADDS predicates) → can only remove the FP class, never introduce one. Appended `own_sales_median_30d`/`own_sales_n`; `fmv_sanity_flags` metric **1→0 ok**; view runs 358ms; sole consumer `v_rpc_trust_health` reads `count(*)` so appended cols are safe; `security_invoker=true` + service-role-only grants re-asserted. Both trust breaches now cleared — board green.
- **Sweep — nothing else actionable (healthy platform):** Sentry — the other 2 unresolved are stale one-offs (9–13d, non-recurring). Perf advisors — 227, all INFO (unused-index / no-PK hygiene; acting on them is net-negative risk). Security advisors — 7 WARN are known SECDEF-executable fns backing real features (`serial_fmv_estimate` MUST stay anon per CLAUDE.md), 167 INFO the deliberate service-role-only RLS posture, `secdef_anon_exec_drift` `[]`. Pipeline fails/24h — all upstream Flow `400/429/500`, transient saturation-class statement timeouts (each with healthy neighbors), by-design guards, or the queued OPENS-HISTORY-BACKFILL-UPSTREAM-500 (off-limits ingest edge-fn + no Deno toolchain here).
